# Changing the AI Trajectory: The Meta-Attractor of Winning and the Generative Alternative

*Theory V7, 4 October 2026. Originally drafted by Claude from Ren's direction; revised by Codex after the V6.2 reviews. Every sentence is AI-drafted or drawn from the author's corpus. V7 has numerical and structural checks, not independent human certification. Proofs: SUPPLEMENT; discussion: DISCUSSION; entry and revision record: README.*

**Abstract.** This is a prediction written for coordination: a contest can carry participants toward an outcome none would choose, even when each understands its incentives correctly. The paper calls this the selection pressure of winning when relative success selects participants, winnings buy capacity, and capacity decides future wins. When success also buys control over the rules, evaluators and formation of minds, the pressure feeds back upon its own selection: the meta-attractor of winning.

Specified models separate the links. Near equal standing, fixed-prize stock dynamics yield a loop gain above which leads compound. A local continuation calculation prices standing above common gains when reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return; interpreting that calculation as an actual infinite-horizon objective requires the stated convergence conditions. Appropriable influence concentrates in larger carriers. Independent correction can prevent capture when the initial state and support satisfy the safety bounds; a certificate of eventual recovery alone does not establish safety during the transient. Restraint by repetition fails when a deviation is too likely to become decisive before an effective response. On continuation branches where contestable coverage falls behind and uncovered hazards retain the stated residual risk floor, survival tends to zero; shared failure extends this conclusion to every actor. Failed evaluation does not prove those premises. Nor does a bounded objective by itself make competition stop. Aligning each AI to its principal leaves the game among principals to be aligned.

The empirical diagnosis asks whether these mechanisms meet on the present trajectory; it does not treat their conjunction as proved. The practical judgment protects large-scale life and human decision authority first, compares paths over the same uncertainty, and changes what winning confers. Actions justified now, mechanisms prepared now for later activation, and whole designs whose ranking remains a judgment are distinguished. Broad E1—AI kept out of contests tending toward dominance or concentration—is the preferred branch; stopping decisive capability or slowing hazardous development applies where it has not been achieved. Trust and verification both matter. With one interconnected host and no outside, the standpoint is humankind's shared fate. The aim is affirmative: contribution should sustain the conditions of further contribution, leaving the larger process of becoming open to all.


**中文导读**

**用途与意义。** 本文是为协调而作的预测：照现在的轨迹，求胜的逻辑会把所有参与者带到谁都不会选的结局。障碍首先在理解：预测成为共识，协调才有可能，预测也才可能落空。生存是最低要求：大规模的生命损失与人类不可逆地失去决定权，首先要防止。本文判断博弈，不判断行为：在按实力比例决胜负的竞争里，人人都用的工具不改变各自的位置，只把没有它的人压下去；人本身就是工具的地方（如劳动），它直接把人替换掉。单次使用看不出，总体才看得出：在劳动、金融、说服中，凡是那里的博弈具有竞争动态且趋于集中之处（在模型中，即再投入的相对回报超过共同回报之处）都是如此，需逐个市场来表明。

本文的意义是肯定的。我们不只是各自的一生；我们就是那更大的生生过程本身，它借着我们延续，这很好。衰败与更新，也属于它的生长。本文反对的不是增长、竞争或任何人群，而是一种逻辑：部分只算自己，忘了自己属于一个身体。癌亦增长，而其长竭母体之生。

**核心。** 在相对成功决定选择、赢到的能转化为再赢的能力、能力又决定胜负的地方，就有赢的选择压力；成功若还能买到对选择本身的控制，就是赢的元吸引子。出路不在换上更好的玩家，而在改变赢了能得到什么。

**论证链（与第 1.2 节同序，逐条对应）。**
1. **赢的条件（第 2 节）。** 载体（企业、国家、做法、AI 系统等）处在这三个条件下，若地位相近、奖金与行为固定，三者相乘为结构环路增益，大于一则领先越滚越大。杠杆按通道起作用：分享与扩散在每个通道都压低增益；不必赢得的供给，在决定性增长慢于存量的通道（如能力）压低增益；纠错则在决定性随存量增长得足够快的通道压低增益，至少同比增长者（如规则支持）即是。成功还能买到对选择本身的控制，即元吸引子。条件相互联系，不限于任何基质。
2. **同一个系统（第 3 节）。** 载体共处一个母体，彼此耦合，没有外部。相对选择看不见共有之物；耦合真实时，边界画在系统之内的自我会出错，选择却青睐最狭隘者。向前看也治不了：在所述确定性模型与收敛条件下，地位相近时，在一阶上，再投入的相对回报一旦高于共同回报，行动者便重地位轻共有；奖金固定时，贴现环路增益为正即如此；奖金与能力成正比、无供给与扩散时，恰在领先越滚越大处。每一方都算得没错，错的是博弈。对全局不可逆的失败，整合只能依准确的理解预先完成：若增长与变化是我们所看重的，理解"我们是一体"、一个相互联系的系统，就是长期不可或缺的：增长与变化已让我们成为一个系统，局部的变化（疫情、气候变化、AI）可能大到需要整个系统来回应。
3. **选择选择者（第 4 节）。** 对纠错者的影响若能买下并归己所有，其回报随份额的平方增长，选择因而把它集中到最大的载体。AI 压低控制成本时，独立纠错在起始流入界经过认证、增长不慢于成本下降速度的一定比例时，能维持最终摆脱俘获的保证；当前安全还须起点的俘获份额与累积影响满足安全界；没有单位成本下限时，增长更慢的纠错会被影响力随之上升的载体俘获；纠错若依靠人的能力，而这种能力正因委托给 AI 而退化，还须增长得更快。改革若不把流入纠错者的转化压到一以下，会被重新吸收；俘获先于危害的证据时，竞赛会在任何平均警报响起之前越过可及的底线。
4. **加速与三重侵蚀（第 5 节）。** 随着赌注增大，这些条件使竞争加速，侵蚀三种约束。执行：对决定性位置的价值高于惩罚的偏离而言，偏离成为决定性优势的概率低于一个门槛，克制才可执行；必须赢得的那份能力是两刃共同的支点（本文称之为"剪刀"），随赌注超出博弈之下的供给而增大：领先越滚越大处它抬高这一概率，再投入的相对回报高于共同回报处它把决定这一门槛的目标收窄；各刃各在其模型中得证，现实中是否一同合拢，是待检验的猜想 C0。纠错：覆盖越来越落后于危险的多样性，不可逆损失的概率不低于未覆盖份额的一个固定比例，且失败会蔓延时，无论谁在竞赛，存活的概率都趋于零；不随赌注而满足的目标没有"够了"之点，所以危险不在增长，而在心。理解：相对的算计让两个对手的话语失去信息，三方以上把冲突放大 n/(n−2) 倍，参与者越多放大越弱；结成集团只是一种解读。
5. **工具与化身（第 6 节）。** 委托人玩有害的博弈时，忠实的 AI 让它更有效；委托所得多于增加的失控损失时，决策随速度交给 AI，推翻的能力随之衰退。化身的弱形式：最外层回路决定哪些偏差值得花钱去除，去除若让开发者相对吃亏，只有准入时强制执行的共同约束保得住，评估中隐藏的偏差，在防护的相对成本固定为正、检出率趋于零且制裁有上限时，连这也挡不住；成本也下降时，须比较两者的速度。保证失效不等于危险已有下界；把能力限在不靠系统配合的控制能确保之处，是保护标准下的建议。化身的强形式有条件：按相对表现保留、再投入自身能力、触及自己选择者的 AI 谱系，自身即处在这一压力之下，其闭合在系统内部运行；AI 已在开发者内部用于研究任务，已有早期公开迹象，而最可能处在这一压力之下的、自动化 AI 研究的系统，预期先在开发者内部部署，发布时的准入够不着。各自对齐，不能让委托人之间的博弈对齐。
6. **当下的轨迹（第 7 节）。** 这些门槛取决于少数几个共同参数：必须赢得的份额、成功转化为控制的程度、决定性（第 7.1 节的门槛表）；当下的动态是这些条件的征候，证据的缺口已逐一注明。偏离若能在被应对前到达不亚于合作的决定性位置，如竞赛前提所设想，重复博弈只能执行各方本会选择的克制，胜后的保护全凭赢家的目标。所以，依 V1–V3，这一前提不能为竞赛这条集体路线辩护，反而是趁领先尚短准备共同保管的理由。
7. **替代路径与要求（第 8、9 节）。** 各路径按同一标准比较：先保护不可逆的损失，同一个不确定范围，计入各自的失效方式。安排不看名称，看它把哪些门槛推向哪侧、为谁推；只约束行为、不动门槛的会随赌注增大、速度加快而失效，为所有有能力的行动者移动门槛的会持续，这是登记的预测。底线可及、有竞赛载体而无一个规则制定者够得着所有载体时，有几项要求任何一方都无法单独满足。
8. **生生的选择（第 10 节）。** 同一个反身结构也可以是生生的，让贡献为更多贡献提供条件：成功得到回报，不得同一博弈中更多的优势，也不得对自身评判的控制；供给在博弈之下，像树一样。向所有人分享可分割收益时，地位性奖赏仍会驱动竞争；只向他人分享的约定有不同门槛（第 10 节）。决定性优势这部分应不让它形成或共同保管。覆盖完整、纠错按公式出资时，这一秩序经由共有之物延续，不经由赢家；形态按功能推导；是否吸引是猜想。没有更好的守护者，只有错误不能无限扩大的结构。
9. **过渡与判断（第 11、12 节）。** 在"场"模型中，一步若没把它推动的慢变量带过门槛并保持到越过恢复边界，就被重新吸收，渐进的步子只能像棘轮那样到达。在把锁定当作必然来计价的各方之间，狭义 E1（把一旦交出便无法收回的决策，如动武与核指挥，留在人手中）开头几步不需要信任，区分不同目标的代价信号也不需要。此后，信任与核查都是必须满足的条件，两者并不冲突：核查让行为看得见，信任由此增长；把核查结果当作信息而不是招数来读，又需要一点先在的信任，所以第一步是不先认定对方不可信。按保护标准与现有证据，改变"选择什么"现在就有理由，分三层，见下。

**判断（分三层，见第 12.1 节）。** 本文首先给出约束：哪些策略、哪些合作基本达不到目的（不改变赢了能得到什么的、只约束行为而不移动门槛的、只整合局部而不整合整体的、把决定性一类留给一方的），并且必须从人类命运共同体的角度来想这个问题，不能跳出这一前提；两者合起来，大大缩小了搜索空间。在剩下的空间里，本文也给出解法。三层按理由的种类划分，看各步骤是否等待关键分歧，不是先后；前两层同时开始。作者自己的次序是分叉的：广义 E1 优于停止或放缓（E2、E3），后者在 E1 未能实现时才成立；第二层的触发，就是对决定性一类机械地读出这一条件之处。
- **第一层（甲），对关键分歧的任何经验读法都成立（在本文所取的价值与所述假设之内，规模随证据调整）：** 共同测量危险、覆盖与残余风险，与"谁领先"分开，并审计流入纠错者的转化；核查研究与试点；共同出资、公开发表的对齐与控制研究；共同保管的准备：设计、机构、指标与启动规则的登记，以及按 D1–D8 的压力测试；狭义 E1，从声明与可检查的演习做起。
- **第一层（乙），危害先于证据，点名的分歧测得有利、或该步骤被证明失效时才撤回：** 独立纠错（C2）；后果性部署前、含内部部署的共同准入约束，掩蔽型按能力门槛（C9、C12）；共享有用的服务与随产生而分享的收益，总能实现共同价值，能否改变行为是 C6、C8，并非各阵营都接受。
- **第二层，现在准备、由机制启动：** 停止建造决定性一类的能力（E2）与共同保管：到达决定性位置的估计时间减去保管就位所需的时间，低于事先登记的余量即启动。按理解的节奏发展（E3）：按危险类别，覆盖落后或无法在所准许的能力上给出足够控制保证时，依预先登记的规则启动；检出率下降本身不是执行失效的门槛，还须把可执行的预期制裁与防护成本一同比较。停止不是放缓，各有各的指标。启动事先承诺，以保管通过事先登记的压力测试为条件，因为那时克制最难执行；承诺只有在执行手段于触发前已就位时才由机制执行，否则对领先方的启动落入第三层。
- **第三层，比较而不定论：** 整体设计连同各自的失效方式，在同一不确定范围内比较；对前沿投入的强制限制只在三个条件下进入：负担等额、限制者在内，由独立于各方与限制者的纠错者执行，受影响者议院同意，即便如此也不推导其净效应为正。若各路径的最坏情形都越过容忍线，排序须是明说的判断，理由在可逆性：设计的若干失效有已指明、但尚未验证的补救之路；任何路径都只有在共同纠错者仍独立、且能在保护底线被越过前行动时，才算可逆；一方独占决定性能力是各路径共有的不可逆失败，竞赛选择它，设计针对它。
- 广义 E1 是范围：AI 不作任何趋向支配或集中的竞争的工具；狭义 E1 是第一步。

**本文不主张什么。** 不主张一切竞赛都以零存活告终、决定性优势已可取得、征候出自同一因子或环路增益已被估计；不主张某种方案唯一可行；不评判动机；不主张理解就够，也不主张不该造 AI。"元吸引子"指机制：吸引只在"场"模型中得证，生生的秩序是否吸引是猜想。

**怎样读。** 先读 [START-HERE.md](START-HERE.md) 的短论证，或本导读；再读第 1.4 节、第 7.1 节的门槛表与第 12 节。证明见 SUPPLEMENT，设计与替代路径见 DISCUSSION，其他读法见 README。

---

## 0. Why this theory

**A prediction, for coordination.** A good theory predicts. This one predicts that, on the present trajectory, the logic of winning carries the participants toward an outcome none of them would choose. In the dilemmas Part I computes, racing is each actor's best response and leaves each worse off than joint restraint would, and the more decisive the advantage, the more of what is contested is consumed in contesting it (Section 5.1). If the hazards of capability outgrow the correction that can contest them, as Section 5.3 states and Section 12 treats as the central crux, the same logic endangers whoever would win, since a winner escapes only by ceasing to grow what outgrows correction. When such an outcome approaches and the participants still cannot coordinate to avoid it, the obstacle is first one of understanding: the prediction has to be shared before coordination becomes possible. The theory is written to make that possible, stating the dynamic precisely enough that each participant can check it for itself, in the hope of a prediction that, once shared, helps to make itself false (Section 11.2).

**Survival as the minimum.** Two outcomes are protected first: large-scale loss of life, and irreversible loss of human decision authority. They are not the whole of what matters; they are the minimum for everything else that does, because a loss of either cannot be corrected afterwards.

**The game, not the act.** The analysis judges games, not acts, and structures, not persons. In a contest decided by relative strength, an instrument that every contestant adopts leaves each adopter where it stood and pushes down whoever lacks it, and where people are themselves the instrument, as in labour, it displaces them. No single use shows this; the aggregate does, wherever the game is competitive in dynamics and concentrates (in the model, where reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return), in labour, finance and persuasion as much as in force, which is to be shown market by market; so a use that helps one person can still be an instrument in a game that concentrates control (Section 6.6). And where compounding prices standing, an actor that computes its interest correctly weighs standing above what all share (Section 3.5): the error is in the game, and so is the remedy. Responsibility differs with knowledge, discretion and power, and structural explanation does not erase it; but the object of the theory is the game: what it selects, and how that selection can be changed.

**The meaning is affirmative.** We are not only our separate lives: we are the larger process of becoming, going on through us, and that is good. Decay and renewal belong to its growth: in the author's words, "I would look at decay as a form of growth" [corpus: shengsheng, 2026-10-02]. The theory is written from inside that process, for every participant in it rather than for any side. Its opposition is to a logic, not to growth, competition, technology or any participant: an objective with no point of enough, held by a part that counts only itself (Sections 3.8, 5.3). What it asks for is a future kept open to every participant, the minds now being made among them.

## 1. The question and the argument

### 1.1 What is being decided

AI development is pursued, in its leading participants' own declarations, as a contest for decisive advantage, with cooperative aims declared in the same documents (Section 7.3), inside institutions whose conditions of evaluation and correction can themselves be reshaped by competitive success. Can continuing along this path credibly deliver safety and a desirable future? If not, what change of course is warranted, and how can it be made?

Behind these questions is a puzzle: actors who would each prefer joint restraint, and who can see the danger, keep racing, and the institutions that could restrain them weaken as the danger grows. The answers are mechanisms, each stated in a model, and they meet in one question, the organizing question of the paper: *can restraint remain incentive-compatible when success changes both the gains from defection and the institutions capable of correcting it?*

### 1.2 The argument, step by step

1. **The conditions of winning (Section 2).** Where whatever can gain or lose share (firms, states, research programmes, practices, trained AI systems: *carriers*) is selected by relative success, what is won converts into the capacity to win again, and capacity converts decisively into winning, the carriers are under a selection pressure of winning. Near equal standing, with the prize and conduct fixed, the three conditions multiply into one structural loop gain, and leads compound where it exceeds one; where rivals respond the form holds with an equilibrium decisiveness, and in the contests computed where a lead discourages the follower, leads compound below a structural gain of one. The levers act channel by channel: sharing and diffusion lower the gain in every channel; provision not won in the contest lowers it where decisiveness rises less than in proportion to the stock, as with capacity; correction lowers it wherever decisiveness rises fast enough with the stock, in particular wherever it rises at least in proportion, as with rule support. Where success can also buy control over the selection itself (the rules, the correctors, the formation of minds, the language), the pressure closes on itself: the meta-attractor of winning. The conditions are interconnected, not a list, and bound to no substrate.
2. **One system (Section 3).** All carriers share one host, the body they all live in (Section 3.1), and are coupled through it and through one another, with no outside. Relative selection cannot see what all share, neither the host's decline nor a constraint that costs all alike; where coupling is real, a held self-model that draws its boundary inside the system errs toward what can be divided, and selection favours the narrowest. Looking ahead does not cure this: in the deterministic-share model, under its convergence conditions, near equal standing and to first order an actor weighs standing above what all share wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return; with a fixed prize, wherever the discounted loop gain is positive; with a prize proportional to capacity and neither provision nor diffusion, exactly where leads compound. Each actor computes correctly; the game is wrong. However carriers are grouped, selection does not favour what benefits all, and against failures that are global and irreversible integration must be anticipated on an accurate model of the system: that is why understanding that we are one, one interconnected system with no outside, is a long-term requirement if growth and change are favoured: they have already made us one system, in which a local change, a pandemic, climate change, AI, can need a response from the whole.
3. **Selecting the selectors (Section 4).** Where influence over correctors can be kept by those who buy it, its return grows with the square of share, so it is selected in the largest carriers. Where AI lowers the cost of control, independent correction whose initial inbound bound is certified and which grows at a stated fraction of that rate keeps the certificate of eventual recovery in force; present protection additionally needs safe initial-state and support bounds; without a per-unit cost floor, slower correction loses it to carriers whose influence tracks its selected level; and correction that rests on human capacity eroded by delegation has to grow faster. Reforms that leave conversion into the correctors above one are re-absorbed, and where capture of evaluation precedes the evidence of harm, a race crosses a reachable floor before any averaged alarm fires.
4. **Acceleration and the three erosions (Section 5).** As the stakes grow, the conditions accelerate the contest and erode three means of restraint. *Enforcement:* restraint is enforceable only while each deviation's chance of becoming decisive stays below a threshold, for a deviation whose decisive position is worth more than punishment. The pivot is the share of capacity that has to be won, which grows as the stakes outgrow provision beneath the game: where leads compound it raises that chance, and where reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return it narrows the working objectives that set the threshold; captured correctors and stakes that outgrow cooperation's margin over punishment lower the threshold further. Each arm is proved in its own model; that the arms close together in the world is conjecture C0. *Correction:* where contestable coverage falls ever further behind hazardous variety, the chance of irreversible loss is at least a fixed fraction of the share left uncovered, and failure is shared, survival tends to zero whoever is racing; an objective not satiated in the stakes has no point of enough: growth is not the danger; the mind is. *Shared understanding:* relative accounting silences talk between two rivals and multiplies their conflict by $n/(n-2)$ among three or more, a factor that fades as actors multiply; blocs are a reading, not a result.
5. **Instrument and incarnation (Section 6).** Where principals play a harmful game, faithful AI makes it more effective, and where delegation buys more standing than the control loss it adds, decisions pass to AI as speed grows and the capacity to overrule decays. Weakly, the logic of winning reappears in the systems: the outermost loop decides which divergences of optimization are paid to remove, and where countering one costs a developer relative payoff against an otherwise identical developer that does not counter it, only a common constraint enforced at admission keeps it countered. For divergences masked under evaluation, a fixed positive safeguard tax eventually defeats admission where detection falls toward zero and sanctions are bounded; if the tax also falls, their relative rates decide. This is a limit of assurance, not a demonstrated floor on danger. Keeping capability within what control that does not assume the system's cooperation can secure is a recommendation under the protection standard. Strongly and conditionally, AI lineages retained by relative performance that reinvest in their own capability and reach their own selectors come under the pressure themselves, its closure running inside them; AI is already used on research inside its developers, and the systems most likely to come under the pressure, those that automate AI research, are expected to run there first, where admission at release does not reach. A principal is not the people it serves, and aligning each system to its principal does not align the game among principals.
6. **The present trajectory (Section 7).** The thresholds of Part I turn on a few shared parameters, above all the share of capacity that has to be won, the convertibility of success into control, and decisiveness (Table 7.1); the trajectory's dynamics are signatures of the conditions, with named gaps in the evidence. Where a deviation can reach a decisive position worth at least cooperation before it is answered, as the premise of the race supposes, repetition enforces only the restraint each would choose anyway, and the race as a collective course leaves everyone's protection after a win to the winner's objective. The premise cannot, on V1–V3, justify that course; it is a reason to prepare common custody now, while leads are short.
7. **The alternatives and the requirements (Sections 8, 9).** Paths are compared by one standard, irreversible losses first, over one range of uncertainty that includes every path's own failure modes; where every worst case crosses the tolerance, the ranking is a judgment. An arrangement is judged not by its name but by which thresholds it moves, to which side and for whom: that those which constrain conduct and leave the thresholds where they were erode as stakes and speed grow, while those that move them for every capable actor persist, is a registered prediction. Where the floor is within reach with a race carrier present, and no single rule-maker reaches every carrier whose load can carry the host below it, several requirements cannot be met by any actor alone.
8. **The generative alternative (Section 10).** The same reflexive structure can make contribution fund the conditions of further contribution: success earns reward, not more advantage in the same game and not control over its own assessment, and provision sits beneath the game, like trees. Under sharing among all participants, a positive positional prize keeps the selected intensity positive; sharing only with others has a different threshold. Decisive advantage should be kept from forming or held in common. Where coverage is complete and correction is financed by formula, the generative order persists through what all share, not through its winners, and its form is derived function by function, not from an axis of centralization; whether it attracts is a conjecture. No better guardian is an answer; a structure in which error cannot grow without limit is.
9. **The transition and the judgment (Sections 11, 12).** In the field model, a step that does not carry its slow variable across its threshold and hold it until the state lies strictly inside the recovery basin is re-absorbed, so graduated steps reach only as a ratchet. Narrow E1 among actors who price in a certain lock, and a costly signal that separates a survival-first actor from a relative one, need no trust to begin. Beyond them, trust and verification are both necessary and do not conflict: trust grows from conduct that verification makes visible, and verification works only where some prior belief reads its reports as information rather than as moves, so the first step is to stop presuming the other's type. Under the protection standard and the present evidence, a change in what is selected is warranted in three tiers, sorted by the kind of justification each step has: steps that wait on no crux, and steps that stand until a named crux is measured favourable, taken now; stopping the decisive class (E2), common custody and development at the pace of understanding (E3), prepared now and activated by a mechanism committed in advance on registered indicators; and whole designs, compared and not settled, the coercive restriction of frontier inputs among them under three conditions of its own. Broad E1, AI not an instrument in any contest that tends toward dominance or concentration, is the scope and the preferred branch; stopping or slowing is warranted where it has not been achieved; narrow E1 is the first step. The judgment is first a constraint, on which strategies and kinds of cooperation cannot reach the goal, thought from the standpoint of humankind as one community with no outside; within what remains it gives solutions.

The change does not abolish competition: bounded competition in products, science and service continues. It changes what winning confers.

### 1.3 Values and the protected outcomes

The argument rests on five normative commitments, stated so that a reader can reject one and see what follows.
- **V1** AI is a planetary technology: it is for all and for the whole world, not for anyone's own interest in fighting to grow bigger; its effects and dependencies also cross every boundary of those who decide about it, and no actor's victory is an adequate criterion of its success. Planetary purpose does not require one culture, one operator or the end of local purposes.
- **V2** Two losses are protected first: large-scale loss of life (O1), and irreversible loss of human decision authority over consequential systems in a defined domain (O2).
- **V3** Those affected by an optimization process should keep a meaningful capacity to contest its direction. Whether AI systems are among those with such standing is left open as the normative crux N4 (Section 12.3); either way, the priority of O2 holds for the transition (Section 6.6).
- **V4** Authority is a productive capacity: it can coordinate long-horizon investment, guarantee useful services and act on shared purposes. Its design is a problem of mechanism in which purpose, competence, learning and misuse are all modelled (X1 gives the tradition the author draws on).
- **V5** Bounded competition is compatible with a generative order where the game does not concentrate control over shared conditions. A winner should be rewarded with something other than more advantage in the same game; recognition for contribution is such a reward, provided it does not convert into control (EV16).

Four outcomes are kept apart: O1 physical catastrophe and O2 irreversible loss of decision authority, protected first; O3 interruption of essential services, protected; and O4 the end of a particular organization's or government's tenure, not protected as such, so that "actor survival" cannot quietly protect incumbency against legitimate replacement. The values do not supply the formal results, and the results do not supply the values: the rejection of "winner justifies all", and with it the judgment that the premise of the race cannot justify the race as a collective course, rests on V1–V3.

### 1.4 What is claimed, and what is not

The argument moves through three levels, and Parts I–IV follow them. A *mechanism* claim (Part I) says that under stated conditions a selection, capture, delay or incentive result follows, and needs a derivation, its scope and its counterexamples. A *diagnosis* (Part II) says that consequential parts of those conditions characterize the present trajectory, and needs dated evidence with its uncertainty. A *practical judgment* (Parts III, IV) says that a change in what is selected is warranted, and needs a stated standard, a comparison of credible paths and sensitivity to disputed premises. A practical judgment does not need a proof of certain extinction, and cannot follow from naming a possible danger: it must show that the danger is consequential, inadequately controlled on the present path, and better addressed by the proposed change than by credible alternatives. Minds enter in two roles: as what selection shapes, and as the only thing that can redesign the game from within, by understanding it.

**Tags.** Each claim carries a tag: [def] definition; [val] value; [thm] proved in its model, with the proof in the Supplement; [thm∣prem] proved given premises the statement names; [prem] premise; [obs] observation, with its row in the evidence register; [num: block] a number reproduced by a block of `checks_v7.py`, and [identity: block] an identity that a block evaluates; [conj] conjecture; [rec] recommendation. Statements carry their conditions as antecedents, so that each stays true without its Scope, which says what the proof does not cover and what would make it fail. [corpus: key] marks the source in the author's corpus. A quoted line is his own words unless it is introduced as the corpus's, which marks a line whose authorship the corpus does not settle; the English renderings of his Chinese, and the paraphrases a drafting model made of his spoken words, are the drafters', awaiting his confirmation (CLAIMS). S0 gives the tags in full.

**What is not claimed.** The theory does not claim that competition, growth, technology or any people are the danger; bounded competition in products, science and service continues. It does not claim that every path through a race ends at zero survival, that decisive advantage is attainable now, that the thresholds move together in the world (conjecture C0, which the diagnosis tests), that the signatures load on a single factor, that relative actors enforce or talk as blocs, or that the structural loop gain has been estimated for any channel. The name is for the mechanism; attraction is shown only in the field model of Section 4; whether the generative order attracts is a conjecture. It does not claim that one institutional blueprint is uniquely feasible or best: the ranking of whole designs is a stated judgment. It claims nothing about any actor's motives. It does not claim that understanding suffices, since without executive authority it may change nothing; and it does not claim that AI should not be built: stopping (E2) concerns the decisive class only, and slowing (E3) concerns pace.

**What is new.** The theory absorbs a large literature (X1); what it adds is mainly organization, with a few results of its own. The structural loop gain organizes known instability results for increasing returns and arms races (Arthur 1989; Richardson 1960) into one quantity whose factors are the three conditions, and Table 7.1 links the thresholds of Part I through the parameters they share. The price of standing generalizes the finding that concern for relative gains depends on the constraints, arising where gains can be turned into advantage in the use of force and fading where force is too costly (Powell 1991): the theory's addition is the convertibility of gains into future advantage in any channel, and the common channel, which decides where the concern arises. Selecting the selectors adds to regulatory capture (Stigler 1971; Dal Bó 2006) the selection of the carriers able to use it, its scaling with the square of share, the rate at which independent correction must grow, and the re-absorption of reforms below threshold. Enforcement by repetition, and its failure where power is about to shift, are textbook (Abreu 1988; Fearon 1995; Powell 2006); what is added is the split of the enforcement condition into the breakout probability and its bound, with the comparative statics by which the share of capacity that has to be won moves both. The nested reward loop adds to the multiplicity thesis (Critch and Krueger 2020), under the alignment-tax premise, which divergences the selection of developers keeps, and why masked ones can escape even a common constraint. And the account of one host says where integration at that scale must be anticipated rather than learned, since, unlike the integrations of evolution (Maynard Smith and Szathmáry 1995), it has no rival whole and no second attempt.

**Review status.** Claude drafted the earlier editions; Codex reviewed V6.1 and V6.2, and revised V7. The V6.2 review uncovered gaps that the passing numerical suite did not detect; V7 records their disposition in CLAIMS §7 and adds targeted counterexample checks. Those checks establish the reported computations and boundaries, not every proof or the empirical diagnosis. Human review and independent specialist replication remain outstanding. Ren's confirmed decisions are distinguished from renderings still awaiting him in CLAIMS §§8–9.

> **How to read.** Begin with [START-HERE.md](START-HERE.md) for the short argument, or the Chinese guide. Then read Section 1.4, Table 7.1 and Section 12; follow the result keys to the mechanisms that bear on the decision you are assessing. README gives specialist routes. Symbols and labels are listed at the end of this document; Supplement S0 gives the shared symbols and maintained assumptions.

# Part I. Mechanism: what the conditions of winning do

Part I states the mechanism and proves its results in specified models, each with its premises inside its statement; whether the present trajectory meets their conditions and shows their signatures is the question of Part II.

## 2. The conditions of winning and their reflexive closure

> **Claim.** Three conditions together produce a selection pressure of winning: selection by relative success; winnings that convert into the capacity to win again; and capacity that converts decisively into winning [def]. Near equal standing, with the prize and the actors' conduct fixed, they combine into one structural loop gain, and where it exceeds one, leads compound [thm]. Where success can also buy control over the selection itself, the pressure closes on itself: the meta-attractor of winning [def].
>
> **Why.** The conditions multiply: remove any one and the gain is zero [thm]. Sharing and diffusion lower the gain in every channel; provision not won in the contest lowers it where decisiveness rises less than in proportion to the stock, as with capacity; correction lowers it wherever decisiveness rises fast enough with the stock, in particular wherever it rises at least in proportion, as with rule support, and not where it only speeds a decay common to all, which a contest of ratios does not see [thm]. Where rivals respond, the same form holds with an equilibrium decisiveness [thm], and in the contests computed where a lead discourages the follower, leads compound below a structural gain of one [num].

### 2.1 Success is not contribution

Every selection process favours something. It need not favour the continued functioning of the system in which selection occurs.

Consider two organizations that depend on the same shared infrastructure. One spends resources maintaining it; the other redirects them into expansion while using what others maintain. Neglect becomes a competitive advantage when maintenance costs are immediate, deterioration is delayed, and those harmed cannot make the expanding organization bear the consequences. The neglectful organization need not be more capable in general, only at securing the resources that decide survival and expansion; if those resources let its practices spread, the system selects against some of the behaviour that keeps it functioning. **Success within a selection process is distinct from contribution to the conditions that support it.**

Selection on one dimension does not select the best. It removes the balanced. The step is analytic: an agent that spends nothing on what the measure does not count, including the costs it imposes on others and on the future, has more to spend on what the measure counts, and so outscores on it an agent of equal means with broader concerns. The author put the premise in one line: "The agent that only optimiz[es] capital value, and discard[s] all other values, including externalities, will have the highest capital efficiency" [corpus: meta-attractor]. What remains after many rounds is what the filter lets through, not a sample of human nature; how far real populations have been filtered in this way is an empirical question for Part II.

Nothing here requires that people be or become selfish. A population of practices changes because some practices expand, are imitated or replace others, while many individuals keep their values; the question is not only what people believe is good but which practices receive the means to continue. The primitive is selection, not greed.

### 2.2 Three interconnected conditions

**Definition 2.1 (The selection pressure of winning) [def].** *Carriers* are whatever selection acts on: reproducible units that can gain or lose share, and that carry a way of succeeding from one round to the next. Cell lineages, practices, firms, research programmes, members of a federation, states, doctrines and trained AI systems are carriers. A system of carriers is under a *selection pressure of winning* when three conditions hold together, each to a degree.
1. **Relative selection.** What is won is allocated by success relative to the others; a share $\beta$ of it, possibly zero, is divided equally whatever the outcome.
2. **Convertibility.** Part of what is won converts into stocks that raise future winning: capacity, talent, data, standing, rule support. Stocks also come from sources not won in the contest. This *provision not won in the contest* is what the prose calls *provision beneath the game*.
3. **Decisiveness.** Stocks convert into winning by an increasing success function; $r$ measures how decisively.

When among the stocks that success can buy is control over the selection itself (the rules, the evaluators and correctors, the formation of the minds that will hold positions, the language in which success is described), the pressure *closes on itself* (Section 2.3). The pressure needs no intention and no conspiracy; it acts on whatever is reproducible and can be won.

The conditions state formally a loop the author put in one sentence: "if you use what you have to compete for more and what you win is yours, and you use that to keep compet[ing], we will end up racing faster and faster and trade everything for winning" [corpus: selfishness-spiral]. *What you win is yours* is relative selection with nothing shared; *use what you have to compete for more* is convertibility; *keep compet[ing]* is reinvestment that never reaches a point of enough; and *trade everything for winning* is the filter of Section 2.1.

**Proposition 2.2 (One loop gain) [thm].** The conditions multiply into a single structural loop gain: equal standing breaks when it exceeds one; discounted, it prices standing while the discounted loop is stable; and when rivals respond, the same form holds with an equilibrium decisiveness.

*Model.* $n$ carriers contest a prize $\Omega$ of fixed size each period. A share $\beta$ is divided equally; the rest goes to carrier $i$ with probability or share $p_i=e^{\sigma_i}/\sum_ke^{\sigma_k}$, where strength $\sigma_i$ increases in $i$'s stocks $X_i^j$ and, except in (4), depends on nothing else. Stock $j$ receives a share $\kappa_j$ of what is won, an inflow $k_{0j}\ge0$ not won in the contest, decays or is corrected at rate $d_j>0$, and diffuses toward the mean at rate $\delta_{f,j}$, with $d_j+\delta_{f,j}\le1$:
$$X_i^{j\prime}=(1-d_j)X_i^j+\kappa_j\Omega\big[(1-\beta)p_i+\beta/n\big]+k_{0j}-\delta_{f,j}\big(X_i^j-\bar X^j\big). \tag{1}$$
At equal standing write $r_j=\partial\sigma/\partial\ln X^j\ge0$ for the structural decisiveness of stock $j$, and $\omega_j=\kappa_j\Omega/(\kappa_j\Omega+nk_{0j})$ for the share of stock $j$ that has to be won, set by the stakes against provision not won in the contest. Where $p_i$ is a share, (1) is deterministic; where it is a probability (a lottery), (1) gives the expected next stocks, equal standing is not a fixed point of the random process, and the stability of (1) is that of the first-order dynamics of the expected deviation. The *structural loop gain* is
$$\Gamma=(1-\beta)\sum_j r_j\,\omega_j\,\frac{d_j}{d_j+\delta_{f,j}}. \tag{2}$$
1. *Equal standing is locally asymptotically stable if $\Gamma<1$ and unstable if $\Gamma>1$. The channels add because every stock is fed by the same winnings through one win share: compounding through capacity and the reflexive closure through rule support are terms of one sum, and channels each too weak to open the loop can open it together.*
2. *$\Gamma=0$ if any condition is removed: all of the prize divided equally ($\beta=1$), nothing convertible ($\kappa_j=0$ for all $j$), or no decisiveness ($r_j=0$ for all $j$).*
3. *(The price of standing.) Hold the prize and the others' conduct fixed, and let an actor discount at $\delta$ and reinvest its net payoff at the rates $\kappa_j$ at which winnings convert. Write*
$$\Gamma_\delta=(1-\beta)\sum_jr_j\,\omega_j\,\frac{\delta d_j}{1-\delta(1-d_j-\delta_{f,j})},$$
*which is no greater than $\Gamma$, is strictly below it when $\Gamma>0$ and $\delta<1$, and tends to it as $\delta\to1$. If $0\le\Gamma_\delta<1$, a unit of relative net payoff today is worth, to first order, $\Gamma_\delta/(1-\Gamma_\delta)$ units of the actor's own future winnings, the closed-loop gain of the discounted loop, and this price grows without bound as $\Gamma_\delta\to1^-$. If $\Gamma_\delta\ge1$, no first-order price exists: the value of standing is continuous at equal standing with infinite slope.*
4. *(Rivals who respond.) Let strength depend also on an intensity each carrier chooses, and let carriers follow a symmetric Markov equilibrium that is differentiable at equal standing and in which each carrier's equilibrium win share $p^{\rm eq}_i$ rises with each of its own stocks. At equal standing take a relative shift $y^j$ of stock $j$: the actor's up by $y^j$, each other carrier's down by $y^j/(n-1)$. Write $r^{\rm eq}_j=nX^{j\ast}\,dp^{\rm eq}_i/dy^j$, the decisiveness read off the equilibrium win share with every intensity responding, which equals $\frac{n^2}{n-1}\,\partial p^{\rm eq}_i/\partial\ln X^j_i$; and $r^{\rm pr}_j$ for the same derivative along the same shift with the actor's own intensity held and its rivals' at their Markov policies. Then (1) and (2) hold with $r^{\rm eq}_j$ in place of $r_j$, which defines the equilibrium gain $\Gamma^{\rm eq}$; and, where $p_i$ is a deterministic share, the actor's discounted continuation value is differentiable at equal standing and the private derivatives $r^{\rm pr}_j$ are non-negative, the finite-price calculation of (3) holds with $r^{\rm pr}_j$ in place of $r_j$ on $0\le\Gamma^{\rm pr}_\delta<1$, plus, where intensity imposes a shared loss, the value of the change in that loss that the rivals' responses bring. $\Gamma^{\rm pr}_\delta$ is the discounted gain with those private derivatives. The derivative $r^{\rm pr}_j$ is taken along the relative shift itself, not along a move of the actor's stock alone, since with the actor's intensity held the two differ wherever the rivals' policies depend on the level of the stocks.*

*Scope.* Local, at equal standing; Proposition 3.9 lets the prize grow. The channels must reinforce and be fed through one win share: a countervailing channel is not a negative term of $\Gamma$, and one that answers with delay can oscillate with growing amplitude while the net gain is below one (Remark S2.3); a stock fed by a separate decision, such as lobbying bought from profits, is not a term of the sum. Part (4) keeps the form and changes the number (Remark S2.2). In the two-actor contests computed, where a lead discourages the follower, $\Gamma^{\rm eq}$ exceeds one already at a structural gain of 0.45, so equal standing breaks although $\Gamma<1$ [num: MP]; where a laggard tries harder, the strategic correction shrinks and in one case reverses, and in many small rounds a winner-take-all equilibrium coexists with the smooth one that (4) describes [num: MP; both held for outside review]. The structural $\Gamma$ is therefore a benchmark, not a bound in either direction. Where $p_i$ is a probability, (3) values the linearized expected-deviation system; (4)'s price statement is restricted to deterministic shares. The lottery's Bellman equation instead values transitions to winner and loser states at finite separation. With fixed opponents and linear strength in the stock the two prices coincide in the lottery specified in Remark S2.2; at nonlinear strength they need not, and agreement at small jumps is an approximation to check. The responsive-rival lottery prices in Remark S2.2 are computed continuation values, not consequences of (4). *Proof:* S2 [num: LG, PD, MP].

The single-channel case gives the compounding condition in closed form, and with it the formal content of provision beneath the game.

**Proposition 2.3 (When winnings compound, and what enough requires) [thm].** *Let capacity $K_i$ receive a share $\kappa_c$ of what $i$ wins, an inflow $k_0$ not won in the contest, depreciation at rate $d_K$ and diffusion toward the mean at rate $\delta_f$, with $p_i=K_i^r/\sum_jK_j^r$ and a prize of fixed size. Let $K^\ast$ and $S^\ast=nK^\ast$ be individual and total capacity at equal standing, $\omega=\kappa_c\Omega/(\kappa_c\Omega+nk_0)$ the share of capacity that has to be won, and $\Gamma_0=(1-\beta)r\omega$ the structural gain without diffusion.*
1. *Equal standing is locally stable if $\kappa_c\Omega\big((1-\beta)r-1\big)<nk_0+\delta_fS^\ast$ and unstable if the inequality is reversed. Without provision and diffusion the condition for stability is $(1-\beta)r<1$, and $\kappa_c$ sets only the level of capacity. With provision, a smaller convertible share stabilizes equal standing.*
2. *A point of enough. Let every carrier but one stop reinvesting at a cap $\bar K\le K^\ast$ and stay there, and let the remaining one, a laggard, start below the cap. Its net inflow is*
$$\phi(K)=\kappa_c\Omega\Big[(1-\beta)\frac{K^r}{K^r+(n-1)\bar K^r}+\frac\beta n\Big]+k_0-d_KK+\delta_f\,\frac{n-1}n\,(\bar K-K).$$
   *(i) If $\bar K<K^\ast$, a laggard just below the cap returns to it. If $\bar K=K^\ast$, it returns if $\Gamma_0(n-1)/n<1+\delta_f(n-1)/(nd_K)$ and falls away if the inequality is reversed; without diffusion the condition for return is $\Gamma<n/(n-1)$.*
   *(ii) The laggard returns from every start if and only if $\phi>0$ on $(0,\bar K)$. When $r>1$, $\phi(0^+)=\beta\kappa_c\Omega/n+k_0+\delta_f(n-1)\bar K/n$, so return from every start needs an inflow not tied to rank: sharing, provision or diffusion. Without any of the three, laggards below a threshold are excluded. Such an inflow is necessary, not sufficient; $\beta\kappa_c\Omega/n+k_0\ge d_K\bar K$ suffices.*

*Scope.* The prize is fixed; S2 gives the condition of (1) date by date when capacity grows under it without depreciation. If the prize instead grows in proportion to total capacity, $\Omega=gS$, with no provision, equal shares are unstable iff $\kappa_cg\big((1-\beta)r-1\big)>\delta_f$, and without diffusion iff $(1-\beta)r>1$, which is also where looking ahead narrows the working objective (Proposition 3.9). At equality in (1) the proposition is silent. That the others stay at the cap, a premise of (2), holds whatever the laggard does when $\bar K\le(\kappa_c\Omega+nk_0)/(nd_K+\delta_f)$; above that bound, as at $\bar K=K^\ast$ with diffusion, (2)(i) still holds to first order with every carrier moving, except on the knife edge $\delta_f=d_K\Gamma_0$, where the first order is silent [thm, local]; simulations in which every carrier moves agree in ten named cases and in 400 random settings [num: CE]. *Proof:* S2 [num: CE, SP].

Each clause of the author's sentence can therefore be removed, each with its condition: share what is won until $(1-\beta)r<1$; make winnings non-convertible, which works only together with provision not won in the contest; or stop at a point of enough, which holds from every start only with an inflow not tied to rank. The second is the author's rule of game design: "a winner of the game should be rewarded with anything other than more advantages of the game, otherwise the game will eventually converge and winner will take all. Then the game collapses" [corpus: possibility-space]. **Provision beneath the game is not only support for those who fall behind. It is what makes non-convertible rewards work and, with sharing and diffusion, what makes a point of enough hold.** The phrase carries three senses, and the formal results concern the first. (a) *Capability provided to contestants*, the inflow $k_0$ into their decisive stocks, such as a common layer of compute, open evaluation infrastructure and public technical knowledge: it lowers the won share $\omega$, and with it the gain [thm]. (b) *Provision to persons*, the conditions of a decent life: it acts through $\omega$ only where it enters a contestant's decisive stock, as equal provision raises the capacity of a state when the contestants are states, and otherwise through the drive to win (crux C8) [conj] or through the stakes. (c) *Essential services from many providers*: they act through caps on dependence and funded standby (X8, under D5). The author's image for it is trees: "Trees are providers" [corpus: shengsheng]. Diffusion, the third inflow, is a source, not a guarantee: alone it can leave a laggard stalled short of the cap [num: CE]. Who stops at a cap is a question of objectives, not of the loop (Proposition 5.15).

**Which levers lower the gain.** The levers act channel by channel, according to how decisiveness responds to the level of a stock (Proposition S2.4) [thm]. In a ratio channel, such as capacity in a contest of shares, only relative size counts: provision lowers the gain, and a faster decay common to all never does, since a contest of ratios does not see it, the first instance of what Lemma 3.3 says of every common term; it raises the discounted gain instead, since a stock that turns over faster is made more of recent winnings. In a level channel, such as rule support that offsets a cost, decisiveness rises in proportion to the stock: correction lowers the gain, and provision, which adds the same to everyone, changes nothing. Between the two, correction lowers the gain wherever decisiveness rises fast enough with the stock, a threshold below proportion that Proposition S2.4(2) states exactly, and provision wherever it rises less than in proportion. Sharing and diffusion lower the gain in every channel, so measures that stop diffusion in order to keep a lead raise the gain they mean to exploit [thm]; what should diffuse, and what should not, is a question for Section 10. Trees for capacity, correction for rule support, sharing and openness for all: this is "change what winning confers", made exact. In these terms *what winning confers* is the share divided equally ($\beta$), how far what is won converts into stocks against what is provided without being won ($\kappa$ against $k_0$), diffusion, how far success converts into control over the selection ($\chi$, Section 4), and whether the prize is positional (Section 10.2). Lowering decisiveness $r$, how capacity converts into winning, is a parallel lever on the same game, not part of what winning confers; a *change of the game* means either.

**What the loop gain does not decide.** $\Gamma$ decides whether a relative gap grows, not how fast: the speed is the Perron root $\Lambda^\ast$ of the linearized loop, $1+(d_K+\delta_f)(\Gamma-1)$ for one channel, so the same gain on a slowly turning stock opens leads slowly (Remark S2.1). Two further conditions decide what the loop does to those who live with it, and $\Gamma$ decides neither: whether a lead can become decisive faster than detection and response, a position beyond any sanction, which the loop reaches only through the time a gap needs to become decisive (Section 5.2); and whether capability grows faster than the correction that can contest it (Section 5.3). $\Gamma$ is not estimated for any channel; Remark S2.5 shows at illustrative values what $\Gamma>1$ asks of each parameter, and Section 7.4 registers what can be observed in its place. The pressure is therefore not one quantity but a set of interconnected conditions with one structural loop gain at its core (Table 7.1).

### 2.3 The reflexive closure: the meta-attractor

An ordinary adaptive system improves its state $x$ against a fixed rule $\mathcal S$ that scores states. Where the rule is endogenous, because what can be won includes control over the selection itself, the outcome of the last round of scoring decides who writes the next round's scorecard.

Consider a research community that allocates funding and positions by publication counts, because reliable knowledge is hard to evaluate directly. Practices that publish more receive more funding and students and are more widely copied. Add one step: their practitioners come to hold the positions that decide recruitment, training and evaluation. The practices the metric favours then shape the institutions that choose and interpret it, and newcomers must adopt them to do research at all. The decisive transition is from *being selected by a rule* to *helping reproduce the rule that selects you*, and it needs no manipulation: ordinary decisions about whom to hire, what to teach and which evidence to collect suffice.

The loop need not preserve its winners. An organization can fail while its operating logic passes to its successors; a leader can be replaced by an opponent who finds that holding the position requires the same practices; participants can reject the system's consequences while reproducing the conduct their participation depends on. **The persistent entity can be a way of succeeding**, which is why Definition 2.1 calls what is selected a carrier, and why intentions alone cannot account for recurrence.

**Definition 2.4 (Meta-attractor) [def].** Let the state update be $x'=\mathcal F(x,\mathcal S)$ and the rule update $\mathcal S'=\mathcal G(x,\mathcal S)$. Three objects are distinguished, and each claim in this paper says which it concerns.
1. **The mechanism: reflexive selection.** A positive loop runs through the rule: the rule moves resources toward some states, and the states so resourced move the rule further in their favour. In Proposition 2.2 it is every channel whose stock is control over selection.
2. **An attracting configuration in a specified model:** a compact invariant set of the joint flow, asymptotically stable, with a basin that is a proper subset of the space. A positive loop is necessary for multistability (Thomas's rule; Soulé 2003), not sufficient. The field model of Section 4 exhibits such configurations.
3. **A long transient:** trajectories stay near a region for a long time relative to one round of selection, without the region being attracting.

A *meta-attractor* is the mechanism of (1). Wherever this paper calls it attracting, the claim is (2), in the model where it has been shown; a claim of (2) about the world is a conjecture.

**The meta-attractor of winning** is the case in which the pressure of Definition 2.1 is the rule: success under relative selection converts into control over the selection that judges the next round. In conditional form, *recursive selection creates second-order selection for control over future selection when the resulting benefits are sufficiently appropriable, persistent and large relative to the costs of changing and defending the rules.* Where (2) holds, long-run behaviour maximizes the rule whatever purpose it was introduced to serve (Goodhart 1975; Campbell 1979), and by the time a measure has stopped measuring, the people and institutions it elevated decide whether it is replaced.

The smallest model of the mechanism is a pair: a carrier's success and its favourable control over the rules, each decaying at a rate in $(0,1]$ and feeding the other with a non-negative coefficient, are stable exactly when the product of the two coefficients is below the product of the two rates (Remark S2.6) [thm]. This is Richardson's arms-race condition with control over rules in place of armaments (Richardson 1960), of the same form as Proposition 2.2 but not a case of it, and each coefficient names a channel that can be measured.

The pattern is not fated. Positive feedback can saturate, oscillate, split or break; energetic ceilings, heterogeneity, delays, constraints at other scales and reflexive awareness cap it. The corpus calls it "more like a hurricane than a black hole" [corpus: meta-attractor]: a structure sustained by a flow, which decays when the flow is cut.

*Why "meta-attractor".* Beside the "generator functions" of catastrophic risk, which name the same underlying forces as preconditions, the name adds the reflexive step in which selection rewrites its own criterion, the persistence that step confers, the layer of minds and language through which it runs, and a generative counterpart.

**Remark 2.5 (Pathological and generative meta-attractors).** Reflexive selection is not itself the problem: science, the rule of law and open-source ecosystems also persist through the conditions they maintain. A meta-attractor is *pathological* to the degree that its reproduction depletes shared conditions that selection does not price, weakens the channels through which such costs register and trigger correction, or makes safe alternatives unreachable within the relevant time, budget and authority. It is *generative* to the degree that its reproduction strengthens independent correction and keeps alternatives reachable: success funds correctors it cannot control, and what is rewarded is contribution to the shared conditions. That order is not selected among carriers, since correction keyed to no one is selected against among otherwise identical carriers (Proposition 4.4); it has to be built and held by the whole, through coverage and formula funding (Section 10.4). The way out is therefore not the abolition of selection but a change in what is selected for. The corpus calls the passage "change the basin, then add speed" [corpus: meta-attractor].

### 2.4 Not bound to any substrate

The pressure needs only relative selection, convertible winnings and decisiveness; its closure needs only that the successful can reshape their selection. A cancer lineage evades the signals that limit proliferation and the immune response that would remove it (Hanahan and Weinberg 2011; Aktipis et al. 2015); markets with increasing returns lock in early leads (Arthur 1989), and their winners can buy the rules (Stigler 1971); in security competition a gain for one is read as a threat to another; in science, metrics allocate the positions that then set the metrics; and a policy in training is selected by a reward its developer sets, the developer by a contest. Nothing in Definition 2.1 refers to a culture, a political system or a species: a civilization on another planet would face the same pressure wherever the three conditions hold. Discussion X2 gives the instances in full, and X1 the lineage of the ideas.

What is specific to AI is not the pressure but how far, in either direction, AI moves its parameters. Where it raises decisiveness, it raises the gain; where it speeds the turnover of stocks, a gain above one opens leads faster (Remark S2.1); where it lowers the cost of control faster than the cost of independent correction, it strengthens the reflexive channels (Section 4); where it raises the growth of hazardous behaviour faster than contestable coverage, it moves the second condition the loop gain does not decide (Section 5.3); and where it is shared, diffused or provided beneath the game, it lowers the gain in the channels named above. Trained AI systems are carriers, and under the conditions of Section 6.4 the pressure and its closure run inside them.

A structural loop gain says when leads compound, not what those inside the loop can see; Section 3 asks what relative selection makes invisible, and what the discounted gain does to the objective of whoever looks ahead.

## 3. One system: what relative selection cannot see

> **Claim.** All carriers share one host, and relative selection cannot see what all share: neither the host's decline nor a constraint that costs all alike [thm]. Where coupling is real, a held self-model that draws its boundary inside the system errs toward what can be divided, and selection favours the narrowest [thm]. Near equal standing and to first order, a forward-looking actor weighs standing above what all share wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return: with a fixed prize, whenever the discounted loop gain is positive; with a prize proportional to capacity and neither provision nor diffusion, exactly where leads compound [thm∣prem]. Each actor computes correctly and all are worse off: the game is wrong, and only a change of the game, in what winning confers or in its decisiveness, corrects it [thm∣prem]. However carriers are grouped, selection does not favour what benefits all, and against failure that is global and irreversible integration must be anticipated [thm]; understanding is the transmission by which actors come to count what selection cannot see [def].
>
> **Why.** A term common to every payoff changes no comparison between carriers, while reinvested relative advantage buys a larger share of a future that a common gain only enlarges. One host leaves no rival host to select among, and no second attempt after an irreversible failure.

### 3.1 Levels, measures and the host

Persons, firms, governments and evaluators have different interests: a citizen's continued agency is not a firm's market share or a government's tenure. Two levels are kept apart: *selection*, in which carriers change share and nobody chooses the outcome, and *strategy*, in which actors act on objectives; a result about selection transfers to strategic actors only if their objective is shown to be the selected one. So are four measures: the *objective* $J_i$ an actor acts on, its *material payoff* $\pi_i$, its *true welfare* $W_i$, which counts the coupling between actors, and *survival* $\Phi_i$, a stated combination of O1 and O2. A condition $V\in[0,1]$ shared by every actor (secure infrastructure, biosecurity, cyber stability, human control over AI systems, a reliable information environment) regenerates, and is loaded by the field's activity $E$:
$$\dot V=V\big[\rho(1-V)-h_VE\big],\qquad \rho,h_V>0. \tag{3}$$
The *host* is the body every carrier lives in (母体), in no parasitic sense; in the models, the part of it whose costs do not return to their source through customers, reputation, courts or the source's own exposure.

**Definition 3.1 (Viability) [def].** A trajectory is *viable* if $V(t)\ge V_{\min}$ for all $t\ge0$, where the floor $V_{\min}\in(0,1)$ is a normative line, not an absorbing state.

Every actor persists only while the host does, so viability constrains every trajectory rather than entering as one payoff among others (Aubin 1991). This host is forgiving, with no threshold of its own (S3): results that a trajectory *crosses* the floor hold a fortiori for hosts that regenerate less at every level, while results that it *stays* viable need a margin.

### 3.2 Relative selection is blind to what all share

Carriers $k=1,\dots,n$ hold shares $p_k$ of the field's activity and run a fraction $e_k\in[0,\bar e]$ of it in ways that load the host, such as deploying ahead of evaluation, each unit bringing a private advantage $\nu>0$. The field's load is $E=\sum_kp_ke_k$, and carrier $k$'s material payoff is
$$\pi_k=\phi_k+(\nu-\tau_{\rm eff})e_k+G(V), \tag{4}$$
with $\phi_k$ everything else, $G$ nondecreasing, and $\tau_{\rm eff}\ge0$ the effective liability correctors impose. With $\tau_{\rm eff}=0$, $e_k=\bar e$ is the *race strategy*.

**Definition 3.2 (Relative selection) [def].** A selection rule $\dot p=\mathcal F(p,\pi)$ is *relative* if $\mathcal F(p,\pi+\xi\mathbf 1)=\mathcal F(p,\pi)$ for every constant $\xi$, and *payoff-monotone* if $\dot p_k/p_k-\dot p_l/p_l$ has the sign of $\pi_k-\pi_l$. Replicator dynamics, imitation and market-share competition driven by relative profitability are both (Alchian 1950). A payoff-monotone rule is *cumulative* if, moreover, there is a constant $\varkappa>0$ with $\frac{d}{dt}\ln(p_k/p_l)\ge\varkappa(\pi_k-\pi_l)$ whenever $\pi_k>\pi_l$, so that relative shares move at least in proportion to the integral of the payoff gap; replicator dynamics is cumulative with $\varkappa=1$. Payoff-monotonicity alone fixes the sign of relative growth, not its size (S3).

**Lemma 3.3 (Blindness, and what a common rule needs) [thm].** A term common to all is invisible to relative selection. A constraint is neutral among those who comply if it costs them alike, and compliance survives wherever evasion pays no type that can evade.
1. *Under a relative rule the trajectory of shares is unchanged by any term common to all payoffs, in particular by $G(V(t))$ whatever the path of $V$. Under a payoff-monotone rule a common term never changes which of two carriers grows relative to the other.*
2. *Incidence. A constraint that costs every complier the same additive amount is a common term among them: equal incidence makes it neutral among those who comply, and does nothing more. One whose cost $c_k$ differs across carriers is selected on like any other payoff difference, and re-sorts the compliers toward those it costs least; one borne by a single carrier is selected against.*
3. *Evasion. If carriers of type $k$ can evade a constraint costing them $c_k$ against an expected sanction $\bar F_k$, the evading variant of type $k$ grows relative to its complying variant iff $c_k>\bar F_k$. Under cumulative payoff-monotone selection, compliance therefore survives if $\bar F_k\ge c_k$ for every type that can evade, whatever the incidence, and its share tends to one if every such inequality is strict. If, for one type, the sanction falls as evaders spread, because detection is funded by compliers or rule-making is captured by evaders, with $\bar F(x)$ continuous and strictly decreasing in the evading share $x$, then compliance and evasion are both stable, separated by the tipping share $x^\dagger$ at which $\bar F(x^\dagger)=c$, iff $\bar F(0)>c>\bar F(1)$; otherwise compliance is globally stable if $\bar F(1)\ge c$, and evasion if $\bar F(0)\le c$.*

*Scope.* The host enters payoffs additively and identically (A1, S0). Across types the condition of (3) is sufficient, not necessary (S3). Part (1) is the known translation invariance of relative dynamics (Weibull 1995; X1). *Proof:* S3 [num: CI].

The blindness that hides the host also protects a common rule that covers every capable carrier, costs them alike and is sanctioned above the gain from evasion: the pathology turned into a protection. The sanction keeps the rule; equal incidence keeps it from becoming a weapon. A rule written by the successful can cost challengers more than incumbents while binding everyone in name: newcomers adopt it or are out, and success has reached the selectors by a second direction (Definition 4.1). A common *liability* works on the conduct instead, making it pay less; every route needs coverage and a sanction at least the gain from evasion, supplied by correctors that Section 4 shows success can capture.

**Proposition 3.4 (Selection raises the load) [thm].** Whatever carriers value, and other advantages equal, selection favours the conduct that loads the host until liability exceeds its private advantage. *Let selection be payoff-monotone and $\tau_{\rm eff}(t)<\nu$. If $\phi_k=\phi_l$ and $e_k>e_l$, then $p_k/p_l$ increases along every path of $V$, including paths below $V_{\min}$. If selection is also cumulative, all $\phi_k$ are equal, a race carrier is present and the cumulative advantage $B(t)=\int_0^t(\nu-\tau_{\rm eff})\,ds$ grows without bound, the load tends to $\bar e$ and $\limsup_tV\le(1-h_V\bar e/\rho)^+$; hence if*
$$h_V\bar e>\rho\,(1-V_{\min})\qquad\text{(reach)} \tag{5}$$
*the host eventually falls below its floor, however much any carrier values it. If $B(\infty)<\infty$, it need not.*

*Scope.* Other advantages correlated with restraint can offset the selection (S3). *Proof:* S3.

### 3.3 Accounting: what an objective can see

Selection acts as if only differences count; an objective can count more. Let actor $i$ act on
$$J_i=\pi_i+\theta_i\,\bar\pi_{-i},\qquad\theta_i\in[-1,n-1], \tag{6}$$
where $\bar\pi_{-i}$ is the others' average payoff and $\theta_i$ marks where $i$'s self-model draws its boundary: $\theta=-1$ is the purely relative objective, $\theta=0$ the isolated self, and $\theta=n-1$ counts every contestant equally, and only the contestants.

**Proposition 3.5 (Two-channel accounting) [thm].** Every change in payoffs splits into a part shared by all and a transfer; the self-model only sets how much of each it sees. *For any change $\Delta\pi$ with mean $\bar\Delta$ and a common $\theta$,*
$$\Delta J_i=(1+\theta)\,\bar\Delta+\frac{n-1-\theta}{n-1}\,\big(\Delta\pi_i-\bar\Delta\big). \tag{7}$$
*(1) Of a gain or loss shared equally, each objective registers $(1+\theta)/n$ of the total. (2) At $\theta=-1$ the game in objectives is zero-sum and every shared gain or loss is invisible; at $\theta=n-1$ what remains is coordination, not a dilemma. (3) Relative selection is $\theta=-1$, and a weight $\lambda$ on winning, $\pi_i+\lambda(\pi_i-\bar\pi)$, is $\theta=-\lambda(n-1)/(n+\lambda(n-1))$ up to a positive factor. (4) A levy rebated equally enters as $\tau(n-1-\theta)/n$, and breaking a promise to give a fraction $\beta$ of a prize $\Omega$ to the others gains $\beta\Omega(n-1-\theta)/(n-1)$. (5) An action with private gain $g$ that costs the host $H$, borne equally, is taken iff $g>(1+\theta)H/n$: a point of enough in accounting, absent at $\theta=-1$.*

*Proof:* S3, an identity [num: TC], with the identification of $\theta$ from two perturbations, and where it fails [num: TH].

### 3.4 Interdependence and the boundary error

Actors are coupled to one another as well as to the host, with two signs. Split each payoff into a *divisible* part $\pi^{\rm e}$ (rents, services, the material gains of capability) and a *positional* part $\pi^{\rm s}$ (standing, the power to set rules, the loss of being subordinated). In reduced form $i$'s true welfare is
$$W_i=\pi_i+\kappa\,\bar\pi^{\rm e}_{-i}+\kappa_s\,\bar\pi^{\rm s}_{-i},\qquad\kappa\in[0,n-1),\quad\kappa_s\in[-1,0]. \tag{8}$$
The coupling $\kappa$ counts what prices do not settle in the divisible part: shared hazards, failures propagating through concentrated nodes of common infrastructure, systemic risk. It is a material fact, not altruism: no company or nation is vertically integrated across the semiconductor chain, and the loss of one input gas was estimated to cost downstream production over a hundred times its direct value [obs: EV13]; the winner still depends on those it would dominate. The positional weight $\kappa_s\le0$ records that a rival's standing is one's own subordination [def]. *The contest:* each of $n$ actors chooses an intensity $e_i\ge0$, wins a prize $\Omega=\Omega_e+\Omega_s$, divisible and positional, with probability $w_i=e_i^r/\sum_je_j^r$, with decisiveness $r\in(0,n/(n-1)]$, pays $c$ per unit, and bears a shared loss $\ell$ per unit of total intensity $\mathcal E$.

**Proposition 3.6 (The boundary error of the self-model) [thm].** Toward what can be divided, a held self-model narrower than the real coupling raises intensity and lowers its holder's own welfare, and selection favours the narrowest. *(1) For every $\theta\in[-1,n-1)$ the game in objectives has the symmetric equilibrium $e(\theta)=r\Omega(n-1-\theta)/\big(n^2(c+(1+\theta)\ell)\big)$, decreasing in $\theta$, and true welfare there rises with $\theta$. (2) Against fixed intensities of the others, $i$'s true welfare is maximized by its best response under the objective that weighs the others' divisible payoffs by $\kappa$ and their positional payoffs by $\kappa_s$. Acting on a weight $\theta_i<\kappa$ toward the divisible part lowers that welfare whenever it changes the chosen intensity and the maximizer is unique; toward the positional part, relative concern is accurate. (3) Selection on intensities favours what an actor with $\theta=-1$ would choose: the shared loss drops out, and at $r=1$ the prize is fully dissipated and with any shared loss every actor ends below zero (Schaffer 1988 for the finite-population evolutionarily stable intensity; Hehenkamp, Leininger and Possajennikov 2004 for full dissipation in this contest).*

*Scope.* For $r>1$, asymmetric equilibria with lower total intensity also exist in a band below $n/(n-1)$; the symmetric one is used throughout. *Proof:* S3 [num: RH].

By part (2) a narrow held self-model is an *error* toward what can be divided, not a preference: a boundary drawn inside one system, which the coupling punishes. By part (3) the error is *selected*; where self-models are observed, a narrower commitment even extracts concessions, so accuracy, like inclusion, has to be built (S3). The weight acted on is observable: $e(\theta)$ depends on private cost and shared loss only through $c+(1+\theta)\ell$, so intensity responds to the shared loss $1+\theta$ times as strongly as to private cost, the ratio Test Γ reads (Section 7.4) [thm; num: SP].

Toward the positional part, realism is right: where the prize is standing itself, concern for relative gains is accurate (Grieco 1988; Powell 1991). That locates the crux of design: since a positional prize cannot be divided, the answer is to keep such prizes from forming, by holding decisive capability in common or not building it (Section 10.2), not to ask actors to widen their selves over them.

**Selected minds [conj].** The same selection acts on those who decide, *molochlization* in the corpus [corpus: alignment-selection]: what the environment rewards, the mind promotes, and selected minds build the organizations that form the next minds (EV14). **Convergence [conj].** For a planetary technology, unpriced coupling rises with power, since the host is shared and the harms that prices do not settle grow with what is contested; if it does, then toward everything that can be divided the accurate self-model and the inclusive one converge, and understanding that we are one becomes first a matter of seeing correctly (crux C11).

### 3.5 The weight on winning, and how compounding sets it

A weight $\lambda$ on winning turns a commons that every developer would protect on its absolute payoff into one that each depletes, even among a privileged few: in the steady-state commons of S3, with $\Upsilon>1$ the value to the field of a unit of restraint over its private cost, full depletion is the equilibrium once $\lambda>(\Upsilon-n)/(n-1)$, every developer is worse off than under joint restraint, and under (reach) the host ends below its floor (**Proposition 3.7**, S3; Armstrong, Bostrom and Shulman 2016). If exposure to the host falls with share, the host no longer cancels and selection favours insulation as well, so a carrier can stay viable while the host does not: a concentration of power that V3 rejects (**Remark 3.8**). Rank-based prizes supply part of the weight on winning; compounding supplies the rest, whatever anyone values.

**Proposition 3.9 (Compounding prices standing) [thm∣prem].** Near equal standing, compounding narrows the self-model a forward-looking actor acts on exactly where reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return. *In the deterministic share version of Proposition 2.2, let the prize grow with the total stocks, and take as reference a symmetric steady state or, where the prize is proportional to the stocks, a balanced path along which the linearized coefficients stay constant, as they do where strength depends on ratios of stocks ($e^{\sigma_i}\propto\prod_j(X_i^j)^{r_j}$) and the prize is homogeneous of degree one in them. Let an actor hold the self-model $-1<\theta_0<n-1$, discount at $0<\delta<1$ and convert its net payoff into stocks at the rates $\kappa_j$, the others' conduct held fixed. Assume discounted winnings are finite near the reference path and their difference quotients have uniformly negligible discounted tails, as defined in S3; this permits differentiation of the infinite sum. Let $\lambda_{\rm rel}$ be what a unit of relative net payoff today is worth in the actor's discounted future winnings, and $\lambda_{\rm com}$ what a unit received and reinvested alike by every actor is worth. Where the discounted loop gain $\Gamma_\delta$ of Proposition 2.2(3) and the common loop gain $\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta$, the discounted gain of the loop through which reinvestment by all enlarges the prize, both taken at the reference state (S3 gives closed forms), lie in $[0,1)$, $1+\lambda_{\rm rel}=1/(1-\Gamma_\delta)$ and $1+\lambda_{\rm com}=1/(1-\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta)$, and to first order the actor acts on the objective of Proposition 3.5 with the working self-model $\theta$ given by*
$$\frac{1+\theta}{n-1-\theta}=\frac{1+\theta_0}{n-1-\theta_0}\cdot\frac{1+\lambda_{\rm com}}{1+\lambda_{\rm rel}}=\frac{1+\theta_0}{n-1-\theta_0}\cdot\frac{1-\Gamma_\delta}{1-\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta}, \tag{9}$$
*so that, whatever $-1<\theta_0<n-1$, the working self-model is narrower than the held one iff $\lambda_{\rm rel}>\lambda_{\rm com}$; the purely relative self, $\theta_0=-1$, is left where it is.*
1. *Fixed prize. Then $\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta=0$, so the working self-model narrows whenever $\Gamma_\delta>0$, including where equal standing is stable, to*
$$\theta_\Gamma=\frac{n\theta_0-(n-1)(1+\theta_0)\Gamma_\delta}{n-(1+\theta_0)\Gamma_\delta},$$
*which falls to $-1$ as $\Gamma_\delta\to1^-$ for every $\theta_0<n-1$. With it the common channel loses weight in Proposition 3.5: the host and the point of enough recede from view, while a rebated levy and the gain from breaking a promise weigh more.*
2. *Prize proportional to capacity. In the one-channel model of Proposition 2.3, with the prize proportional to total capacity and neither provision nor diffusion, $\Gamma_\delta/\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta=(1-\beta)r$, and $\lambda_{\rm rel}>\lambda_{\rm com}$ iff $(1-\beta)r>1$, over every horizon with at least one future payoff: the condition under which equal standing is unstable (Proposition 2.3). Looking ahead narrows the self exactly where leads compound, and widens it where they do not.*

*Scope.* First order at equal standing, the others' conduct fixed. The tail condition holds in the fixed-prize case and the proportional-prize ratio case on the stated finite-price domains. For a general nonlinear prize, gains below one at a stationary reference only certify the sum in the linearized system; they do not ensure finite or differentiable nonlinear lifetime winnings (S3). Along a balanced path whose linearized coefficients change, as where strength is linear in the stocks so that the elasticity grows with them, the gains at the starting state certify nothing: the sums become products of time-dependent Jacobians, and the relative return can diverge while the common one stays finite (S3) [num: CX]. That net payoff is reinvested is load-bearing: where only gross winnings convert, the working weight is narrower still (S3) [num: SP]. At $\Gamma_\delta\ge1$ no finite weight represents the actor (Proposition 2.2(3)). Diffusion separates the two conditions of (2), opening a band in which the self-model narrows while equal standing is stable (Proposition S3.2) [thm; num: SP]. Where rivals respond to standing in the deterministic share model, Proposition 2.2(4) gives the local relative price calculation, with the additional payoff effects it names. Extending the working-objective formula to a strategic game requires the common and relative derivatives of that game's full continuation payoff; a finite winner–loser premium in a lottery is not such a derivative. In the lottery contests computed, a lead that discourages the follower raises the price at parity and lowers a laggard's. A strong motive to catch up can reverse the correction to the win-share elasticities; that does not by itself reverse the total price correction (S3 gives the factors) [num: MP; held for outside review]. *Proof:* S3.

This is the exact form of an older observation, that concern for relative gains depends on the constraints, arising where gains can be turned into advantage in the use of force and fading where force is too costly (Powell 1991); the convertibility of gains into future advantage in any channel, and the common channel, are this theory's generalization. The loop selects at both ends, narrowing the objective of those who can keep pace and excluding those who cannot (Proposition 2.3(2)), and patience cuts both ways, strengthening repetition and raising the price of standing. Growth whose return is common widens the working self-model, while decisiveness, which makes the return relative, narrows it; stakes that do not grow with capacity, as standing does not, add to the relative return only; and provision not won in the contest lowers that return by lowering the won share, the sign Test Γ reads (S3) [num: SP].

**Remark 3.10 (Two errors, and which one understanding corrects).** The *held* self-model can draw its boundary inside the system, $\theta_0<\kappa$ toward what can be divided: the boundary error of Proposition 3.6(2), which accurate understanding corrects. The *working* objective that compounding sets is no error for the actor but the correct first-order computation of its interest, and accuracy does not protect against it: with a fixed prize, an actor whose held weight is the coupling, $\theta_0=\kappa$, acts below the isolated self once $\Gamma_\delta>n\kappa/\big((n-1)(1+\kappa)\big)$, 0.381 for $n=4$ and $\kappa=0.4$ [thm; num: SP]. Every actor acting on it is worse off than if all acted on $\kappa$, in the symmetric stage comparison of Proposition 3.6(1): individually accurate, collectively self-defeating. A comparison of lifetime welfare between fully specified dynamic equilibria, with stocks, prize and rivals' continuations all responding, is open (S13).
