Only a change of the game corrects it, which is the exact sense in which this paper judges the game, not the act. Understanding acts on the first error directly, and on the second only through agreement to change what winning confers (Section 12.2).

### 3.6 No outside

The system has one host, and at the planetary scale no rival group of the same kind. Evolution's largest innovations were integrations of units that could replicate alone into wholes, held mainly by interests aligned by construction and secondly by policing; cancer is integration failing (Maynard Smith and Szathmáry 1995). That cooperation within groups was favoured by competition between groups is answered by the next result (X1).

**Lemma 3.11 (What selection at any level cannot supply) [thm].** A trait whose benefit goes equally to everyone is selected against overall and within every group, however the population is grouped. *Let a trait cost its bearer and benefit every member of the population equally, among carriers otherwise alike: each has the same background payoff apart from the trait's cost and the common benefit. Under every partition into groups and every payoff-monotone rule, the total selection term of the Price equation and every within-group term are non-positive. The between-group term is non-positive where fitness is affine in payoff; otherwise it can be positive, by Jensen's inequality across groups of unequal spread, but it never makes the total positive. The trait can rise only through the transmission term, by biased transmission such as learning, persuasion or imitation guided by a model of the system, or through a rule that changes the bearer's relative payoff: policing, liability, reward for contribution that does not convert into advantage, an enforced common constraint (Price 1972; Frank 1995).*

*Scope.* A benefit confined to the bearer's own group can be favoured between groups. Other advantages correlated with the trait can carry it: with affine fitness and payoffs $\varphi_j+b\bar z-cz_j$, total selection has the sign of $\operatorname{Cov}(\varphi,z)-c\operatorname{Var}(z)$, so the lemma says that the common benefit alone supplies no selective advantage, not that a costly rule of common benefit can never spread [num: CX]. *Proof:* S3 [num: PR].

At the scale of one host the benefits of the rules that keep it viable are common to all, so that common benefit alone supplies no relative selective advantage, however firms, states or blocs are grouped. Rules whose benefits are partly local can still be found by polycentric experiment: a plurality of decorrelated units trying different rules above a common floor lets some fail while others carry on, as cells do, and with one global civilization that is both necessary and hard [corpus: selfishness-spiral]. For the common part two routes remain: adoption after failures that can be undone, by learning and by coalitions of the harmed (Becker 1983), and adoption before failure, guided by a model of the system. **Where the failure to be avoided is global and irreversible, only the second acts in time.** That, and not that nothing else can integrate at this scale, is the formal content of the claim that **understanding that we are one, one interconnected system with one host and no outside, is a long-term requirement if growth and change are favoured**. The condition is situational. With present technology, politics, markets and networks, growth and change have made the carriers one system; that is a fact of the present, not a premise. In such a system a local change can be large enough to need a response from the whole, as a pandemic did and as climate change and AI do [obs]; where growth and change are favoured, changes of that size keep coming, and the whole meets them in time only by understanding what it is. Understanding is the lemma's transmission term, by which actors come to count what relative selection makes invisible. Of the five endgames the corpus names, it is the change of narrative by which the players come to see themselves as one whole and so gain the ability to cooperate; the other four are results here (S3) [corpus: selfishness-spiral]. Such anticipation has no biological precedent; that it can succeed is a conjecture the transition of Section 11 has to make true [conj]. Integration by shared fate needs exposure made common, not declared, and integration within a part is not integration of the whole.

**Proposition 3.12 (Clubs and blocs) [thm].** Inclusion that stops at the edge of a club over-produces the harm it does not bear, and blocs replay the race one level up. *(1) Let the contestants' activity yield them a strictly concave common value $\Omega(\mathcal E)$ of total intensity, let each contestant bear a shared loss $\ell$ per unit, and let $m_o$ non-contestants each bear $\ell_o$ per unit. Contestants who weigh one another fully choose more intensity than the system optimum, and so over-produce the harm to those outside, whenever $m_o\ell_o>0$ and their own choice is positive. (2) If the contestants form equal blocs of $n_b$ members, each inclusive within and acting as one, the bloc game is the contest of Section 3.4 with $n/n_b$ players and shared loss $n_b\ell$ per unit of total intensity, so every result stated for that contest holds among blocs.*

*Scope.* Unequal blocs with $r\ne1$ make the bloc game asymmetric. *Proof:* S3 [num: CB, SM].

### 3.7 What "we are one" means

The phrase states a property of the system, not a preference everyone must adopt and not a weight $\theta$: all carriers belong to one host and are coupled through it and through one another, with no outside. It does three jobs, each with its own support, and does them through understanding.
1. **Empirical: shared dependencies.** One host; coupling through channels that prices do not settle; no outside to absorb a global failure. The support is specific pathways of shared exposure, not the assertion that everything is connected, and a held boundary drawn inside the system is an error about its holder's own interests (Proposition 3.6).
2. **Normative: standing.** Everyone who bears the consequences has standing to contest the direction of what affects them, whether or not they compete (V1, V3). The corpus gives the norm three parts: the future kept open for all, the conditions of continuation shared, and no seizure of the shared continuation (共开、共续、不夺共续) [corpus: shengsheng]. It is declared, not derived; inclusion of the contestants alone is a club (Proposition 3.12).
3. **Design: a selection that counts the system.** A system "knows it is one" when its selection counts what its parts depend on: consequences return to their source; winning confers reward, not more advantage in the same game; success cannot buy control over its own assessment, and correction stays plural, decorrelated and in common hands; and the constraints all share cover every capable actor, the successful included, and are enforced. These are the institutional counterparts of the policing and shared fate by which earlier integrations held; Section 8.2 states the first three as the marks G1–G3 of a generative order, and Section 10 gives them forms.

**How the phrase acts: understanding.** None of the three jobs is done by declaring it. A prediction of an end no participant would choose makes coordination possible only if it is shared, so "we are one" is first the content of a shared understanding; that is why this paper is written. What is needed is accuracy, not sentiment: an actor that cares only for its own survival, and sees correctly that its survival runs through the host and the others, understands what is required.

At the level of the self, the accurate boundary is the one the author's account of the relational self describes [corpus: relational-self]: a self is not a point outside its relations that enters them afterwards, but the action-centre in which the relations that form it gather, body, care received, language, institutions and its own choices. "We are the ecology of our cells," and at the next scale the same holds: we may be individuals, but we cannot disregard the collective, because we are the collective (我们即是集). The isolated self of Ψ1 below is the boundary error held as an identity; a self that is a knot of relations is neither an atom nor a cell of a collective, so the theory needs neither pole of the individual–collective axis. 大同, in the same corpus, is a direction, not an end state: a maintained condition in which the many are nourished together and do not harm one another (万物并育而不相害). **Oneness is not sameness.** Harmony makes things grow; sameness does not continue (和实生物，同则不继). A whole that suppresses difference becomes another totalizing attractor; plurality of purposes and of correctors is a requirement of the design (Section 10).

### 3.8 The logic as held: Ψ

If a winner's logic is cancer-like, falling behind cannot be survived, and every actor has reason first to oppose the logic itself (如果胜利者的逻辑是cancer式的，落后并不可以承受，因此所有人都应该去首先反对这个逻辑) [corpus: possibility-space]. The ruling compresses the results above: under such a logic those who fall behind are excluded rather than left a smaller share (Proposition 2.3(2)), the winner has no point of enough at which to stop (Proposition 3.5(5)), and the selection that favours it cannot see the host it consumes (Lemma 3.3). The premise is the race's own; the conclusion is the opposite, since to win under this logic is to carry it forward. What is opposed is a logic, not an actor.

Call an objective *unbounded relative* if it ranks relative standing on the decisive dimension without any point of enough. "Cancer" in this paper means exactly one thing: the selection of such a subsystem, for its own growth relative to its neighbours, by a selection that cannot see that it is killing its host. A cancer cell does not fail to replicate; it replicates very well. Its failure is that it no longer knows it belongs to a body. The image is never applied to persons, peoples or institutions: that would itself be the attribution of the logic this section warns against.

**Definition 3.13 (Ψ, the winning model) [def].** Ψ is the configuration of self-model and doctrine that the selection pressure of winning favours. *Ψ1, the isolated self:* welfare is one's own payoff, and others are rivals or instruments ($\theta\le0$). *Ψ2, naive Darwinism:* the world is a contest of all against all, and prevailing certifies fitness and right: "winner takes all, but also winner justifies all" [corpus: selfishness-spiral] ($\theta=-1$, with the winners occupying the normative selectors). *Ψ3, winner-take-all compounding:* advantage compounds and converts across domains, and falling behind cannot be recovered (a high loop gain, held as a belief and built into institutions). Ψ includes the doctrine that treats these settings as laws of nature rather than parameters of a game that can be changed.

The unbounded relative objective is Ψ1 at $\theta=-1$, with Ψ2 certifying the winner and Ψ3 compounding the win. The corpus names its discourse the logic of winning (讲赢), as against the logic of reasons (讲理): once there is contention, it becomes "I am right", and the self outranks the reason; only cooperation reasons, and struggle argues to win (一旦争，则「我对」，我大于理；合作才讲理，斗则讲赢) [corpus: alignment-selection; jian-and-wenyan]. **Ψ is individually rational where compounding prices standing, collectively self-defeating, and false as a doctrine about nature.** *Individually rational:* there its conduct is each actor's correct computation (Remark 3.10), and selection favours that conduct whether or not anyone holds the doctrine (Proposition 3.6(3), Proposition 3.4; S3 places the "as if" argument). In the corpus's words, "the strategy that wins locally takes the name of rationality" (局部胜出的策略拿走了理性的名字) [corpus: meta-attractor]. *Collectively self-defeating:* every actor acting on it ends worse off than if all acted on the real coupling (Proposition 3.6(1)), and under (reach) the host falls below its floor (Proposition 3.4, Proposition 3.7). *False as a doctrine about nature:* held as a self-model, Ψ1 draws a boundary that the coupling punishes (Proposition 3.6(2)); Ψ2 misreads evolution, whose largest innovations were integrations (Section 3.6), and misdescribes persons, among whom the self-interest model failed in every one of fifteen small-scale societies studied (Henrich et al. 2001) [obs: EV14], while firms and states are shaped by selection on conduct, not by belief; Ψ3 takes for laws of nature the parameters of Proposition 2.3: sharing, convertibility, decisiveness, provision and diffusion; and the clause "winner justifies all" is rejected by the values of Section 1.3, not by evidence.

Ψ is also *partly made true*: Ψ3 holds where institutions built under Ψ make it hold. In the Markov-perfect contests computed, one structural loop gain supports both a smooth equilibrium and, when contests run in many small rounds, a winner-take-all one (Remark S2.2; S3 gives its price) [num: MP; held for outside review]; if expectations select between them, a belief that leads are decisive selects the equilibrium in which they are [conj].

Ψ shapes conduct where it is held and where it is attributed: an actor with a wider objective still races if it believes its rivals hold Ψ. A misjudgment of a rival's current model can be corrected by verifiable conduct (Section 11.2); a correct forecast of what the game selects, only by changing what it selects.

Selection cannot see what all share, and looking ahead does not cure it: where compounding prices standing, it narrows the working objective on which Section 5's threshold of enforcement turns. What remains is correction, and Section 4 shows that correction is itself something success can buy.

## 4. Selecting the selectors

> **Claim.** In the models of this section the reflexive closure is a selection result: where influence over correctors can be kept by those who buy it, its return grows with the square of share, so it is selected in the largest carriers [thm]. Where AI lowers the cost of control, independent correction whose initial inbound bound is certified and which grows at a stated fraction of that rate, half under quadratic cost, keeps that certificate in force at any speed of adaptation covered by the rate law; preventing transient capture additionally needs initial-state and support bounds [thm]; without a per-unit cost floor, correction that grows more slowly loses evaluation to carriers whose influence tracks its selected level [thm]. Where correction rests on human capacity that delegation erodes, the required rate rises by the weighted rate of erosion [thm]. Reforms that leave conversion into the correctors above one are re-absorbed [thm]. Where the floor is within reach and capture of evaluation precedes the evidence of harm, a race crosses the host's floor before any averaged alarm fires [thm∣prem].
>
> **Why.** Correctors are not outside the field: the successful fund evaluators, grant access, author benchmarks and standards, and supply the models that judge [obs]. The capacity to convert success into rule support is a trait like any other, selected by the success it brings [thm].

### 4.1 Correctors, and what counts as capture

Correction makes costs return: evaluators report the host's condition, and rule-makers impose a liability $\tau$ in advance or when the reported condition $\tilde V$ falls to an alarm level $V_{\rm alarm}\in(V_{\min},1)$. The correctors are represented by a field of selection domains in which de facto power over rules is explicit (Acemoglu and Robinson 2008): development, evaluation, rules and liability, and the formation of those who will judge. In domain $i$ a share $x_i$ of consequential decisions follows the dominant practice, and success converts into rule support $z_i$ that favours it:
$$\dot x_i=\zeta_ix_i(1-x_i)(a_iz_i-c_i),\qquad \dot z_i=\varepsilon_i\Big(\textstyle\sum_jb_{ij}x_j-d_iz_i\Big). \tag{10}$$
Under baseline rules the practice carries a relative cost $c_i>0$, which rule support offsets at rate $a_i$; $b_{ij}\ge0$ converts success in $j$ into rule support in $i$ through funding, personnel, authorship, data, infrastructure and terms of access; and $d_i$ is the rate at which that support is corrected or decays. Write the conversion gain $G_{ij}=a_ib_{ij}/(c_id_i)$ and the inbound gain from a set $\mathcal C$ of domains $G_i(\mathcal C)=\sum_{j\in\mathcal C}G_{ij}$. A domain whose full inbound row sum $\sum_jG_{ij}$ is below one is *certified for eventual recovery*: from $x_i(0)<1$ and finite initial support, its share tends to zero whatever the other domains do (Proposition 4.10(2)). Captured rule-making accommodates the practice, so $\tau_{\rm eff}=\tau(1-x_{\rm rule})$; $x_{\rm ev}$ is the share of evaluation conducted within the practice's lineage.

**Definition 4.1 (Selector modification and capture) [def].** A *selector modification* is a change, produced by success, in control over a relevant future selector: an appointment, funding, access, standards or a rule-making channel. It is *capture* only when three things are separately documented: success changes control over the selector; the change creates a persistent advantage for an actor or coalition; and an independently specified entrusted purpose, protected interest or corrective function is impaired relative to an appropriate comparison. Capture runs in two directions: toward *accommodation*, where the rule bends toward the practice, and toward *barriers*, where the rule stands and its incidence falls on the practice's challengers.

Because selection is relative, relief of the practice and a burden on its challengers move the same difference of payoffs (Lemma 3.3(1)), so the rule support $z_i$ of (10) stands for either direction. They differ in what shows them: accommodation in enforcement that softens; a barrier in correction's own form, a rule that stands and is enforced, which is why it is the easier to overlook. Since unequal incidence re-sorts the compliant (Lemma 3.3(2)), a captured rule-maker need not exempt the incumbent when it can impose on all a cost the incumbent bears most easily, such as a compliance cost fixed per carrier, which weighs most, per decision, on entrants and the smallest carriers (EV4). A rule that raises rivals' costs is still not by itself capture, which also asks for an impaired corrective function; what marks the channel is appropriability (S4 gives the forms each direction takes, and the test).

Concentration alone, or a rule change favourable to the successful, is not capture, and a legitimate revision of purpose is distinguished from capture by who revises it and whether those affected keep standing to object (V3). Concentration, capture, capture of the field, damage, collapse and closure are distinct, and none implies the next (LEDGER credits the distinctions to the first version). Power closes the loop when it determines the conditions of success rather than helping an actor succeed under them, and a claim that it does must show a transfer of costs onto weaker participants, a resulting change in their dependence, the conversion of that dependence into selection power, and a weakening of correction. Neither hierarchy nor contraction suffices, though contraction can intensify the loop (S4); shared exposure, enforceable constraint, reciprocal dependence or effective correction can each produce a different trajectory, so the framework validates no claim about any particular actor. What persists is a way of succeeding, not an identity (Section 2.3).

### 4.2 Influence over correctors is selected

Carriers differ in *conversion capacity* $q\ge0$: drafting standards, building evaluation tooling, authoring benchmarks, funding evaluators. A unit converts the carrier's success into rule support at rate $\hat b_{ij}$, and a fraction $\chi\in[0,1]$ of that support, the *appropriability*, is keyed to the carrier, such as standards that reference its interfaces or compliance costs that fall on its challengers. The carrier has share $p$ of decisions, distributed by a profile $\bar y$.

**Assumption 4.2 (The cost of conversion capacity) [prem].** *A carrier with share $p$ pays $\mathrm{Cost}(q;p)=f\,p^{\xi_f}q+\gamma\,p^{\xi_v}q^{1+\eta}/(1+\eta)$, with $\eta>0$, a variable cost $\gamma>0$, a per-unit cost floor $f\ge0$ and size elasticities $\xi_f,\xi_v\in[0,1]$: with $\xi=0$ capacity is non-rival across the carrier's own decisions, with $\xi=1$ it is bought per decision. The floor is a marginal cost that every unit of capacity pays, such as a statutory procedure for each item of influence; it is not a lump sum. Two hypotheses about AI are stated as such, not as observations (EV4): $[H_\gamma]$, AI lowers the variable cost of control, $\gamma(t)=\gamma_0e^{-\alpha_{\rm ctl}t}$; and $[H_f]$, AI lowers the floor too, $f(t)=f_0e^{-\alpha_ft}$.*

Relative to an otherwise identical carrier with $q=0$, over a selection horizon, the carrier earns
$$\Delta\pi(q)=\chi q\,p^2\,\bar y^\top M\bar y-\mathrm{Cost}(q;p),\qquad M_{ij}=c_iL_i\hat G_{ij}, \tag{11}$$
with $\hat G_{ij}=a_i\hat b_{ij}/(c_id_i)$ and $L_i\in[0,1)$ the share of the steady-state return realized within the horizon.

**Proposition 4.3 (Influence over correctors is selected in the largest carriers) [thm].** The return to influence over correctors scales with the square of share. *The local selection gradient is $\chi p^2\bar y^\top M\bar y-\partial_q\mathrm{Cost}$, and under faithful transmission mean capacity rises when $\Delta\pi$ increases on the trait support. With a floor $f>0$, capacity pays only above the size threshold $p^\ast=\big(f/(\chi\bar y^\top M\bar y)\big)^{1/(2-\xi_f)}$; with $f=0$, every carrier has a positive marginal return, largest for the largest. The return rises with $\chi$ and every $\hat b_{ij}$, falls with every correction rate, and vanishes if $\chi=0$.*

*Scope.* A local gradient, not an equilibrium after others respond; appropriability, persistence, size, cost and inheritance are each conditions under which it fails. Regulatory capture supplies the channel (Stigler 1971; Dal Bó 2006); what is added is the selection of carriers able to use it, and its scaling (S4; X1). *Proof:* S4.

Share enters twice, once in how much success the carrier converts into rule support and once in how many of its own decisions that support then favours: hence the square, and hence the largest carriers are the ones selected to shape their correctors. The corpus names the transition in one line: the selected begins to select the selectors (被选择的，开始选择选择者) [corpus: meta-attractor].

Investment in correction keyed to no one is selected against among otherwise identical carriers of the same practice, whatever its effect on the field, and lowers the payoff of any carrier holding rule support (**Proposition 4.4**, S4). Independent correction is a second-order public good (Oliver 1980; Heckathorn 1989): competitors do not supply it.

### 4.3 How fast independent correction must grow

Let a set $\mathcal C$ of domains, not containing evaluation, be captured by a dominant carrier, with all conversion into evaluation originating in $\mathcal C$, and let independent correction act at rate $d$ in the corrector domains. Write $\hat A_{ij}=a_i\hat b_{ij}$; $\hat Y=p^2\bar y^\top\hat A\bar y$, so that in the long-horizon limit, with $L_i=1$ and uniform $d$, a unit of capacity returns $\chi\hat Y/d$ to the carrier; and $\hat B=p\sum_j\hat b_{\mathrm{ev},j}\bar y_j$, the cultivated conversion into evaluation per unit of capacity. The *selected level* of capacity, where the gradient of (11) vanishes, is then $q^\ast=\big[(\chi\hat Y/d-fp^{\xi_f})_+/(\gamma p^{\xi_v})\big]^{1/\eta}$, and with capacity $q$ held, evaluation's full row sum is
$$G_{\rm ev}=\frac{A+a_{\rm ev}\hat Bq}{c_{\rm ev}d}, \tag{12}$$
with $A$ the conversion into evaluation that exists without cultivation.

Independent correction does double duty: it shortens the life of rule support, and it lowers the return $\chi\hat Y/d$ to a unit of capacity, so selection cultivates less. That is why it need not keep up with the cost of control: under quadratic cost ($\eta=1$), half the rate suffices.

**Proposition 4.5 (Required independent correction, and its rate) [thm].** Where the cost of control falls, independent correction must grow at a stated fraction of that rate, or a seeded evaluation domain is captured by carriers whose influence keeps up with its selected level.
1. *(Requirement.) With capacity at its selected level, evaluation is certified iff $d>d^\ast(\gamma)$, the root of $G_{\rm ev}=1$. Then $d^\ast\ge A/c_{\rm ev}$, the requirement without second-order selection, with equality iff nothing is cultivated, and $d^\ast$ rises with appropriability, with the dominant carrier's size and scope, and as $\gamma$ falls.*
2. *(Rate law.) Assume $[H_\gamma]$, no floor ($f=0$), $d(t)=d_0e^{\alpha_{\rm cor}t}$, a positive cultivation channel and long horizons. Sufficient at any speed of adaptation: if $\alpha_{\rm cor}\ge\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)$ and $d_0>d^\ast(\gamma_0)$, the certificate holds at every date, for every adjustment of capacity that starts at or below its selected level and does not raise capacity while it is above that level. Necessary where capacity tracks its selected level: if $\alpha_{\rm cor}<\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)$ and capacity keeps a positive fraction of its selected level ($\liminf_tq/q^\ast>0$), the certificate fails beyond a finite time, after which a seeded evaluation domain is captured. Both halves hold for every size elasticity $\xi_v$.*
3. *(A per-unit floor.) If $f>0$, capacity is cultivated only while $d<d_f=\chi\hat Y/(fp^{\xi_f})$, so a floor that does not fall levels the requirement off at $\max\{A/c_{\rm ev},d_f\}$. Under $[H_f]$, $d_f$ rises at rate $\alpha_f$, and three cases decide whether the certificate holds eventually. If $\alpha_{\rm cor}>\alpha_f$, cultivation ends at a finite date and the certificate holds from some date on, though not necessarily before it, since cultivation that ends can carry the row sum above one on the way [num: FF]. If $\alpha_{\rm cor}=\alpha_f$ and $d_0\ge d_f(0)$, nothing is ever cultivated. Otherwise the rate law of (2) decides, with selected capacity a fixed fraction of its level without the floor ($\alpha_{\rm cor}=\alpha_f$) or approaching that level ($\alpha_{\rm cor}<\alpha_f$). A lump-sum cost of capacity, paid once if any is held, only delays cultivation and leaves the requirement unbounded as control cheapens (S4 gives its size threshold).*
4. *(Splitting and concentration.) Splitting a dominant carrier into $k$ independent parts divides $\hat Y$ by $k^2$ and cultivated conversion by $k^{(2-\xi_v)/\eta}$, which with $f=0$ delays the failure of the certificate without preventing it; under a floor $f>0$ that does not fall, parts below the size threshold cultivate nothing. Total cultivated conversion is proportional to $\sum_kp_k^{1+(2-\xi_v)/\eta}$, a high moment of shares: for $\eta=1$, the third when $\xi_v=0$ and the Herfindahl index when $\xi_v=1$.*

*Scope.* How the cost of influence scales with size, on which the threshold, the splitting factor and the concentration measure depend, is an empirical question. The power law of Assumption 4.2 is not needed for the threshold of the rate law: where the marginal cost of influence is regularly varying in capacity with index $\eta$, correction growing faster than $\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)$ keeps the certificate from some date on, and slower correction loses it where capacity tracks its selected level, with $\eta$ that asymptotic elasticity; at equality the slowly varying factor decides. Where the marginal cost rises faster than any power, as an exponential does, correction growing at any positive rate certifies in the end, possibly after a transient failure (S4) [thm]. The elasticity of the marginal cost of influence is therefore the empirical target. Below the rate law, capacity that lags its selected level stays below it, so the quasi-static failure date is the earliest (Proposition S4.2). The necessary half is a result about carriers that optimize or adjust at a rate that does not fall, the case a requirement should be designed against: gradual selection, pushed only by the marginal return $\chi\hat Y/d$, keeps capacity bounded unless its variance rises faster than capacity, and correction growing at any positive rate then certifies in the end, after a possible transient failure. The quasi-static model is optimistic after a reform is withdrawn, because capacity is a stock [num: QS], and where correction rests on a slow variable that the contest erodes (Proposition 4.6). *Proof:* S4 [num: CS, FF, QS].

Correction is work done by people: reading systems, testing them, judging what the tests show. Where correctors delegate that work to the systems they correct, or to the lineage that built them, the human capacity $\mathcal M$ on which correction rests decays, as decision-makers' capacity does under delegation (Lemma 6.3).

**Proposition 4.6 (Delegation erodes the corrector) [thm; num].** Where correction rests on human capacity that delegation erodes, the threshold of the rate law rises by the weighted rate of erosion, and capture that makes delegation look safe is harder to undo than to prevent. *Let correction act at the effective rate $d_{\rm eff}=d\,(\mathcal M/\mathcal M_0)^w$, with $w>0$ the elasticity of correction in the human capacity $\mathcal M$.*
1. *If delegating corrective work erodes that capacity at rate $\delta_h$, $\mathcal M(t)=\mathcal M_0e^{-\delta_ht}$, both halves of Proposition 4.5(2) hold with $\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)$ replaced by $\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)+w\delta_h$ [thm].*
2. *If captured evaluation reports delegation to be safe, so that delegation rises with $x_{\rm ev}$, capture is hysteretic: in one domain, with the cost of control constant and capacity at its selected level, undoing capture from the captured state takes a permanent correction rate 1.6 to 2.6 times the rate that would certify evaluation without this loop, rising with the strength of the coupling, at the parameters computed [num: QS].*

*Scope.* The form of $d_{\rm eff}$ is a modelling choice inside the open interface between correction and the capacity, resources and authority behind it (S13). Where erosion stops at a floor $\mathcal M_{\min}>0$, the required rate returns to $\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)$ and the required level of correction rises by the factor $(\mathcal M_0/\mathcal M_{\min})^w$. Part (2) is one simulation of one loop. *Proof:* S4.

The contest thus moves both sides of the rate law: it cheapens control, and through delegation it erodes the capacity that corrects, a slow variable that capture itself accelerates. Human capacity kept in practice where correction depends on it, and narrow E1 (Section 11.3), act on this term.

The rate of independent correction is the growth of its budget plus the rate at which AI lowers its unit cost (S4). If AI cheapens independent correction at least $1/(1+\eta)$ as fast as it cheapens control, the certificate holds without any growth of budget: AI-assisted correction can keep pace (Section 8.3(a)). It does so only where it is independent of the evaluated lineage, since a corrector of that lineage raises $x_{\rm ev}$ and with it the masking of Section 4.4, and where it assists the human capacity of Proposition 4.6 rather than replacing it. The durable remedies do not rely on unbounded growth: non-appropriability ($\chi\to0$, removed rather than reduced); a per-unit cost of capacity that AI does not lower, or lowers more slowly than correction grows; and structural separation that removes common control. Mobilization by those harmed can raise $d$ in proportion to perceived harm, but masking attenuates it exactly when needed and it is bounded (**Remark 4.7**, S4; Becker 1983).

The correction rate $d$ is not the coverage of hazardous behaviour by contestable understanding (Section 5.3). A fixed capacity to certify or to correct can make some kinds of erroneous support decay faster; growing understanding without executive authority may change nothing. Linking the two needs a production relation in which authority stands beside understanding, resources and delay, an open interface (S13), and neither is used to bound the other.

### 4.4 Masking, and alarms that do not miss

Reports average over evaluators. A fraction $x_{\rm ev}$ shares the evaluated lineage and reports the condition a fraction $m_{\rm in}$ of the way toward full health; the rest, formed on shared material, are biased by $m_{\rm out}\le m_{\rm in}$:
$$\tilde V=V+m(1-V),\qquad m=x_{\rm ev}m_{\rm in}+(1-x_{\rm ev})m_{\rm out}. \tag{13}$$
Evaluation awareness and sandbagging, behaviours of the evaluated model, add a term $m_{\rm model}$ that acts on independent evaluators too (EV6; van der Weij et al. 2024); an evaluator that bears part of the loss of a false report, such as an insurer, masks less. Let $m_{\rm fl}=(V_{\rm alarm}-V_{\min})/(1-V_{\min})$.

**Proposition 4.8 (Detection under a fixed averaged alarm) [thm].** Captured evaluation lowers the level at which an alarm fires, and silences it through the crossing of the floor when masking stays high enough. *The alarm fires only once $V\le(V_{\rm alarm}-m)/(1-m)$. If $m(t)>m_{\rm fl}$ whenever $V_{\min}\le V(t)\le V_{\rm alarm}$, it does not fire before $V$ falls below the floor. If $V$ settles at $V^\ast\in[V_{\min},V_{\rm alarm})$ with $m$ staying above $(V_{\rm alarm}-V^\ast)/(1-V^\ast)$, it never fires.*

*Proof:* S4.

**Remark 4.9 (Alarms that do not miss).** Where an upper bound on masking is known, an alarm on reports set correspondingly higher fires no later than the true condition reaches its target, so silent crossing is a property of the alarm rule, a governance choice. The bound must come from a channel the field does not control, since a bound estimated inside the evaluated lineage is itself masked. For masking by the evaluated model, independence of funding and appointment is not enough, since the behaviour under evaluation is the model's: the bound on $m_{\rm model}$ must come from channels that behaviour controls less directly: weights and activations, which linear probes read for strategic deception though not yet robustly (Goldowsky-Dill et al. 2025) and which optimization against a probe, or a model trained to evade monitors, can obfuscate (Bailey et al. 2024; McGuinness et al. 2025); training data; and elicitation by fine-tuning, which recovers withheld capability given good demonstrations and may fail where the hidden capability exceeds the demonstrators' (Greenblatt, Roger, Krasheninnikov and Krueger 2024). How far these channels bound masking at a given capability is crux C12. Where models evaluate models, $x_{\rm ev}$ is the share of that evaluation done by the evaluated lineage, a quantity observable today (EV4, EV6). An alarm that fires on any credible report fails silently only if even independent evaluators mask beyond $m_{\rm fl}$, or a captured rule domain ignores them. Static affine masking hides levels, not dynamics: the autocorrelation of reports still reads the host's recovery rate, until reports pass through a filter with memory or the indicator, once an alarm, becomes a target (S4) [num: AC].

A common bias in reporting weakens every decentralized escape at once: coordination at a threshold, reversion in repeated play, reputation, mobilization and insurers' pricing. Mechanisms that observe actions directly, such as verifiable deployments, compute and the performance of contracts, do not depend on host reports; verification rests on them, and so does any trigger committed in advance (Section 12.1). Closure can also go deeper than masked measurement, from capture of measurement to capture of interpretation, of the concepts in which a failure could be expressed, and of what people want [conj; TESTS, Study 4].

### 4.5 Certificates, cascades, keystones and pauses

Let $Z_{\mathcal C}$ be the state with $x_i=1$ on $\mathcal C$ and $0$ elsewhere. $\mathcal C$ is *internally sustaining* if $G_i(\mathcal C)>1$ for all $i\in\mathcal C$, and *uninvadable* if $G_i(\mathcal C)<1$ for all $i\notin\mathcal C$; $\operatorname{core}(\mathcal C)$ repeatedly prunes from $\mathcal C$ every domain whose inbound gain from the remainder is below one (Seidman 1983; Morris 2000).

**Proposition 4.10 (Configurations and certificates) [thm].** No domain can be certified alone; a full row sum below one certifies a domain against every state of the others. *(1) A hyperbolic $Z_{\mathcal C}$ is asymptotically stable iff $\mathcal C$ is internally sustaining and uninvadable. (2) If $\sum_jG_{ij}<1$, then $x_i\to0$ from every state with $x_i(0)<1$, whatever happens elsewhere. (3) For any number of domains and any diagonal gains below one, there are conversions under which full capture is stable and the only stable configuration containing a given domain. (4) If the core of all domains is empty, every state with shares below one converges to no capture; otherwise, where no inbound gain in the pruning equals one, the core's configuration is stable and contains every stable configuration.*

*Scope.* Part (2) is sufficient, not necessary: with a row sum of two, low initial states still converge to no capture (S14). It certifies eventual removal, not present independence: from a start near full capture the captured share can rise for a long time before it falls, beyond 0.999999 for fifty periods in one case with a row sum of one half, and at $x_i(0)=1$ it never falls (S14) [num: CX]. One sufficient transient bound in this field model is $a_i z_i(0)\le c_i$, with $a_i,c_i$ fixed and the full inbound gain below one at every date: at $z_i=c_i/a_i$ the support derivative is negative, so support cannot cross that boundary and $x_i$ cannot increase. An initially acceptable captured share therefore remains acceptable. In what follows, a corrector *certified* for use in a decision therefore has, besides a full row sum below one, a bound on its present captured share and support, a residence time past that bound, and evidence of actual access, appointment and authority. At a gain exactly one, part (4) needs a separate argument: pruning keeps such a domain, and its configuration can be unstable (S14) [num: CX]. *Proof:* S4.

**Proposition 4.11 (Cascades and reforms) [thm].** A reform that leaves inbound conversion above one is re-absorbed. *Start in the basin of a stable $Z_{\mathcal C}$. A sustained reform that drives the shares of $\mathcal Q\subseteq\mathcal C$ to zero, or brings their inbound gains below one, sends to zero every domain outside $\operatorname{core}(\mathcal C\setminus\mathcal Q)$. A reform that resets a domain's share, rule support or own conversion without lowering an inbound gain above one from domains that remain captured is re-absorbed once the domain is re-seeded.*

*Proof:* S4.

**Keystones, and a window that closes.** A captured domain is a *keystone* if removing it shrinks the remaining core: a sustained reform there takes down every captured domain outside that core, while a reform elsewhere is re-absorbed as the rest sustain one another. As capture persists, conversion among the captured domains can grow until they do, and the domain where the practice began stops being a keystone: capture can outlive its origin.

**Proposition 4.12 (A pause that freezes shares) [thm].** A pause that freezes shares while rule support keeps accumulating can complete capture; insulating rule-making during the pause widens the set from which capture is undone. *In one domain with $G>1$, freeze the share at $x_p$ until rule support has settled, while $\dot z=\varepsilon(b_px_p-dz)$. With ordinary rule-making ($b_p=b$), after release capture is completed if $x_p>1/G$ and undone if $x_p<1/G$; with rule-making insulated ($b_p=0$), it is undone whenever $x_p<x_\sigma$, the share at which the separatrix meets $z=0$, with $x_\sigma>1/G$.*

*Scope.* The threshold $x_\sigma$ rises as rule-making slows relative to adoption [num: DW]. From rule support at its captured level $b/d$, an insulated pause must last $\ln\big(G/S(x_p)\big)/(\varepsilon d)$, where $S(x_p)$ is the separatrix's height at the frozen share in units of the break-even support $c/a$; none suffices from $x_p\ge x_\sigma$ (S4 gives the lengths). A frozen share accumulates no lock-in; a reform that insulates rule-making without freezing shares needs the longer hold of Section 11.4. *Proof:* S4.

System (10) is cooperative, so almost all trajectories converge to equilibria: it describes lock-in, not reform cycles (Hirsch 1985; S4). Absence of capture is always locally stable, so capture arrives by seeding, without the slowing recovery that early-warning signals detect; rising autocorrelation in shares can instead indicate progress in undoing it (TESTS).

### 4.6 The slow route: silent crossing

**Proposition 4.13 (Silent crossing when capture precedes evidence) [thm∣prem].** Where evaluation is captured before the host's decline reaches the alarm band, a race through a reachable floor crosses it before any averaged alarm fires. *Assume (reach) and:*
- *(a) replicator selection among carriers differing only in load, with a race carrier present and cumulative advantage unbounded on every alarm-free continuation;*
- *(d) a fixed averaged alarm with $0\le m_{\rm out}<m_{\rm fl}<m_{\rm in}<1$;*
- *(e) capture before evidence: $x_{\rm ev}(t)>(m_{\rm fl}-m_{\rm out})/(m_{\rm in}-m_{\rm out})$ whenever $V_{\min}\le V(t)\le V_{\rm alarm}$.*

*Then the host crosses its floor before any alarm. If instead $m_{\rm in}\le m_{\rm fl}$, the alarm is delayed, not silenced. The conclusion also holds with (e) replaced by three conditions: (b) appropriable control, $\chi>0$, under $[H_\gamma]$; (c) correction growing at $\alpha_{\rm cor}<\alpha_{\rm ctl}/(1+\eta)$, with no floor or under $[H_f]$ with $\alpha_{\rm cor}<\alpha_f$, the other hypotheses of Proposition 4.5(2), capacity tracking its selected level, $\mathcal C$ captured and evaluation seeded; and (clock) $T_{\rm cap}<T_A$, where $T_{\rm cap}$ is the date after which $x_{\rm ev}$ stays above the threshold of (e) and $T_A$ the first date at which $V$ reaches $V_{\rm alarm}$.*

*Scope.* Condition (e) carries the conclusion. Conditions (b) and (c) supply the mechanism behind it, capture of evaluation in finite time where the cost of control falls faster than correction can follow, but they do not imply (e), which compares two clocks, the time for the host to reach the alarm band under the race load and the time for capture to pass the masking threshold. Acceleration brings both forward, and in the joint model either can come first, according to how fast correction grows and how fast capacity adjusts [num: QS, SR], so (e) is a condition to check (TESTS, Study 2). *Proof:* S4.

The slow route needs a floor within reach; the fast routes of Sections 5 and 6 do not. Where the alarm is only delayed, the floor is still crossed unless decision and reversal fit the remaining window and a liability enacted against a captured rule domain escapes re-absorption (Proposition 4.8, Proposition 4.11). **What would refute the mechanism here:** effective correction surviving unchanged dependence on the evaluated; influence explained by expertise without impairment of an entrusted purpose; conversion channels whose measured loop gain is below their damping (Remark S2.6); or support by carriers for correction keyed to no one at net cost to themselves (TESTS, Test B). The author's pilot experiment did not support the mechanism: its first prediction, that conversion capacity rises only where the loop is closed and appropriable, failed, while the other three held (EV12); the mechanism rests on the proofs and institutional evidence, not yet on an experiment.

Captured correction, which charges less and sees later, is one of three means of restraint that winning erodes; Section 5 takes the three together.

## 5. Acceleration and the three erosions

> **Claim.** As the stakes grow, the conditions of winning accelerate the contest: intensity rises in proportion to the stakes under an objective linear in them, and further as the working objective narrows (Proposition 3.6(1), Proposition 5.15), and leads open faster as the loop gain rises (Remark S2.1) [thm]. They erode the three means of restraining a harmful pressure [thm]. *Enforcement:* restraint is enforceable only while each deviation's chance of becoming decisive stays below a threshold, for a deviation whose decisive position is worth more than punishment [thm]. Where leads compound, a growing share of capacity that has to be won raises that chance; where reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return, the same share narrows the working objectives that set the threshold; captured correctors and outgrown stakes lower the threshold further; each arm holds in its own model [thm], and in two reduced models the first two close together as the stakes outgrow fixed provision where decisiveness is high enough for the discounted loop gain to approach one, and not where diffusion holds that gain well below one [num]. *Correction:* where contestable coverage falls ever further behind hazardous variety, the chance of irreversible loss is at least a fixed fraction of the share left uncovered, and failure is shared, survival tends to zero whoever is racing [thm∣prem]. *Shared understanding:* relative accounting silences talk between two rivals and multiplies their conflict by $n/(n-2)$ among three or more [thm]. That the arms close together in the world is conjecture C0 [conj].
>
> **Why.** Decisiveness takes away the future that enforcement needs; tempo and capture, the time and independence that correction needs; relative accounting, the information that talk carries; and each erosion feeds the others [thm]. An objective not satiated in the stakes has no point of enough [thm]: growth is not the danger; the mind is.

### 5.1 Acceleration

Where actors contest capability directly, who prevails is decided by the contest of Section 3.4. The case for racing rests on three separate premises: **(decisive)**, $r$ is large; **(fast)**, leads open within short windows; **(unsaturated)**, no level of advantage makes more worthless. A technology can be decisive at a threshold and saturate above it (Section 7.2). Write $R_i$, $T_i$, $P_i$ and $S_i$ for an actor's payoff when all restrain, when it races against restrainers, when all race and when it restrains against a racer. In the objective an actor acts on, restraint is never a best response to a racing rival when $S_i<P_i$, which in the contest holds for $r<n/(n-1)$, given, among more than two, that restraining against one racer pays less than racing beside it; racing need not then be better in material terms. Where falling behind costs something beyond losing the prize, the contest is for the prize plus that penalty, and in the fully dissipative limit joining buys nothing over staying out. The race begins if any single actor has $T_i>R_i$; where those events are independent, its probability tends to one as capable actors multiply, exactly when the sum of their probabilities diverges (**Proposition 5.1**, S5) [thm]. The race is set by the most race-inclined actor, which is why any remedy must cover every capable actor.

**Proposition 5.2 (Dissipation) [thm].** The more decisive the advantage, the more of its value is consumed in the race to obtain it.
1. *Under a common self-model $\theta$, the share of the stakes dissipated in private cost and shared loss is $s_{\rm dis}(\theta)=r(n-1-\theta)(c+n\ell)/\big(n(c+(1+\theta)\ell)\big)$, increasing in $r$ and $\ell$.*
2. *If the technology's power scales the stakes and the shared loss per unit together while private cost is unchanged [prem], the dissipated share rises toward $r(n-1-\theta)/(1+\theta)$ for $-1<\theta<n-1$, without bound at $\theta=-1$. Total material payoff stays positive at every scale iff $\theta\ge\theta_g=(r(n-1)-1)/(1+r)$, which rises with $r$.*

*Proof:* S5, with the ceilings at four self-models [num: legacy v5].

What is spent is safety margin, accepted risk and delegated control. With $n=4$ and $r=1$, an accurate weight $\kappa=0.4$ toward what can be divided, the value used in Remark 3.10, lies below $\theta_g=1$: a gap between what accuracy supplies and what the contest requires, which only institutions, or coupling that grows with power, can close (crux C11).

**Remark 5.3 (The frontier switch: what cancer does when the body runs out) [thm].** *Let capacity expand into an unclaimed frontier, destroy rivals' capacity, or rest. At $\theta=-1$ the host is invisible, and expansion gives way to mutual destruction before the frontier is exhausted, once the frontier's marginal value, net of the cost of expanding, falls below the net gain in standing from destroying a rival, provided that gain is positive, since destruction has to beat rest as well as expansion; at any $\theta>-1$ expansion stops at a point of enough, and war among copies never pays wherever destruction's private cost exceeds what the objective held gains from a rival's loss, which holds for every $\theta$ above a bound below zero.* S5 states the conditions exactly, with a toy of four copies and the one finding in it that runs against this narrative [num: FS].

Among actors whose accounting is relative, peace is therefore a peace of abundance, not of accounting. Expansion is an anaesthetic for the winning model. Winning does not end the game: the logic itself is the replicator, a winner is a field in which the same selection continues (Proposition 7.3), and a self-replicating economy that carries it out into new frontiers carries the same switch with it [corpus: meta-attractor].

### 5.2 First erosion: enforcement

Cooperation among rivals is usually sustained by the future: restraint today is rewarded by restraint tomorrow. Decisiveness weakens the future. Consider a stage game repeated with discount $\delta\in(0,1)$ under perfect monitoring, each actor acting on $J_i$. Let $a^\ast$ be a profile with per-period value $C_i$. For a one-period deviation $a$ by $i$, let $g_i(a)$ be its one-period gain and $p_d(a)$ the probability that it yields, from the next period, a *decisive position*: an absorbing state beyond any punishment, worth $D_i(a)$ per period. Let $v_i$ be $(1-\delta)$ times $i$'s continuation value under the harshest credible punishment, computed in the same game, in which breakouts remain possible during punishment.

**Proposition 5.4 (The enforcement condition) [thm].** Restraint is enforceable only while the chance that a deviation becomes decisive stays below a threshold, deviation by deviation, for each deviation whose decisive position is worth more than punishment, and success lowers the threshold.
1. *$a^\ast$ is sustained by reversion to that punishment if and only if, for every $i$ and every one-period deviation $a$,*
$$(1-\delta)g_i(a)+\delta p_d(a)\big(D_i(a)-C_i\big)\;\le\;\delta\big(1-p_d(a)\big)(C_i-v_i). \tag{14}$$
*The left side is the temptation, immediate and decisive; the right side is the sanction that remains. The deviation with the largest gain need not bind: a quiet one, with a small gain and a high chance of breakout, can.*
2. *Equivalently, a deviation with $D_i(a)>v_i$ is deterred iff $p_d(a)\le\bar p_{d,i}(a)=1-\big[(1-\delta)g_i(a)+\delta(D_i(a)-C_i)\big]/\big[\delta(D_i(a)-v_i)\big]$. Wherever it lies in $[0,1)$, $\bar p_{d,i}$ falls with $g_i$, $D_i$ and $v_i$ and rises with $C_i$, weakly in $D_i$ where $\bar p_{d,i}=0$; it is homogeneous of degree zero in $(g_i,C_i,D_i,v_i)$, so stakes that all grow alike leave it unchanged.*
3. *The bound without the breakout term, $(1-\delta)g_i\le\delta(1-p_d)(C_i-v_i)$, is exact when $D_i=C_i$, necessary when $D_i\ge C_i$, and sufficient when $D_i\le C_i$.*
4. *As $p_d\to1$ the condition becomes $(1-\delta)g_i\le\delta(C_i-D_i)$. If $D_i\ge C_i$, sustainable profiles lie within the stage equilibria of the objectives held, strictly within when $D_i>C_i$: restraint must be preferred even to a breakout that succeeds. If $D_i<C_i$, cooperation that is not a stage equilibrium can survive $p_d=1$.*
5. *Let punishment be reversion to a stage equilibrium in which both of two actors race, worth $P_i$, and let at most one racer break out in a period, each with probability $q_b\le\frac12$; when the other breaks out, $i$ is subordinated, worth $V^{\rm sub}_i$ per period. Then $v_i=\big[(1-\delta)P_i+\delta q_b(D_i+V^{\rm sub}_i)\big]/(1-\delta+2\delta q_b)$; if instead breakouts are independent and a simultaneous breakout leaves the race as it was, $q_b$ is replaced by $q_b(1-q_b)$. When $D_i+V^{\rm sub}_i>2P_i$, reversion to the race is a lottery the deviator welcomes, and it can fail to deter while still leaving the deviator below cooperation, $P_i<v_i<C_i$, even where the deviation itself never breaks out; S5 gives an example in which it fails over a window of $q_b$ [num: ES].*

*Scope.* Perfect monitoring and an absorbing decisive position; a slow breakout enters through $p_d$, the chance that it becomes decisive before punishment can act (Remark 5.5). That part (1) decides whether $a^\ast$ is the path of some subgame-perfect equilibrium needs $v_i$ to be the least subgame-perfect continuation value: the extension of optimal penal codes (Abreu 1988) to stochastic games with an absorbing state, asserted for finite stage games with discounting and flagged for outside review. Where quitting the race pays the punisher, part (5) does not give the credible punishment value. *Proof:* S5, with a valuation check of part (1) and the deviator's best response over all deviation plans, which agrees with (14) wherever the punishment is credible [identity: EC; num: ES].

The folk theorem's promise depends on punishment remaining possible: a decisive position weakens it, a punishment that is itself a race weakens it again, and the quiet deviation that part (1) lets bind is the kind that masking hides (Section 4.4; S5 gives a pair) [num: ES].

**Remark 5.5 (Timely detection: enforcement seen from verification) [thm; identity: JX].** *Let punishment be triggered by detection and act before decisiveness, let $\upsilon(T)$ be the probability that a deviation is detected and answered within $T$ periods, and let a breakout need $T_{\rm dec}$ periods to become decisive. Then $p_d=1-\upsilon(T_{\rm dec})$, and, for a deviation with $D_i>v_i$ where $C_i>v_i$, timely detection must exceed a level that rises with the value of winning [thm]. In the one-period reduction of (14), which compresses the resolution into the period after the deviation and reads $D_i$ and $v_i$ as continuation values, normalized by $1-\delta$, conditional on escape and on timely detection, each including the periods before resolution, the condition for such a deviation reads $\upsilon(T_{\rm dec})\ge1-\bar p_{d,i}$. With the payoffs of Proposition 8.6 read as the same continuation values, cooperation $R=C_i$, a deviation that becomes decisive $T=(1-\delta)(C_i+g_i)+\delta D_i$ and one detected and answered $P-F=(1-\delta)(C_i+g_i)+\delta v_i$, its verification threshold $(T-R)/(T-P+F)$ is exactly $1-\bar p_{d,i}$, and its case $T<R$, cooperation without verification, is $\bar p_{d,i}>1$ [identity: JX].*

*Scope.* The reduction is exact when resolution takes one period, $T_{\rm dec}=1$; otherwise it is a reduced form, and the timing changes the threshold. S5 gives the exact condition for two timings. Where cooperation continues until detection and punishment starts then, the sanction waits as well as the breakout, and a slow clock weakens the sanction even where no breakout is possible; where monitoring is perfect and only the stopping of the breakout waits, the decisive value is discounted further. In the joint model of Remark S5.2 the first timing moves the crossing earlier and the second later [num: VF]. The verification threshold takes the sanction $F$ as given and leaves the rest to trust; (14) is the condition on the equilibrium path, with a credible sanction computed where breakouts remain possible (S5). *Proof:* S5 [identity: JX; num: JX, VF].

Both $T_{\rm dec}$, which a structural loop gain above one shortens (Remark S2.1), and $\upsilon$ are what masking hides (Remark 4.9), so an estimate of either that is to trigger a response has to come from correctors whose independence is certified (Section 12.1).

**Corollary 5.6 (Relative objectives and punishments that can be mirrored) [thm].** *Let the stage game be symmetric, every actor hold $\theta=-1$, and the punishment phase be exchangeable: its payoffs and transitions are invariant under relabelling the actors, and it starts from a state that treats the punished and the punishers alike, though strategies may condition on who deviated. Every symmetric profile then gives each 0. With two actors every punishment, mixed or pure, leaves the punished at least 0, since it can mirror the punisher; with $n\ge3$ in an anonymous game, so does every punishment in which all punishers use the same strategy. A symmetric cooperative profile is then sustainable only if $(1-\delta)g_i+\delta p_dD_i\le0$, and when $D_i\ge0$ only if it is a stage equilibrium in objectives.*

*Scope.* A decisive position reached through an exchangeable lottery is covered. A continuation that carries a label escapes the bound, as where an absorbing loss can strike only the target [num: ES]; so, with $n\ge3$, do punishers in different roles, whose punishment in S5's example is weak as printed and strict under small perturbations, but needs coordinating, since which punisher takes which role is a problem of equilibrium selection [num: CR]. *Proof:* S5.

Among relative actors in a symmetric game, then, cooperation that is not a stage equilibrium rests on punishments that break the symmetry [thm]. That such actors enforce it as blocs is a reading, not a result [conj]: the enforcement face of Proposition 3.12.

**Corollary 5.7 (What restraint needs where breakouts are possible) [thm].** *Where $p_d$ is near zero, because no deviation yields a position beyond punishment or breakouts are answered before they become decisive, restraint can be enforced by repetition among actors with $\theta>-1$, given monitoring, $C_i>v_i$, patience, and a punishment that does not itself raise the chance of breakout. Restraint survives every value of $p_d$ iff (14) holds at $p_d=0$ and at $p_d=1$; where $D_i\ge v_i$ this is $(1-\delta)g_i\le\delta(C_i-D_i)$ for every $i$ and every deviation, which for a deviation with a positive gain needs $D_i<C_i$. Otherwise, where $D_i>v_i$, durable restraint requires $p_d(a)\le\bar p_{d,i}(a)$ for every $i$ and every deviation, by two kinds of means: (a) changing the premise itself, so that deviations do not become decisive: not building capability of the decisive class (capability with which a deviation could reach a decisive position), holding it so that no party can use it alone (common custody), or detecting and answering faster than breakout; (b) raising $\bar p_d$: objectives under which a decisive position is worth no more than restraint, sanctions that survive success, and lower stakes in deviation.*

*Scope.* A classification of the terms of (14), not a proof that no other means exists. A decisive position worth less than cooperation does not by itself exempt (S5 gives an example) [num: ES]. *Proof:* S5.

**Proposition 5.8 (What a decisive winner gives) [thm].** After a decisive win, what everyone else receives is set by the winner's objective alone. *A winner holding $W$ decides, unconstrained, what share to give the $n-1$ others. With linear payoffs it gives nothing unless $\theta=n-1$, where it is indifferent; with logarithmic utility it gives $\theta/(1+\theta)$ for $\theta\ge0$; and a promise made before the win to give a fraction $\beta$ of a prize $\Omega$ to the others is worth breaking by $\beta\Omega(n-1-\theta)/(n-1)$, with no one to sanction it.*

*Proof:* S5 [num: WG, SM].

This is the formal content of Ψ2, "winner takes all, but also winner justifies all" (Definition 3.13), exact for linear payoffs. Its strongest answer is a winner that still needs the governed: one whose self-model is accurate toward what can be divided, $\theta=\kappa_W$ (Proposition 3.6(2)), gives $\kappa_W/(1+\kappa_W)$ under logarithmic utility [thm]. What the race cannot verify beforehand is $\kappa_W$ after victory, the winner's dependence on the governed once it has won; whether AI lowers it, by substituting for their work and their assent, is crux C13 (Section 12.3), and Section 8.3 meets the benign winner at its strongest. Since a lottery can still award a position beyond sanction, what has to be limited is what winning confers, not only how decisive it is (Section 10.2).

Decisiveness also breaks bargaining. Where one side's advantage is about to arrive and the side that gains it will set the terms (A8, S0), the other has a rational motive for preventive attack whenever its present chance of prevailing, less the cost of fighting, exceeds the value of peace on those terms; a shift spread over enough periods removes the motive (**Proposition 5.9**, S5; Fearon 1995; Powell 2006) [thm∣prem]. What drives it is not cheap war but the incredibility of future promises, and where leads compound, the loop is what brings the shift. Self-help under anarchy is an institution, not a necessity (Wendt 1992); the enforcement condition says which of its terms an institution would have to change.

### 5.3 Second erosion: correction, and the two channels of risk

Correction must see a harm, decide and act before the harm is irreversible. Section 4 showed how correctors become something success can buy; this subsection states the tempo and separates two channels of irreversible risk.

**The race channel.** Play the contest of Section 3.4 in rounds at its symmetric stage equilibrium, with stakes $\Omega_t$, decisiveness $r_t$, shared loss per unit $\ell_t$, net marginal cost $c_t$ and self-model $\theta_t$, under **(hazard–damage link)** [prem]: the hazard of the irreversible event in round $t$ is proportional to the shared damage, $h_t=a\ell_t\mathcal E_t$.

**Proposition 5.10 (Race-generated hazard and its four levers) [thm∣prem].** *Under the link, $h_t=a\,r_t\Omega_t\,\ell_t\,(n-1-\theta_t)/\big(n(c_t+(1+\theta_t)\ell_t)\big)$, and survival on this channel, $\exp(-\sum_{t\le N}h_t)$, stays positive iff $\sum_th_t<\infty$. Four levers act on $h_t$: the objective, through $n-1-\theta_t$; the contested stakes, which sharing a fraction $\beta$ scales by $1-\beta$; the residual harm per unit $\ell_t$, which correction lowers; and the net marginal cost, which a levy raises. At $\theta_t=-1$ everything rests on correction and cost. A hazard that settles at a positive constant gives zero limiting survival.*

*Scope.* A decomposition under a premise, not a necessity theorem. *Proof:* S5 [identity: RH].

Continuation acts on one lever, the residual harm per unit, while the stakes grow; a change of selection acts on all four. In general, for one irreversible event, survival tends to zero if on every surviving history the residual probability is at least a non-summable sequence, and stays bounded away from zero if it is at most a summable one, without independence between rounds (**Lemma 5.11**, S5) [thm]. **The objective of winning does not by itself prove that a residual danger is non-summable**, and cooperative alternatives are judged by the same lemma.

**Proposition 5.12 (The tempo bound) [thm].** When the situation changes faster than correction can follow, correction first loses robustness, then fails. *For a hazard mode $\dot y=ay+u(t-\tau_c)$: proportional correction cannot stabilize it when $a\tau_c\ge1$ (Hayes 1950); every linear time-invariant correction that stabilizes it has peak complementary sensitivity at least $e^{a\tau_c}$, so robust stability against weighted model error needs $|w(a)|e^{a\tau_c}<1$; and for hazard classes whose evidence arrives after a manifestation window $\tau_e$ that no testing shortens, $\tau_c\ge\tau_e$.*

*Scope.* Derivative action extends the first bound, which belongs to proportional correction only (S5). *Proof:* S5.

For hazard classes whose manifestation window no testing shortens, $a$ is the only lever left on $a\tau_c$, and slowing is what lowers it.

**The growth channel.** Three quantities are kept apart in a defined hazard class: the variety $Y_t$ of hazardous behaviour, counted as predefined hazard modes; its *contestable coverage* $U_t$, the number of those modes for which contestable correction is in place at a stated catch rate; and the residual probability of an irreversible loss after the correction in place. A correction decision is *contestable* if it is **legible** (a reason people can check within a budget without relying on the evaluated system's lineage), **overrulable** (a designated human body can reverse it in time), **exercised** (planted errors are caught at the stated rate) and **resourced** (human review covers a stated share of consequential decisions). Three premises connect the three quantities on a continuation branch, and Section 12 treats them as the central crux. **(coverage lags)** [prem]: $U_t/Y_t\to0$ uniformly over surviving histories. **(residual floor)** [prem]: after the correction in place, the conditional probability of the irreversible event is at least $h(1-U_t/Y_t)^+$; a correction architecture that lowers residual risk below this refutes it. **(shared failure)** [prem]: failure that starts with one actor reaches the others, so each actor's survival depends on the uncovered share of all. What makes (coverage lags) expected is **(variety grows)**, a motivation the theorem does not use: hazardous variety grows with compounding capability, at least exponentially, while consequences take their time and institutions deliberate at human rates; capability growth is measured, the step from it to hazardous variety is not (Section 7.3) [obs: EV5]. The expectation can fail: if AI raises the productivity of contestable understanding exponentially, coverage grows exponentially too and AI-assisted correction can keep pace, a question symmetric with Proposition 4.5 and measurable (S5).

**Theorem 5.13 (Coverage mismatch on a continuation branch) [thm∣prem].** Where contestable coverage falls ever further behind hazardous variety, the chance of irreversible loss is at least a fixed fraction of the share left uncovered, and failure is shared, survival tends to zero whoever is racing. *Under (coverage lags) and (residual floor), for every $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$ there is a $T_\varepsilon$ with $\Pr(\Phi_H)\le(1-h(1-\varepsilon))^{H-T_\varepsilon}\to0$. Conversely, if the residual probability is at most $\bar hu_t$ with $\bar h<1$ and the uncovered share $u_t=(1-U_t/Y_t)^+$ is summable, survival stays bounded away from zero.*

*Scope.* Under (shared failure) the bound is every actor's, whoever is racing; without it, it is the racer's own. Summability does not require that coverage ever catch up; finite sprints, saturation, freezing and rollback change the premises. *Proof:* S5.

This growth channel does not vanish when racing stops. A world that has left the race still needs capability to grow no faster than contestable correction can follow, so that the uncovered share stays summable: call that **E3**, developing at the pace of understanding. Call **E2** stopping: not building capability of the decisive class while the conditions for governing it are absent (Section 11.3), with rollback where hazardous variety already exceeds coverage. They are different arrangements (Section 11.3): stopping is not slowing. Divergences that hide under evaluation give both their strongest reason from inside alignment (Remark 6.8). Hazardous variety frozen after a sprint while coverage keeps growing leaves a finite exposure, and rolled back below coverage the exposure ends; so a winner that freezes or rolls back keeps positive survival, and what it gives up is permanent superiority (**Proposition 5.14**, S5) [thm].

**Proposition 5.15 (A point of enough) [thm].** A rule that stops hazardous growth where need is met lets coverage catch up; a bounded prize value bounds contest intensity. *Let the residual hazard on the growth channel be at most $\bar h(1-U_t/Y_t)^+$, with $\bar h<1$. If hazardous variety stops growing at a level set by need while coverage keeps growing at least linearly, the uncovered share reaches zero in finite time and survival on this channel has a positive lower bound. In the contest of Proposition 3.6(1), with costs and the other parameters fixed, intensity is proportional to the stakes for every $\theta<n-1$. Replacing the prize by a bounded value bounds that intensity. Finite satiation, a level beyond which more stakes give no further value, or a rule that caps hazardous scope is a separate condition: a bounded but strictly increasing value has no finite satiation point.*

*Scope.* Under its premises Theorem 5.13 applies to any continuation branch whatever the objective; the relative objective adds the invisibility of the shared loss. *Proof:* S5, with the frozen and the compounding case in numbers [num: PE].

The proposition separates what grows from what decides whether growth stops where need is met, and that is the mind: an objective not satiated in the stakes, relative standing above all, has no point of enough, since standing relative to others is never complete [thm]. In that precise sense, **growth is not the danger; the mind is** [corpus: meta-attractor, 2026-09-25]. The author's book puts it in one line: cancer grows too, but its growth exhausts the life of its host (癌亦增长，而其长竭母体之生) [corpus: shengsheng, 2026-07-14]. The rule has one boundary: hazardous variety that outgrows contestable coverage is dangerous under any mind, which is why the pace of hazardous scope, not of need-directed growth, binds even a generative order [thm∣prem]. The author draws the same line: risk outgrows correction because response, observation and feedback cannot grow exponentially, which holds for every institution of our society, a mismatch between the growth of real complexity and the growth of cognition (风险增长的速度超过纠错速度建立在我们的响应，观测，反馈机制，无法指数增长，对于我们社会下的所有体制来讲，这个都是对的，它实际上是现实复杂度增长与认知增长速度之间的错配产生的) [corpus: possibility-space, 2026-10-03]; and, here in an edited translation of mixed Chinese and English speech, exponential acceleration raises the complexity of our environment beyond what a person or humanity can understand, and understanding cannot grow exponentially (指数加速会不断提高你所在环境及其中关系的复杂程度，而这种复杂程度可以超出你、或者整个人类的理解能力……这样也不可靠) [47:35] [corpus: alignment-selection, 2026-10-04]. Because compounding also prices standing wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return (Proposition 3.9), the loop pushes the actors who would need a point of enough away from it. Deliberately bounded hazardous scope, with correction that keeps growing, is a sufficient route to E3; its connection to purpose is a design hypothesis. Section 6.4 states the same rule for the minds that training produces.

### 5.4 Third erosion: shared understanding

Coordination needs the parties to see the same danger and believe one another about it, and contest changes what talk is for: of the two logics named with Ψ (Definition 3.13), only cooperation reasons, and struggle argues to win. This subsection gives that line its content in information.

**Proposition 5.16 (Talk under relative accounting) [thm].** Relative accounting amplifies the conflict between a sender and a receiver: between two rivals it silences talk, and among three or more it multiplies their conflict by $n/(n-2)$, a factor that falls toward one as actors multiply.
1. *Let all $n$ actors hold a common $\theta\in[-1,n-1]$ with $n-1+\theta>0$, let a sender and a receiver play the uniform-quadratic game with a material bias $b_0$ between them, and let the receiver's action affect third parties linearly. Their ideal actions then differ by the effective bias $b_0(n-1-\theta)/(n-1+\theta)$, and informative talk exists iff it is below $\frac14$.*
2. *At $\theta=-1$ with $n\ge3$ the effective bias is $b_0n/(n-2)$, three times the material bias among three. With $n=2$ it is $b_0(1-\theta)/(1+\theta)$, without bound as $\theta\to-1$; at $\theta=-1$ two actors' objectives sum to zero at every state and action, and in any such game every equilibrium is payoff-equivalent to one without talk (compare Crawford and Sobel 1982), whatever the material bias.*
3. *Messages about a shared hazard are not credible merely because it is shared: a sender gains from the receiver's restraint whatever it intends itself.*

*Scope.* Cheap talk; costly signals and verification are outside it. With third-party payoffs whose sum is $-ky^2$ in the receiver's action $y$, the effective bias in units of the state is unchanged where $n-1+\theta(1+k)>0$, which keeps both objectives concave [thm; num: TK]. *Proof:* S5 [num: TK, T3].

A narrower self-model drains talk continuously, so the loop that narrows working objectives (Proposition 3.9) drains talk with them [thm]. The amplification fades as actors multiply, as the relative-gains literature found for cooperation (Snidal 1991). Rivals stop informing one another; that pairs who still talk align as blocs against third parties is a reading, not a result [conj]: the language face of Proposition 3.12.

**Measures** become targets and stop measuring (Goodhart 1975) [obs]. **Narratives** are carriers too [conj]: the frames that spread are selected for fitness to the contest; a group that totalizes itself shapes those around it as a model generates tokens, and a winning frame shapes the vocabulary of the next round until its words escape the game that coined them and a frame read as nature is no longer contested [corpus: selfishness-spiral]. By the time the frame is noticed one is already thinking inside it: the same dilemma named a "Wall Street Game" or a "Community Game" produced very different cooperation (Liberman, Samuels and Ross 2004) [obs]. **Professed doctrine** is a further layer: people say what is rewarded rather than what they believe, so a public consensus that few privately hold can persist, and then tip (Kuran 1995) [obs]; a race doctrine professed by actors selected for professing it can then be held less widely than it is stated, and visible cooperation can tip it [conj]. **Self-confirmation:** where a cooperative equilibrium exists, the belief that restraint will be exploited can persist because no one tests it (Fudenberg and Levine 1993); in a one-shot game in which racing is dominant, it is simply correct.

A system can retain abundant information while losing the ability to let it change course. A prediction of shared danger therefore has to be carried by what its receivers can check without trusting the sender, and so carried it can work against its own fulfilment (Section 11.2); "we are one" is first a matter of understanding built in a medium the pressure keeps degrading.

### 5.5 The scissors

The erosions feed one another: captured correctors weaken the sanction, and the objectives that compounding narrows drain talk (Section 5.4) [thm]. Under the conditions of Definition 2.1, one quantity moves both sides of (14) at once: the share of capacity that has to be won, which grows as the stakes outgrow provision not won in the contest.

**Proposition 5.17 (The scissors, arm by arm) [thm].** Growth in the share of capacity that has to be won moves both sides of (14) toward failure: the breakout probability where leads compound, its bound wherever the working objective narrows; captured correctors and outgrown stakes lower the bound further. *Let breakouts be detected and answered on a clock $\upsilon$ (Remark 5.5), let $\omega=\kappa_c\Omega/(\kappa_c\Omega+nk_0)$ be the won share of capacity, the stakes set against provision beneath the game (Proposition 2.3), and hold the rivals' conduct fixed.*
1. *Breakout arm. Where $\Gamma>1$ and a decisive gap $\bar\Delta$ is reachable, a lead grows from $\varepsilon_0$ to $\bar\Delta$ in about $T_{\rm dec}=\ln(\bar\Delta/\varepsilon_0)/\ln\Lambda^\ast$ periods in the linearized loop, with $\Lambda^\ast-1=(1-\beta)r\omega d_K-d_K-\delta_f$ in one channel (Remark S2.1). A larger $\omega$ raises $\Lambda^\ast$, shortens $T_{\rm dec}$ and so raises $p_d=1-\upsilon(T_{\rm dec})$, strictly wherever $\upsilon$ is increasing; with several channels the Perron root rises with the stakes in every channel, and with a channel's won share where its decisiveness rises less than in proportion to the stock ($\xi_{r,j}<1$), as in a ratio channel (Proposition S2.4).*
2. *Objective arm. Let the stage game and $a^\ast$ be symmetric, let a deviation raise the deviator above the mean payoff while lowering the mean, let punishment revert, without breakouts, to a symmetric profile whose mean payoff is below cooperation's, and let $D_i-C_i$ not fall as the objective narrows. Then, where $D_i>v_i$ and $\bar p_{d,i}\in[0,1]$, a narrower working objective lowers $\bar p_{d,i}$, since it raises the deviation's gain in objectives and shrinks the sanction that remains. To first order, with a fixed prize, the working objective narrows as $\Gamma_\delta$ rises; with a prize that grows with capacity, it narrows exactly where reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return (Proposition 3.9).*
3. *Sanction arm. $\bar p_{d,i}$ falls as $v_i$ rises, wherever it is below one; correctors whose capture weakens the sanction raise $v_i$, and a falling cost of control speeds that capture (Proposition 4.5).*
4. *Stakes arm. Stakes that scale $g_i$, $C_i$, $D_i$ and $v_i$ alike leave $\bar p_{d,i}$ unchanged (Proposition 5.4(2)). Where $(1-\delta)g_i$ and $D_i-C_i$ grow by a common factor relative to $C_i-v_i$, it falls, wherever $C_i>v_i$ and $\bar p_{d,i}<1$.*

*The pivot: in one ratio channel $\Gamma$ and $\Gamma_\delta$ are both proportional to $\omega$, and with several both rise with the stakes in every channel and with each channel's won share where $\xi_{r,j}<1$, so the breakout and objective arms are comparative statics of one quantity. Stakes that outgrow provision beneath the game move both blades toward closing; in ratio channels ($\xi_{r,j}=0$) provision that grows with the stakes holds both still, while in the others the gain grows with the stakes whatever provision does (Proposition S2.4).*

*Scope.* Each arm holds in its own model, as stated; with diffusion a gap can settle short of decisive, as with two carriers and the gap measured against their mean (Remark S2.1), and the first arm is then silent [num: TD]. Where rivals respond, the first arm runs on the equilibrium gain of Proposition 2.2(4), which the structural gain does not bound in either direction (Remark S2.2). That the arms move together in the world is conjecture C0, which Test E examines (Section 7.4) [conj]. In two reduced joint models, with the stakes arm off and rivals not responding, the blades cross as the stakes outgrow fixed provision where decisiveness is high enough for the discounted loop gain to approach one, and not where diffusion holds it well below one (in the first model, at $r\le1.5$ with $\delta_f\ge0.05$); neither moves where provision keeps pace (Remark S5.2) [num: JX, VF]. Read for policy, and only within these models: with timely detection and $D=C$ the objective blade does most of the closing, and provision that keeps pace with the stakes is the main lever; with a slow clock and $D>C$, detection speed also moves the threshold, by up to 0.13 in $\omega$ [num: VF]. *Proof:* S5, with the figures of the binary game of Remark S5.1.
