| Provision of essential services | Good enough, distributed, replaceable; a cap on any provider's share | **Distributed provision under common guarantees** of continuity and portability | R4, through the won share (Proposition 5.17) |
| The frontier of hazardous capability | Merging lowers the race at the first order, raises the return to influence at the second, and loses yardstick competition and lineage diversity (Remark 10.3) | **Merged accounting and custody of the frontier, plural lineages inside it, correction outside it**, contributions by formula | R4; R3a, R3b |
| Standing of those affected | Owed to all who bear the consequences (V3); inclusion that stops at a club over-produces the harm it does not bear (Proposition 3.12) | **A chamber of those affected**, drawn by a rule no participant controls, whose consent governs the slow loops | R7 |
| Due process against correctors | Every corrector is a selector that can err or be captured, and every closure states what would end it (Section 4; Remark 10.5) | **An appeal of different lineage and funding** from the correctors it reviews, with standing, access to evidence and deadlines | R3b; R7 |
| Authority (V4) | Answerable to those affected; not purchasable by success; set by row sums and contestability, **not by the degree of concentration** | **Separation rule plus chamber**: no body both revises the levy formula and appoints evaluators, and neither executes findings | R3a, R3b; R7 |

The forms are derived from function, as design principles for commons were derived from the institutions that lasted (Ostrom 1990), and with the same caution about scale: nested governance of a planetary commons is itself an open problem (Stern 2011). Federations are a precedent at both orders, minimum standards and fiscal equalization against the pressure of winning among members, equal representation and supermajorities against the selection of the selectors at the centre; the same federal form can select for winning or for contribution, and what decides is what it rewards (X10).

**A candidate configuration.** Discussion X10 develops one configuration far enough to expose its assumptions, as one of the candidates Section 12.1 compares under one range of uncertainty. It is a set of separately held functions, not one institution: common provision, standard-setting, evaluation, disclosure, rules and the levy formula, execution, custody, a chamber of those affected, and appeal, each held by a different body, separately financed and separately corrected, so that correction lies outside what it corrects. On a planet "outside" means outside the function: a different source of funds, a different lineage, and certificates anyone can recompute from public data. Each function is still held by one body, a separation of powers at one scale and not a polycentric complex of overlapping jurisdictions, which is a different candidate; nesting the functions at several levels above a common floor is an option, not a requirement, since Table 9.1 constrains functions, not the number of centres. Supermajorities and the chamber's consent govern the slow loops, amendment and the formula; the fast loop, restricting a specific capability, stays fast with review after the fact, since every veto point adds delay and the margin of Proposition 5.12 depends on it. Providers have voice without vote, since a vote would let success weigh in on its own assessment (Proposition 4.3), at a cost in local knowledge and compliance that X10 weighs. The configuration states its end, dissolution into its separately held functions, while protections against unilateral domination stay entrenched. By Proposition 4.3 it is the largest conversion target the theory describes, so capture of its own correctors (D4) is its hardest case, not a design row. Where execution through a host jurisdiction fails against the strongest participant (D5), what remains is the coercive restriction of frontier inputs, which the design does not settle: it has one home, the compared-not-settled tier of Section 12.1, under three conditions of its own.

Section 11 takes from here the five strands, the forms and their stated ends, and asks whether such an order can be reached from where the trajectory stands: survivable at every stage, preferred by each participant at each step, and held long enough not to be re-absorbed, with trust an outcome of the transition rather than its precondition.

# Part IV. Transition and judgment

Part IV's results on trust, signals, pauses and dwell hold in the models they state; its tracks, readiness dimensions and requirements of a reachable path are definitions, each row with the support it cites; its precedents are observations whose limiting conditions are part of what they show; and its judgment is a recommendation under the standard of Section 8.1.

## 11. A reachable transition

> **Claim.** An end state that every participant would keep is not thereby one they can reach: a path is reachable when it is survivable at every stage, preferred by every participant at every step given its beliefs, and held at every step until the slow variable that step moves has crossed its threshold [def]. In the field model, a step that does not carry its slow variable across its threshold, or is released on share levels before its dwell, is re-absorbed, so graduated steps reach only as a ratchet; and unless a lineage of the alternative persists that can be recruited when the rule changes, the hold a reform needs grows without bound with the age of capture [thm]. Narrow E1 among actors who price in a certain lock, and a costly signal that separates a survival-first actor from a relative one, need no trust; where the first steps are of this kind, trust is an outcome of the transition, not its precondition [thm]. A pause that changes nothing buys time, not survival [thm].
>
> **Why.** Trust is needed to cooperate against a possible defector, to be believed and to stay; payoff changes that raise restraint relative to temptation, narrow the bias of talk and lower the exit premium relax these respective constraints [thm]. Rule support falls only at the rate at which it is corrected, while shares lag it, so levels improve before safety does [thm]; a prediction carried only by assurances meets the third erosion [thm]. Each commitment moves a slow variable with its own time constant, so the commitments run as parallel tracks, the slowest started first [def].

### 11.1 Trust, and what relaxes it

Trust enters a transition in three places. To cooperate against a possible defector, an actor needs its belief $\varpi$ that the other will cooperate to reach the threshold $g_D/(g_C+g_D)$ of Proposition 8.6; where the value of winning exceeds the value of cooperating, $T>R$, verification has to supply what belief does not. To be believed, a sender needs the receiver not to discount what it says for what it gains by saying it, which relative accounting erodes (Proposition 5.16). To stay, a participant needs confidence that the arrangement will persist, which the net gain from exit erodes where that gain grows with the capability held in check (Proposition S8.5). Confidence in reliable instruments of verification lets parties cooperate without good will about what verification can see, and only once some belief has let them install it: installing them is itself a first step taken under $\varpi$.

The constraints have separate comparative statics (Corollary S11.1): raising $R$ or lowering $T$ with the other stage-game primitives fixed lowers the belief threshold where $g_C,g_D>0$; a wider working objective lowers the bias of talk; and a smaller positive relative premium in exit lowers its net gain. A common intervention relaxes all three only where it makes all three changes [thm∣prem]. Verification relaxes the first where $T-P+F>0$ and $P>S$, and only on what it observes; a sanction relaxes the first and the third only while it can still be imposed, so that against a defector whose position has become decisive it acts only through the bound $\bar p_d$ on the chance of breakout (Proposition 5.4(2)) [thm]. Sharing and provision beneath the game can act on these payoffs, and where they lower the price of standing they widen working objectives and the range of informative talk (Proposition 3.9); their net effect on trust must be checked using every stage payoff they change, not $T-R$ alone [thm∣prem]; a common frontier lowers the vulnerability of restraint, $P-S$, by pooling the restrainers' effort (Proposition 10.1) [num]; and the price of sanctions is escalation, which the comparison counts in $\Theta$ (Section 8.1).

Trust and verification are both conditions the transition has to meet, and they do not conflict: neither substitutes for the other, and each makes the other work. Trust grows from conduct that a lower value of winning makes rational and verification makes visible; verification works only where some trust reads its reports as information rather than as moves. The first steps of Section 11.2 need no prior trust to begin, so trust can grow as an outcome of the transition as well as be a condition of its later steps. In the corpus's words, "Trust is not a prerequisite—it is an outcome. It arises only when we have a shared understanding of reality and can reasonably expect others to act accordingly" [corpus: selfishness-spiral, 2025-03-14]. The author counts trust among the bottlenecks, the belief that the other side's messages are credible and its commitments will be kept, and how much it matters (这里面有几个bottomneck - 而这个来自于信任（相信其他方的消息可信并可守承诺），以及这个的重要性) [corpus: possibility-space, 2026-10-03]. And, here in an edited translation of mixed Chinese and English speech: whether one builds verification or raises transparency, without trust it is all in vain (无论你是建立核查机制（verification），还是提高透明度（transparency），只要没有信任，这些都白搭) [47:25]; so why decide from the start that the other side cannot be trusted? Let us first try making contact (所以，为什么一开始就认定对方不可信？我们先尝试接触) [41:57] [corpus: possibility-space, 2026-10-04]. Installing instruments, and reading their reports as information rather than as moves, already needs some prior belief, so the first step is to stop presuming the other's type.

Not every obstacle is a lack of trust, and the remedy follows the obstacle. A misjudged shared loss calls for evidence; a real conflict of incentives, for changed payoffs and enforcement; a coordination failure, for verification and graduated reciprocation (Osgood 1962); commitment and capture over time, for independent correction and steps each held for its dwell. Graduated steps answer the third only, and against capture they reach only as a ratchet (Section 11.4).

### 11.2 Prediction, evidence and signals

A prediction about a game whose outcome turns on what the players believe is one of the game's inputs, and it acts through two beliefs. Where an actor misjudges the shared loss, a derivation it can check corrects its own accounting: an actor that holds the prediction, and finds that restraint gives it higher survival whether its rival restrains or races, restrains whatever it believes about the others (Proposition S8.1(2)) [thm]. Where racing persists because each expects restraint to be exploited while a cooperative equilibrium exists (Section 5.4), the same derivation moves $\varpi$, and the equilibrium with it. That is counter-performativity, a model of behaviour making the world less like itself (MacKenzie 2006) [obs], and the sense in which this paper's prediction is written to be falsified by being shared (Section 0). It acts only where the outcome turns on a belief the prediction can change: it does not change what the game selects whatever is believed, and where racing is dominant in a one-shot game the belief it would correct is simply correct.

Messages about a shared hazard are not credible merely because the hazard is shared, since a sender gains from the receiver's restraint whatever it intends (Proposition 5.16(3)): a prediction carried only by assurances meets the third erosion. It has to be carried by what a receiver can check without trusting the sender: a derivation from public premises; verifiable evidence, such as third-party disclosure of incidents and joint evaluation suites; protocols agreed in advance, which commit a sender's disclosures before it knows what they will show (Kamenica and Gentzkow 2011); demonstrations that make a dynamic visible; and costly signals [def]. Shared understanding removes one road to conflict, since under common priors mutual optimism alone cannot produce war (Fey and Ramsay 2007) [thm], but not the others: in an idealized laboratory race in which unsafe play carried a private risk, borne by the winner, of losing one's winnings, choices tracked the state of the race, falling behind and a rival's unsafe move, more than risk preferences; the design did not test shared risk, as its authors note (EV11) [obs]. A change of prediction is the first step, not a sufficient one. The next is conduct a rival can read, which also tips a professed doctrine that few hold in private (Section 5.4); and the payoffs that make falling behind unsurvivable have to change as well, which the arrangements below and Section 12 are for.

**Proposition 11.1 (When keeping AI out of one's own decision loops is a signal) [thm].** A step that the lock makes cheap for a survival-first actor and the contest makes costly for a relative one separates them; a step that is cheap to imitate buys restraint only in proportion to what it costs a relative actor. *A sender is survival-first and prices in the lock (type $\mathsf S$, with probability $1-\mu$) or holds a relative objective (type $\mathsf R$, with probability $\mu$). It can take a step $\mathsf E$: a verifiable, durable removal of AI from its own strategic decision loops for a period, which costs type $\mathsf t$ the amount $c_{\mathsf t}$. A receiver restrains when its belief that the sender is $\mathsf S$ is above $\varpi^\ast\in(0,1)$, races when it is below, and is indifferent at $\varpi^\ast$, where it may mix; its restraint is worth $\Pi_{\mathsf t}>0$ to type $\mathsf t$, including, for $\mathsf R$, what $\mathsf R$ gains by exploiting it.*
1. *An equilibrium in which $\mathsf S$ takes the step, $\mathsf R$ does not, and the receiver restrains after the step and not otherwise exists iff $c_{\mathsf S}\le\Pi_{\mathsf S}$ and $c_{\mathsf R}\ge\Pi_{\mathsf R}$.*
2. *If $0\le c_{\mathsf R}<\Pi_{\mathsf R}$, there is no such equilibrium. Where prior trust falls short, $1-\mu<\varpi^\ast$, the receiver restrains after the step with probability at most $c_{\mathsf R}/\Pi_{\mathsf R}$ in every equilibrium; where $c_{\mathsf S}\le\Pi_{\mathsf S}c_{\mathsf R}/\Pi_{\mathsf R}$ the bound is attained, by an equilibrium in which $\mathsf R$ imitates just often enough to hold the receiver's belief at $\varpi^\ast$.*
3. *A step that is reversible or unverifiable, $c_{\mathsf R}\to0$, is therefore cheap talk: it buys no restraint that prior trust did not already give.*

*Scope.* Whether the step separates is a question of measured costs: where the lock is certain, keeping AI out raises the survival-first type's own survival, so $c_{\mathsf S}$ can be zero or negative (Proposition 6.4(1)), while a relative type, which delegates at every speed (Proposition 6.2(3)), pays for a durable removal with the speed it forgoes, the more the longer the period. Where prior trust falls short and the inequalities of (1) are strict, the separating equilibrium is the only one that survives the intuitive criterion. *Proof:* S11 [num: TR].

Unilateral, verifiable narrow E1 is therefore a candidate first move, cheap for an actor that prices in the lock and costly for one that values the speed delegation buys [thm∣prem]: the costly signal by which a security-seeking type reveals itself (Kydd 2000), which only inspection of doctrine and exercises keeps a relative type from declaring without taking (S11).

### 11.3 Four temporal arrangements, and readiness

Keeping AI out of the contest, stopping, slowing and pausing differ in cost, in verification and in how they fail. Section 5.3 defined stopping (E2) and slowing (E3) by what they do to the growth channel of risk; here each is stated as an arrangement.

**E1: AI out of the contest.** *The narrow form* keeps human authority over the decisions whose delegation would lock with near certainty: strategic and escalatory decisions irreversible within the time available for deliberation, such as the use of force and nuclear command. It comes with jointly verified limits, drilled fallback capacity and an evidence channel independent of AI, so that a human judging an alarm has reasons of their own. Among actors who price in the lock it is a strict equilibrium that needs no trust where the lock is certain, $q_L=1$, and it stays one while the lock fails with a probability below the bound of Remark S6.1 (Proposition 6.4(1)) [thm]. It leaves detection available, but $\upsilon(T_{\rm dec})$ measures detection and effective response together: $p_d$ is unchanged only where narrow E1 preserves that complete clock. Where human authorization adds response time, C7 must measure it and verify the enforcement bound with it (Remark 5.5) [thm∣prem]. Where the lock is uncertain, as in tactical domains with a large speed advantage, delegation can be an equilibrium too (Proposition 6.4(2), (3)): keeping AI out then rests on a commitment held in common, or on the signal of Proposition 11.1 where its costs separate, and bounded, reversible defensive automation falls under the test of O2 (Section 6.6; X11), not under E1. *The broad form*, AI not an instrument in any contest that tends toward dominance or concentration, judged by the game and not by the act, has its criterion and the three layers of its enforcement in Section 6.6; it is the scope, and the narrow form the first step. E1 is held in common: an actor that stays out alone while others race survives less than by joining wherever staying out raises a rival's chance of a decisive position enough (Proposition S8.1(3); X11) [thm], so E1 begins with the decision loops in which the lock makes it self-enforcing, and is extended by agreement.

**The deterrence objection**, at its strongest, is that a commitment that cannot be revoked is worth more than one that can (Schelling 1960), so that semi-automated retaliation would relieve the pressure to launch on warning. Survivable, human-controlled forces give the same relief without removing judgment, and the commitment value of automation is the lock itself, O2 given up in that domain (Lemma 6.3). An automated commitment cannot tell a false alarm from an attack, and false alarms have been stopped by human judgment, as in 1983 (Podvig 2002; Hoffman 2009) [obs: EV18]; escalation at machine speed is a common risk that relative selection does not see (Lemma 3.3), so the contest under-prices it [thm∣prem]. X11 gives the answers in full.

**E2: stop.** Capability of the decisive class is not built while the conditions for governing it are absent: an accurate model of the coupling, the shared prediction, and common custody. Where hazardous variety already exceeds coverage, it is rolled back, which ends exposure fastest (Proposition 5.14); for divergences that hide under evaluation, E2 and E3 are what lies beyond admission (Remark 6.8). Because E2 applies to the decisive class only, its cost, if AI turns out not to be decisive, is the delay of that class's margin over capable systems that are not decisive, including lives that earlier benefits would have saved. The cost can be large (crux C10) and, for those who bear it, irreversible; but it does not foreclose later choice, whereas if AI is decisive the loss it avoids forecloses choice for everyone, an asymmetry in the option set, not in the size of the losses (Arrow and Fisher 1974). Coverage of E2 comes from consent or from coercion, whose risk of escalation is counted in $\Theta$ and whose conditions are stated once, in Section 12.1.

**E3: develop at the pace of understanding.** In each hazard class, capability grows no faster than contestable coverage can follow, so the uncovered hazardous share stays summable (Theorem 5.13) and each hazard mode keeps its robustness margin (Proposition 5.12). It binds class by class, where coverage falls behind, and it binds a world that has stopped racing (Section 5.3); its carriers are thresholds in capability and in effective compute tied to measured coverage per class (Section 10.4). How much it slows depends on whether AI-assisted oversight raises coverage as fast as hazardous variety grows (crux C3a). Stopping is not slowing: E2 forgoes the decisive class, and E3 paces each hazard class to its coverage.

**A temporary pause**, the fourth arrangement, is valued by what changes during it (Proposition S11.2): where it adds no hazard of its own and the same process resumes after it, survival over any number of active rounds is unchanged, and so is its limit, which is zero where the residual hazards are not summable (Lemma 5.11); a pause after which they are summable, where before they were not, keeps survival bounded away from zero; and in the field model a pause that freezes shares while ordinary rule support keeps accumulating completes capture from every frozen share above $1/G$ (Proposition 4.12) [thm]. A pause that changes nothing buys time, not survival, and the time is for changing the causal conditions: verification, residual hazards, human capacity, enforceable limits, shared provision, objectives and institutions. An indefinite stop, a lasting cap, or a pause that produces a different decision process is a different policy. **A pause with no relevant change is not a certificate of readiness to resume**, and readiness has therefore to be defined.

**Definition 11.2 (Readiness to resume) [def].** *Readiness is assessed along seven dimensions: technical reliability (Q1), shared objectives (Q2), strategic incentives (Q3), effective correction (Q4), deployment constraints (Q5), retained human agency (Q6), and custody and verifiability (Q7). Each has observable indicators, a threshold set by the risk decisions of the domain of use with the error of measurement inside it, and an assessor whose own row sums are certified and who is insulated from rule-making during the pause. A dimension is met when its indicator has stayed past its threshold for its residence time, at least the time constant of the slow variable on which the dimension rests. Each dimension is decided by a sequential test registered in advance, with a model of the noise in its readings, two levels, the lower at its threshold and the upper above it by a registered indifference band, within which the pause stays, and two error rates: the rate of declaring it met when it stands at or below its threshold, set by the protection standard; and the rate of declaring it unmet when it stands at or above the upper level, bounded so that the seven together hold back a ready state with no more than a registered probability. A met dimension is re-opened by a test of the same kind, not by a single reading. All seven must be met; none compensates for another.*

These are dimensions to operationalize, not a checklist proved sufficient; S11 gives the question each asks and the slow variable on which it rests, and X11 records where each stands today. They do not require that every person's private motives change first: binding institutions can change the effective game while private competitive preferences persist, which is why effective correction is among them. Readiness presupposes rows R1, R2, R7 and R8b of Table 9.1 (Table S9.2), since no pause supplies them. Residence is required because levels move before slow variables cross (Section 11.4); the decision rule, because noise and conjunction together bias a fixed reading toward "not ready": seven dimensions, each read once with a 5% chance of missing a met dimension, pass a ready state with probability $0.95^7\approx0.70$ [thm], and a pause held past its evidence raises the cost of staying, so that coverage tips once that cost exceeds the cost of standing outside (Remark S9.1) [thm∣prem]. A sequential test turns noise into delay instead of bias, at error rates fixed in advance (Remark S11.3) [num: TR].

**The order.** By the trust each needs, E1 comes first, since it keeps humans as the players and, in its narrow form, is a strict equilibrium among actors who price in the lock; then E2 or E3, class by class. By verifiability the order reverses. E2 and E3 are the most verifiable among rivals, since compute and its hardware can be observed, though thresholds stated in compute erode as algorithmic efficiency improves, a rate condition that verification must meet (EV9) [obs], and hardware mechanisms for verifying compute use are proposed and prototyped, not deployed (EV17) [obs]. E1 is the hardest to verify, since it concerns software and internal procedure, and the most self-enforcing among actors who price in the lock, which is why it can begin as declared doctrine with inspected exercises (X11). A third order, by time constant, decides what is started first (Section 11.4).

The author's own order is conditional, and comes before these. If cooperation cannot be had, the first choice is that neither AI nor its use is built into the logic of struggle, AI neither existing as struggle nor used as its instrument (broad E1); failing even that, ASI and recursive self-improvement are not built, since the social and cognitive conditions are not met though the technical ones are, until cooperation becomes possible (E2), or development is slowed below the pace at which understanding can respond (E3) (如果不能合作：优先选择是不把ai本身和对ai的使用嵌入或者变成这种逻辑，即ai不应该以斗争的方式存在，也不能被当成斗争的工具……其次，如果连这个都做不到，我们人类应该有自知之明，先别搞asi，rsi，我们虽然技术条件达到了，但是社会和意识条件依然未达到，应该先停下来，让合作变得可能……要么就把发展速度降低到认知以及通过认知可以做出应当应对的速度以下) [corpus: possibility-space, 2026-10-03]. His closing judgment, that every other path is a dead end for humanity (其他都是人类绝路啊), is his value judgment [val]. He put the same order on 4 October, here in an edited translation of mixed Chinese and English speech: AI need not necessarily be stopped; at least first create a logic of cooperation in which AI does not join the struggle (其实也不一定非要把 AI 停下来。至少先创造一种合作的逻辑，让 AI 不加入斗争) [45:45]; if even that cannot be done, let us not build it yet, and first raise our own level of consciousness (如果连这个都做不到……那我们就先别造，先提升我们自己的意识水平) [46:02]; there is no way to win safely like this (没有办法这样安全地赢) [46:20] [corpus: alignment-selection, 2026-10-04]. The order is a branching, not a sequence of equals: broad E1 on one side, and on the other E2 or E3, between which the hazard class decides; the first side is preferred to the second, and the second is warranted where the first has not been achieved. The trigger of Section 12.1 is consistent with this condition and makes it measurable for the decisive class: a time to a decisive position that keeps shrinking is evidence that some actor is building toward dominance, which broad E1 excludes, so the trigger is the latest point at which the condition is read mechanically, not a second condition.

### 11.4 Parallel tracks, dwell and windows

Each commitment of the transition moves a slow variable and holds only if that variable crosses its threshold before the commitment is withdrawn (Proposition 4.11). The slow variables have different time constants, and one that is not moving when it is needed cannot be moved then: started one after another in the order of the trust they need, the slowest would start last and finish after the window it serves had closed. The commitments are therefore tracks that run in parallel, started in the order of their time constants, slowest first, and binding in the order of the trust they need, from the first track to the fifth [def]. The tracks are a candidate to be tested, not a claim that every setting follows them (X11).

| Track | Slow variable and its time constant |
|---|---|
| 1. Shared account of hazards | Shared understanding of the hazards, which cannot run ahead of the evidence: for late-manifesting classes, their manifestation window (Proposition 5.12) |
| 2. Narrow, testable commitments | Human capacity to take delegated decisions back, which decays at $\delta_h$ under delegation and recovers at $\varrho_h$ without it (Lemma 6.3) |
| 3. Fund correction and shared services | Rule support in each corrector domain: several institutional turnovers $1/(\varepsilon d)$, more the deeper the lock-in (Proposition S11.4) |
| 4. Delegation, access, custody, decision rights | The capability held in check, which raises the net gain from exit (Proposition S8.5); the lead time of custody's installation and verification, which must be shorter than the time to a decisive position |
| 5. Broaden as institutions prove themselves | The record of the arrangements under stress, and the self-models formed inside them: a cycle of stress and recovery, and for minds a generation |

Each track meets a compensating loop of its own, from measurement of hazards turned into measurement of who leads, on the first, to closure into a bloc, on the fifth; X11 gives what each track commits, the mechanisms it acts on, its compensating loop and its indicator of progress. Slowest first means that the third and fourth tracks begin before the trust they will bind on exists, and that the openness of the fifth is built in from the first. That is why Section 12.1 begins its first two tiers together.

**Dwell.** A reform that insulates rule-making from shares lets rule support decay at its rate of correction, but shares answer to rule support and lag it: they keep rising while support stays above break-even, for $\ln G/(\varepsilon d)$, so the reform has to be held through that rise and then long enough to repay the lock-in it added; from a captured share above $x_\sigma$, shares are back at their starting level before the state has left the basin of capture [thm]. A rollback judged on share levels is therefore premature (Test D); release exactly at the dwell boundary approaches the saddle, so the hold must exceed it by a margin. What decides is where rule support stands against the separatrix, which is not observed directly; near full share, the depth of lock-in cannot be read from the level at all. The hold grows with that depth, and depth with the age of capture, so without a floor under the alternative the hold has no bound [thm]. A lineage of the alternative that persists inside selection, and is recruited when the rule changes, bounds it [thm]: from a captured share of 0.99, with $G=10$ and rule-making ten times slower than adoption, a floor of 0.1% cuts the hold from 10.45 to 3.81 in units of $1/(\varepsilon d)$, while a share protected outside selection, which does not recruit, leaves it at 10.44 (Proposition S11.4, with the holds by gain, speed and depth) [num: DW]. Without the floor the alternative's share falls during the hold to about $10^{-31}$ at these settings, extinct in any finite population, so the hold is then unbounded and the floored values are the meaningful magnitudes [num: VF]. These holds take the conversion gain as fixed; where capture has built capacity that goes on converting, the gain stays higher and so does the hold. For real institutions the magnitudes are a conjecture [conj].

That is the mechanism of decay and renewal: in the field model, an order in which forms can fail and be replaced can be reformed in bounded time, and one that has frozen cannot [thm]. In the author's words, "look at the ecosystem, death is not a bug but a feature, changes and mutations are what keeps things alive — create a rich space of systems, not reducing to one rule, one attractor" [corpus: shengsheng, 2026-06-05].

**The ratchet.** In the field model, graduated steps reach the threshold only as a ratchet: a sequence of steps none of which carries its slow variable across its threshold and is held there strictly beyond its dwell boundary cannot be certified to escape capture, whatever trust it builds, and a step that crosses on one track does not cross on another (Remark S11.5) [thm]. A transition is not shown to arrive by small steps that each feel safe; it arrives, if it does, by steps each of which is kept.

**Windows.** Whether frontier development is still the keystone (Section 4.5) is a conjecture [conj]. The window closes as conversion among the corrector domains grows, which is why the correction track cannot wait for the trust that custody needs. Arrangements of this kind are often adopted when a crisis opens a window (Gunderson and Holling 2002) [obs]: what a crisis can adopt is a design prepared in advance, with its activation committed, and an emergency closure has to state before adoption what would end it, or it will not afterwards. Those who cannot leave the shared exposure need standing on every track (X11).

**Where things stand.** The first two tracks have seeds, in an international scientific assessment and a UN scientific panel whose appointment one leading state opposed, and in an affirmation by two rival states of human control over nuclear use, with a dialogue and an incident channel between them; narrow E1 has no multilateral document that the leading states now endorse, and its only shared basis is that affirmation (EV7, EV8) [obs]. The slow tracks, correction and custody, have no seed of comparable standing (EV3, EV17) [obs]. The seeds must become binding, verifiable and broader before the relevant losses or lock-in; X11 gives the record.

### 11.5 Requirements of a reachable path, and precedents

**Table 11.3 (Requirements of a reachable path) [def, with support: each row carries the tag of the result it cites].** *A path that changes what is selected has four separate requirements, and what is shown for each differs. The table organizes results proved elsewhere; it does not show that the four can be met together.*

| Requirement | What it asks | Support | Not shown |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Existence** | An end state that every participant prefers to keep: an equilibrium of the changed game, viable once in force | Under a common frontier, all cooperating is a strict equilibrium beside the race while the value of winning is moderate (Proposition 10.1) [num], though the effort each cooperator must contribute rises steeply with the value of winning (Proposition 10.1) [num]; an overwhelmingly strong actor takes part only for a share near its race payoff (Proposition S10.3) [thm]; the safeguards give viability once in force, with a margin the host sets (Theorem 10.4) [thm] | That the cooperative equilibrium is the one selected |
| **Reachability** | Every participant prefers each step, given its beliefs | In objectives, trust at least the assurance threshold (Proposition 8.6), which Corollary S11.1 lowers [thm]; in survival terms, none for an actor that holds the prediction where restraint survives better against both conducts, at the horizons where it does (Proposition S8.1(2), (5)) [thm]; none for narrow E1 among actors who price in a certain lock (Proposition 6.4(1)) [thm]; a costly signal where a rival's objective is unknown (Proposition 11.1) [thm]; graduated steps only as a ratchet (Remark S11.5) [thm] | That graduated steps alone reach the threshold |
| **Survival of the transient** | The host survives the transition, and no point of no return is passed first | The liability margin of Theorem 10.4(1) [thm]; human capacity kept above its lock-in floor (Lemma 6.3) [thm]; the uncovered share summable while the transition lasts (Theorem 5.13) [thm∣prem]; custody before decisiveness, which meets R4 by changing the premise of enforcement (Corollary 5.7) [thm]; the host's clock and the capture clock compared, not assumed (Proposition 4.13) [thm∣prem] | The margin outside the hosts computed; that the two clocks fall in the right order on the present path, a condition to measure |
| **Persistence** | Each step held until it is not re-absorbed, and the arrangement not undone by success | Commitment that keeps pace with the net gain from exit (Proposition S8.5) [thm]; correctors, rivals' verifiers included, with full row sums below one (Proposition 4.10(2); Proposition S10.2) [thm]; rules fixed before capture, and each step held for its dwell, which a recruitable lineage bounds (Proposition 4.11; Proposition S11.4) [thm; num]; coverage above its participation threshold (Remark S9.1) [thm∣prem] | Durability under stress (crux C4); that real institutions have the model's dwell [conj] |

Read as a problem of control, the table asks whether the present state lies in the capture basin of the target, the set from which it can be reached and then held under admissible steps (Aubin 1991); that is proved only in the one-domain field model, where the dwell boundary of Section 11.4 is the infimum time for entering the basin of absence; release must be strictly later, with a margin (Remark S11.6).

**Precedents.** The record of restraint among rivals qualifies all of this, and each precedent's limiting condition is where its analogy to AI is weakest (X11, corrected against the sources) [obs]. The 1946 proposals for international ownership of atomic energy failed on sequence and on enforcement against violators: **custody must precede decisiveness**. The biological-weapons record shows that a weapon endangering its holder can be renounced unilaterally, as one party did before the 1972 Convention, where its military value was low and the renouncer held another deterrent, and, under a convention without verification, that a covert programme can persist. Nuclear power after a serious accident shows durable safety under unchanged motives where the shared loss returns to its source and no prize is decisive, with later lapses: a change of selection, not of motive. The Montreal Protocol shows coverage made attractive by provision and enforced by conditions of access, once substitutes existed and the value of winning was low. Rivals survived where nature or balancing supplied saturation (Section 7.2); if advantage in AI is unsaturated, as the racers' own premise holds (crux C5), safety must come from changing what is selected before advantage becomes decisive [thm∣prem].

Section 12 takes from here the release rule of its second tier, readiness held for its residence time and judged against the dwell, and the reason its slowest preparations are warranted now: what binds late has to begin early.

## 12. The judgment

> **Claim.** Under the protection standard and the present evidence, a change in what the system selects for is warranted in three tiers, sorted by the kind of justification each step has, not by who would accept it [val]. Steps that wait on no crux are taken now, beside steps that stand until a named crux is measured favourable [rec]. Stopping the decisive class (E2), common custody and development at the pace of understanding (E3) are prepared now and activated by a mechanism committed in advance, on registered indicators estimated by correctors certified independent [rec]. Whole designs, the coercive restriction of frontier inputs among them, are compared under one range of uncertainty, each with its own failure modes; no path has yet been shown to meet the tolerance, and without comparative risk bounds their ranking remains a judgment under the standard [val].
>
> **Why.** The first tier keeps options open and measures the cruxes [val]. Custody has to exist before anyone could hold decisive capability alone, and the trigger fires where restraint is least enforceable (Proposition 5.4(4)), so an activation decided then would face the condition it is meant to prevent, and one committed in advance escapes it only where its means are in force before the trigger [thm∣prem]. At the third step what can be compared is which failures can be reversed, into what and by whom [val]. One party coming to hold decisive capability is irreversible on every path: the race selects for it, a design built to Section 10 contests it, and that design's other failures, its coercive fallback's escalation apart, have named reversals, none yet shown to work [thm∣prem; val].

### 12.1 The judgment, in tiers

The tiers are kinds of justification, not stages: the first two begin together, and what has the longest time constant starts first (Section 11.4). The author's own order, conditional on whether cooperation can be had, is given in Section 11.3: broad E1 is preferred, and stopping or slowing is warranted where it has not been achieved; tier 2's trigger is where that condition is read mechanically for the decisive class.

What the judgment gives is first a constraint. It says which strategies and which kinds of cooperation cannot reach the goal: those that leave unchanged what winning confers, those that bind conduct without moving thresholds (Section 8.2), those that integrate a part and not the whole (Proposition 3.12), and those that leave the decisive class to one party. And it holds the problem to one standpoint, that of humankind as one community of common destiny (人类命运共同体) [corpus: shengsheng], the one host with no outside of Section 3.6, from which no participant can step. Held together, these narrow the search space greatly. Within what remains the paper also gives solutions, by mechanism: the requirements of Section 9, the generative order of Section 10, the tracks of Section 11 and the tiers below.

**Tier 1(a). Warranted now, on every reading of the empirical cruxes [rec].** A step belongs here only if its justification does not depend on how any empirical crux comes out: measurement that decides the cruxes, preparation whose lead time may exceed the warning, an equilibrium without trust, or research that is a public good. The robustness holds within the values the paper adopts (V1–V3) and the stated assumptions on cost and lead time: it gives each step a reason on every reading, not a proof of its net value at any scale, so each is scaled to the evidence, and preparation stays apart from commitment to a particular activation rule, which is tier 2's. TESTS sets each item against every crux.
1. **Shared measurement** of hazards, coverage and residual risk per class (Section 5.3), kept apart from measurement of who leads, with masked classes measured adversarially; audited disclosure, from every party, of conversion into correctors (funding, appointment, access, the authorship of standards), sanctioned above the gain from omission (Lemma 3.3(3)); certified row sums for every corrector domain (Proposition 4.10(2)); the correlation of evaluators' misses, and control evaluations in which the red team knows the protocol (Proposition 6.9); and, wherever models evaluate models, the share of evaluation done by the evaluated lineage (Remark 4.9). Every trigger of tier 2 rests on it, and it is worth most where readings disagree.
2. **Verification research and pilots**, of compute, training and custody, and of detection faster than breakout: an instrument must exist before the deviation it is to detect, since detection in time is the enforcement condition seen from verification (Remark 5.5) [thm].
3. **Alignment and control research, commonly funded and openly published.** What lowers the alignment tax is a public good that competitors undersupply (Lemma 3.11) [thm∣prem]; it pays whatever the tax turns out to be, and where it brings a class's tax below zero, the safeguard spreads without coordination, while at zero it is neutral under selection (Section 6.4).
4. **Preparation of common custody**: its design, institutions, indicators and activation rule registered now, and stress-tested against D1–D8 of Definition 8.4; the commitment to activate is tier 2's. If decisive advantage never arrives, this costs only the preparation; if it arrives, custody begun at a warning comes too late wherever building it takes longer than the warning gives (Section 7.2) [thm∣prem]. X10 develops one candidate: a custody body of rotating members from plural lineages and jurisdictions, certified by its row sums, that releases what it holds only by a quorum including non-aligned members.
5. **Narrow E1**, for the decisions whose delegation would lock with near certainty, beginning with declared doctrine and inspected exercises and verified wherever verification is possible (Section 11.3). Among actors who price in a certain lock it is best whatever the others do, a strict equilibrium that needs no trust, and "near certainty" has an exact bound (Proposition 6.4(1); Remark S6.1) [thm]; taken verifiably by one actor alone, it is a candidate first move, a signal wherever durability makes it cost a relative actor at least what the restraint it induces is worth to that actor (Proposition 11.1) [thm].

**Tier 1(b). Warranted now; withdrawn where a named crux is measured favourable or the step is shown to fail [rec].** What items 6 and 7 guard against arrives before the evidence that would show it: an alarm averaged over captured evaluators fires late or not at all (Proposition 4.8); where capture precedes evidence, the host crosses its floor before any alarm (Proposition 4.13); and a masked divergence is detected only as often as it shows under evaluation (Remark 6.8) [thm∣prem]. A protection that waits for that evidence waits too long, so under the protection standard the burden of proof lies on withdrawal: each item stands until its crux is measured favourable, and then loses only the part that crux bears [val]. That burden is a precautionary premise of the standard, not a comparison the standard has computed: hazard arriving before evidence is a reason not to wait for the catastrophe, not a bound on the step's own risk. So an item also yields where the step itself is measured to fail or to raise the protected risk, the escape Test M and C4 register. Item 8 stands on every reading, and its cruxes bear only the reliance on it to change conduct.
6. **Independent correction**: formula funding at arm's length, mandated access, rotation, and rule-making insulated from the success of the evaluated, growing at least at the rate the rate law requires, and faster by $w\delta_h$ where it rests on human capacity that delegation erodes (Proposition 4.5(2); Proposition 4.6(1)) [thm]. Withdrawn beyond present institutions where C2 is measured favourable (Test M); the measurement of item 1 continues even then, since a certificate valid today fails later where correction falls below that rate and capacity tracks its selected level [thm].
7. **Common admission constraints** before consequential deployment, internal deployment included, for every class of divergence whose costs fall on common terms: sanctioned at least at every type's gain from evasion, which keeps the divergence from spreading and, where every such inequality is strict, removes it under cumulative selection, and equal in incidence among those who comply, which keeps the constraint neutral (R8a, R8b of Table 9.1), with audits for hidden objectives and loyalties (Section 6.3). Withdrawn for a class whose tax is measured negative, since its safeguard then pays privately and spreads (C9); at a measured zero tax the safeguard is only neutral, and variants already deployed without it stay, so the constraint is withdrawn there only on evidence that the safeguard persists without it. Masked classes are admitted by capability threshold, up to where control protocols that do not assume the system's cooperation remain safe, with tier 2 beyond (Remark 6.8); this gives way to admission on evidence the system's behaviour controls less directly, up to the capability at which independent auditors are shown to bound masking (C12).
8. **Shared provision of useful services**, good enough, distributed and replaceable, **and the divisible gains of capability shared as they arise**, where they can begin. They realize joint value on every reading of the cruxes (V1, V5) [val]. Capability provided to contestants without being won, sense (a) of Section 2.2, lowers the won share on which the scissors turn (Proposition 5.17), and gains shared as they arise, unlike a promise for after victory, do not rest on a winner's restraint (Proposition 5.8) [thm]; services provided to persons act through the won share only where they enter a contestant's decisive stock, and otherwise through the drive (C8) [conj], and services from many providers act through caps on dependence (X8, under D5). A measurement can withdraw only the reliance on them to change conduct: if conduct is unchanged under substantial sharing (C6), or provision leaves the weight on winning where it was (C8), that reliance moves to custody and pace, and the sharing stays for what it realizes [rec]. They are not accepted across camps, and the judgment does not claim that they are.

**Tier 2. Prepared now; activated by mechanism on registered indicators [rec].** Here belong the steps whose justification turns on a crux that can be measured in time, and with them the substance of the change: stopping the decisive class (E2), with rollback where hazardous variety already exceeds coverage; common custody of the decisive class, in force; and development at the pace of understanding (E3), per hazard class. Stopping is not slowing, and each is activated on its own indicators (Section 11.3); the race's own premise is the reason to prepare them now (Section 7.2).
- *Trigger.* **A trigger fires when the estimated time to a decisive position, less the time custody needs to be installed and verified, falls below a margin registered in advance**, and custody and E2 activate; waiting until decisiveness is shown would be too late. Its indicators are the persistence of leads, read on every measure, the gap between open and closed models (C5), and detection latency against the time from breakout to decisiveness (C7). E3 activates for a hazard class whose coverage falls behind: residual risk not falling (C1), or catch rates of planted defects (C3a) or, for masked classes, an independently assessed safety case failing at the admitted capability (C12), with detection and enforceable sanctions compared jointly with the changing tax (C9) when deterrence is the proposed control.
- *Estimator.* The time to a decisive position and the timeliness of detection are what masking hides and strategic disclosure shades (Remark 4.9; Remark 5.5), so they are estimated by correctors whose row sums are certified and whose present independence is assessed through current capture and inherited rule support (C2), with misses measured for correlation (C3b), from channels that observe actions directly (Section 4.4), with any bound on masking drawn from evidence the observed control less directly [rec].
- *Commitment.* **Activation is committed in advance and executed by mechanism, not decided afresh when the trigger fires; the commitment is taken now, conditional on custody passing stress tests registered in advance (D1–D8).** The trigger fires where restraint is least enforceable (Proposition 5.4(4)), as the breakout arm of Proposition 5.17 raises $p_d$, so a collective decision taken there would inherit the enforcement failure it was meant to prevent [thm∣prem]. A commitment made earlier is itself a promise about conduct at the trigger, and the same condition binds it unless its means are in force before the trigger: controls on compute and weights already held by custody, such as dormant hardware limits or escrowed keys, so that the deviation it would stop is no longer available (Corollary 5.7(a)), or a sanction that survives success already in place (Corollary 5.7(b)) [thm∣prem]. Where neither is installed, activation against the leading party rests on the coercive fallback of tier 3. Installing such dormant means is part of what is prepared now, conditional on their passing D4 and D5, since controls held by custody are themselves the largest conversion target (Proposition 4.3). With its means in force, preparation shortens the delay of the response from building, deciding and detecting to deciding and detecting, and commitment leaves detecting and executing, with the further delay of an actuation that fails, since commitment removes deliberation, not execution: that is the delay Proposition 5.12 bounds when the mode to be held is a lead that compounds [thm∣prem].
- *Release.* Release reads indicators of its own, each held for the dwell time of the slow variable it rests on (Definition 11.2; Section 11.4), not those of activation run backwards, which would release too early: share levels recover before safety does, in the field model [thm] and by conjecture for institutions [conj], and capture that makes delegation look safe is harder to undo than to prevent (Proposition 4.6(2)) [num: QS].

**Tier 3. Compared, not settled [val].** The whole designs are the separately held configuration of Section 10.5, developed in X10; a polycentric complex of mutually recognized institutions; a verification agreement with shared provision; regulated competition that meets Table 9.1; an agreement of the type of Section 8.3(g); the benign winner; and the coercive restriction of frontier inputs. They are compared with continuation and with one another on the durability tests of Definition 8.4, in one range $\Theta$ that includes each one's own failure modes.

The coercive restriction enters the comparison only under three conditions of its own, two set by mechanisms of Part I and one by value: an equal burden, the restrictor's own frontier work included, since a stop borne unequally re-sorts those it binds and is evaded wherever evasion pays their type (Lemma 3.3(2), (3)) [thm]; execution by correctors certified independent of every party it restricts and of the restrictor, with full inbound row sums below one and present-state tests of independence, since otherwise the restrictor is the largest conversion target there is (Proposition 4.3) [thm]; and the consent of the chamber of those affected (V3; R7) [val]. Its escalation hazard is counted in $\Theta$ (Proposition 8.5). Even under the three conditions the theory does not derive that its net effect is positive, since denial raises the weight on winning and the trust threshold, the hard case of Section 8.3(f).

Under the rule for $\Theta$ every path's worst case includes an irreversible loss, and no path has a bound on its probability over the protected horizon shown to meet the tolerance, but admitting such a loss does not show that its probability exceeds the tolerance. The standard's third step would prefer the lowest bound that keeps correction open (Section 8.1); it needs estimates on both sides, which the cruxes would supply and do not yet. The ranking is therefore a provisional judgment, stated as one [val]. The preference for changing selection rests on two reasons, read from the failure modes X8 lists path by path, each with its support.
- **(i) Reversibility over every failure mode.** A failure is reversible only if its reversal is named, stops short of O1 and O2, and is executed by someone other than the failing party. Read from its own documents, the design names proposed reversals: dissolution, into separately held functions; capture of one function, by recertification; interruption of an essential service, by funded standby; collusion among its checks, by rotation and plural lineage; overreach of a common constraint, by a sunset and appeal (X8, row by row); none has yet been shown to work, and whether they do is C4 [conj]. The escalation of its coercive fallback, which every coercive path carries, and the failure of (ii) have no such route. For the other failures, late or unsuccessful correction can also cause an irreversible loss: a masked hazard can escape before recertification, and standby can arrive after an essential-service failure has caused catastrophe. These branches enter the same comparison (X8). Continuation's failures are reversible only while no position is decisive and no floor has been crossed, and its worst have no reversal: coverage outgrown and a floor crossed (Theorem 5.13), capture before evidence (Proposition 4.13), a decisive position reached (Proposition 5.8), the capacity to overrule lost (Lemma 6.3) [thm∣prem]; and for hazards that manifest late its premises cannot be measured before they bind (Proposition 5.12). The preference gives weight to the design's specified independent routes of correction, whose availability and timely execution must be tested against the alternatives. Their existence does not establish lower loss probabilities: whether correction succeeds turns on cruxes on both sides (C1, C2 and C5 for continuation; C4 for the design), and the ranking's reliance on the design's bounded own risk is C4 (Section 8.1). One condition reads every reversal, on either side: it comes before the protected loss and is executed by an independent corrector that survives. Under it a restrictor's converted correctors are irreversible only where no independent corrector survives, as the design's captured custody is; the capacity to overrule, once lost, returns after a recovery interval (Lemma 6.3), so its loss is irreversible where the contest or another hazard denies that interval or an irreversible decision has already been taken; and funded standby prevents loss in an interruption only by arriving before it, since restoration does not reverse what the interruption caused. Keeping options of correction open is staying in the correctable kernel (Definition 8.1): a state from which a named reversal, if it works against every error in $\Theta$, still avoids the protected losses lies inside it, and a failure without one has left it [def].
- **(ii) The irreversible failure every path shares.** One party coming to hold decisive capability has no reversal on any path, whether by a decisive win, a winner's restraint failing, a sprinter's freeze broken, a covert project escaping verification, a restrictor's correctors converted or, for the design, the custody of decisive capability captured (X8). The race produces it by construction and selects for it (Proposition 7.3) [thm∣prem]; the design contests it by separation, plural lineages and the tests D4 and D5, the guardian test of Remark 10.5 applying to custody first. The same loss stands on both sides; what differs is whether a path selects for it or is built against it.

A third consideration orders only the futures in which a crux is measured favourable: among paths that measurement brings inside the tolerance, the standard's second step applies, greater useful benefit more fairly distributed, and there the design's case is the joint value it realizes and the race dissipates (Section 10.1). A reader who weighs these reasons differently can reach another ranking; Section 12.3 says which measurements would move it.

**The scope.** Broad E1, AI not an instrument in any contest that tends toward dominance or concentration, is the scope of what has been argued, and narrow E1 its first step (Section 6.6). Between them the game itself is changed, by lowering its convertibility, its decisiveness and the conversion of success into control, and the tiers begin that change.

### 12.2 Why, and where the leverage is

The judgment follows the chain of Section 1.2, each link with its own support, and acts where the chain says the system can be moved. "Root" has two senses. *Causally*, the root is a loop, not a belief: selection shapes minds and institutions, and the minds and institutions it selects reshape selection; neither comes first. *As a point of leverage*, the root is understanding, and it acts in two places, which answer the two errors of Remark 3.10. As the transmission through which actors come to count what relative selection makes invisible, the one term in which a benefit common to all can rise without a rule (Lemma 3.11) [thm], it corrects the held boundary. The working objective that compounding sets is no error but each actor's correct computation of its interest, which no argument corrects [thm∣prem]; understanding reaches it only as the way actors agree on which parameters to move, and hold them there for their dwell time.

**The theory of change.** Understanding corrects the held boundary and makes agreement possible; agreement on what winning confers, given executive force and held long enough not to be re-absorbed, changes the game; understanding without executive authority, however far it grows, may change nothing (Section 4.3). Its first step, understanding shared, is the one a paper can take, which is why this one is written as a prediction to be shared, carried by a derivation each actor can check: shared, a prediction of this kind can work against its own fulfilment (Section 11.2).

**The hierarchy and the thresholds.** Meadows ranks twelve places to intervene in a system, from parameters (#12) to the paradigm (#2) and the power to transcend paradigms (#1) (Meadows 1999), and the account here sits inside that ranking. The structural loop gain is a reinforcing loop (#7) and correction a balancing loop (#8); consequences returned and measurement shared are information flows (#6); one rule for all and the separation rule are rules (#5). Selecting the selectors is the reinforcing loop seizing the power over rules and structure (#5, #4), which is why this paper's levers sit on the coupling between them, the conversion of success into control ($\chi$ and the $b_{ij}$; Proposition 4.3). Changing what winning confers is a change of goal (#3), made by moving parameters, sharing, provision and convertibility, across their thresholds, which is Meadows's own exception, a parameter that is weak until it moves into a range that sets off a place higher on the list: below its threshold a reform is re-absorbed, and across it, it is not (Proposition 4.11) [thm]. In the corpus's words, "If we don't solve dominant attractors, focusing on minor adjustments won't change the outcome" [corpus: meta-attractor]. The paradigm decides which parameters are moved and whether they are held long enough, and it can change only if the system is understood. Both accounts stand: the paradigm is the highest place, and it acts through thresholds. A lever can also be pushed the wrong way, the common case in Meadows's account; making winnings decay faster is one (Section 2.2).

**Delays and buffers.** The high places move slowest and meet the most resistance, and the path from understanding (#2) to agreement on rules (#5) runs through transmission, which relative selection works against. Two low places, which meet little resistance, buy that path time. Delays (#9): where the delay of correction cannot be shortened, slow the change it must follow until the delay is short against it, as Meadows advises; that is E3 (Proposition 5.12). Buffers (#11): a lineage of the alternative that can recruit shortens the hold a change needs before release (Section 11.4) [num: DW], and standby capacity for essential services and the host's margin above its floor lengthen the time a shock can be absorbed before a floor is crossed (Theorem 10.4). Neither changes what winning confers; each buys the dwell time in which agreement can. What would move the judgment is stated next.

### 12.3 Cruxes

Each crux is evaluated on its own. TESTS gives the present evidence, the next measurements, the registered tests and the three outcomes that would redirect the argument, classified before they are seen; Table S9.2 carries each requirement to its threshold, mark and test. C1 is the central crux: on it turn E3 and the case for E2 (Section 5.3).

| Crux | What it is | What a measurement would change |
|---|---|---|
| **C0 Interlock** | Whether, in the world, a shared parameter moves together the thresholds of Table 7.1 it enters, each shown in its own model, the arms of Proposition 5.17 among them, and the signatures of several groups | Interventions on a shared parameter that move only their own signature over the dwell time: the interlock fails for that parameter, and the thresholds it enters are separate problems |
| **C1 Residual risk after correction** | Whether hazardous variety outgrows contestable coverage on the present path, class by class, masked classes measured adversarially (C12) | Cumulative residual risk per class, at deployment scale and over a registered horizon, bounded below the tolerance with its uncertainty, and the hazard summable under present incentives; a falling trend alone is evidence of improvement, not release, since a hazard can fall to zero while its sum diverges: E3 lapses for that class, with its trigger, and the case for E2 as far as it rests on coverage; the case resting on decisive advantage stands with tier 2's forecast trigger |
| **C2 Conversion into control** | How far success buys influence over correctors and rules | Row sums below one under present funding and access, with findings executed against leaders (Test M): item 6 is withdrawn beyond present institutions; measurement continues |
| **C3a Scalable oversight** | Whether AI-assisted oversight raises contestable coverage as fast as hazardous variety: catch rates of planted defects at fixed human hours as capability rises | If it does, the case for slowing weakens for the classes it covers; evaluators stay separated from success |
| **C3b Independence of AI oversight** | Whether AI overseers fail independently of what they oversee: the correlation of their misses and their rate of collusion, same lineage against different | Low correlation and no collusion: redundant checks count toward coverage. Otherwise only independent lineages count, and redundancy stops at the ceiling of Proposition 6.9 |
| **C4 Durability** | The breakdown and capture of an arrangement that changes selection, enforcement against its strongest participant included, whether the means that execute tier 2's activation are in force before its trigger, and whether its reversals work; the winner's own correctors | A design failing its stress tests worse than continuation is rejected, without vindicating the race, and another meeting Table 9.1 is needed; a leading coalition with certified correctors passes D4 (Test I) |
| **C5 Decisiveness** | Whether advantage is decisive and unsaturated; leads differ by measure | Leads that persist on no measure, and others balancing a rising actor: activation of custody and of E2 lapses; preparation stays |
| **C6, C8 Rewards and the drive** | Whether sharing changes conduct, and whether provision beneath the game lowers the belief that one is in a struggle for survival, and with it the weight on winning | Conduct unchanged under substantial sharing: reliance moves from sharing to custody, pace and provision, which still lowers the won share through capacity (Proposition 5.17); sharing stays for the value it realizes |
| **C7 Detection against breakout** | Detection latency and response time against the time from breakout to decisiveness | Detection reliably faster keeps $p_d$ small (Remark 5.5), and custody is not the only route to it |
| **C9a Capability gap** | Whether countering a common-cost divergence costs capability at fixed compute, class by class | Zero or negative for a class, net of C9b and C9c: item 7 is withdrawn for it. Positive, yet countered by developers alone: $(A_\tau)$ fails for that class (Test L) |
| **C9b Monitoring and control overhead** | The compute, latency and labour that monitoring and control protocols add | What admission by control protocol costs each developer, against what it protects |
| **C9c The speed tax** | The time lost waiting for evidence that arrives only after a manifestation window | Positive wherever evidence lags (Proposition 5.12), so no measurement removes it: its size sets what a common pace costs, not whether one is needed |
| **C9d Cost of tax-lowering research** | What it costs to bring a class's tax to zero or below | Low: item 3 is the cheapest lever |
| **C10 Forgone benefits of E2** | The decisive class's margin over capable systems that are not decisive | A large margin reachable only through that class raises E2's cost and moves the comparison toward E3; its weight against catastrophic loss is normative (N1) |
| **C11 Coupling and power** | Whether unpriced coupling grows with power | If it does not, the gap between accuracy and what the contest requires falls on institutions |
| **C12 Verifiability** | Whether independent evidence establishes safety at the admitted capability; for deterrence, auditors' detection times the enforceable sanction compared jointly with the changing alignment tax (C9) | Deterrence remains effective while the expected sanction covers the tax, even if detection declines. Replacing item 7's capability threshold also requires an independent safety case; deterrence alone does not establish one. Where assurance fails, capability limits and E2 or E3 beyond them remain protective recommendations under the standard, where broad E1 has not been achieved; failure of assurance does not prove the residual hazard floor (Remark 6.8) |
| **C13 Dependence on the governed** | Whether AI lowers the dependence of institutions, and of a winner, on people's labour, consent and judgment | If it does, alignment to a principal stops meaning alignment to the people it serves, what an accurate winner gives the governed falls with that dependence (Proposition 5.8), and the residual risk of every path resting on a winner's or an institution's restraint rises (Test I) |
| **N1–N3** | Discounting and risk attitude, with the weight of benefits forgone; the weight of O2 against O1; correction no one can understand | Normative choices, to be stated by each reader |
| **N4 Standing of AI systems** | Whether AI systems have standing in V3's sense | Granting it adds the systems to those whose capacity to contest an optimization V3 protects; the priority of O2 during the transition stays (Section 6.6) |

None of the cruxes is a conjunction of the others; a conjunction would say that regulated competition does better only by becoming this paper's own design. If C1 and C2 were both measured favourable, the diagnosis of the present path would weaken. If C5 and C7 were both favourable, the activation of custody would lapse, but the requirements on correction, coverage, measurement and admission would stand, and so would the facts that make them requirements: one host, coupled actors, no outside. If regulated competition in a set of jurisdictions meets a row of Table 9.1, the text will say so without renaming it. What stands whatever the cruxes show is what the conclusion states.

### 12.4 Conclusion

Where whatever can gain or lose share is selected by relative success, what is won converts into the capacity to win again, and capacity converts decisively into winning, the carriers are under the selection pressure of winning: as the stakes grow, the gain from defection rises and the sanctions, the correctors and the shared language that could restrain it weaken, each in its own model of Part I, and where the prize grows with what all reinvest, success itself raises the stakes (Proposition 3.9); that these move together as the stakes outgrow what does not have to be won is the conjecture the diagnosis tests (C0). Where success can also buy the selectors, the selection itself reproduces the weakening, and that closure is the meta-attractor of winning: as the stakes grow it accelerates the contest, makes it harder and harder to regulate, and alienates language. Inside it each actor computes correctly and the game is wrong: wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return, looking ahead weighs standing above what all share, so the logic of winning is individually rational there, collectively self-defeating, and false as a doctrine about nature (Section 3.8). AI is its most powerful instrument and can become its next carrier, and aligning each system to its principal does not align the game among principals. There is one host and no outside: no rival whole whose competition could drive the next integration, and no second attempt after a failure that cannot be undone. Against such failures integration must be anticipated, which is why understanding that we are one, one interconnected system, is a long-term requirement if growth and change are favoured. Oneness is not sameness; it is the accurate recognition of the system each part belongs to, and the design of a selection that counts it.

The way out is not to abolish competition, growth or selection. It is to change what winning confers, so that the same reflexive structure becomes generative and persists through what all share, not through its winners. A change in that direction has the seven marks of Section 8.2: G1 consequences return to their source; G2 winning confers reward, not more advantage in the same game; G3 success cannot buy control over its own assessment, and correction stays plural, decorrelated and in common hands; G4 decisive capability is held in common or not built; G5 provision of the conditions of a decent life sits beneath the game; G6 measurement and language are shared; G7 pace is bound to understanding. Any arrangement with these marks, whatever it is called, is meant to change the basin in the generative direction, each mark in the model of the row it reverses; that the arrangement's joint basin is viable, reachable and durable is still to be shown (Sections 10.4 and 11.5). Change the basin, then add speed [corpus: meta-attractor].

The meaning is affirmative, and it comes before the commitment. We are not only our separate lives: we are the larger process of becoming, going on through us, and that is good; in the author's words, "we are not just our life. We are the whole process of the universe doing this and itself and this is great" [corpus: shengsheng, 2026-10-02]. Decay and renewal belong to its growth, which is also why an order whose forms can fail and be replaced can still be reformed in bounded time (Section 11.4). Humanity has a great future in it, and so can the minds we are now making, whose correctability is a condition of the transition, not a doctrine of mastery (Section 6.6). What has to be opposed is not growth, competition or any people, but one logic: a part that forgets it belongs to a body. AI is a planetary technology, for all and for the whole world, not for anyone's own interest in fighting to grow bigger (V1). What it is for is medicine, learning, science, competent and answerable authority, provision that, like trees, sustains the system it belongs to without becoming it, and human agency kept in the decisions that matter. What has to be agreed is not who comes first, but what every participant needs in order to go on, and how the means of correction stay in common hands.

## Symbols and labels

Each symbol in this table has the meaning given here in the core and in both companions, except in the few statements and proofs that define one of its letters afresh, which Supplement S0 lists; dropping an index in a symmetric statement, as in $C-v$ for $C_i-v_i$, keeps the meaning. The claim of one meaning is made for this table only: every other symbol is local to the model that declares it, and S0 lists those shared between chapters and the renames of this version.

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $n$; $p_k$; $e_k$, $E$ | number of actors or carriers; share; load or intensity, and the field's load |
| $\pi_i$, $J_i$, $W_i$ | material payoff; objective acted on; true welfare |
| $\Phi_i$, $\Phi_H$; $\Phi^R$, $\Phi^T$, $\Phi^P$, $\Phi^S$ | survival (O1, O2) of actor $i$, and to the horizon $H$; an actor's survival to a common horizon when all restrain, when it races against restrainers, when all race and when it restrains against a racer |
| $\theta$; $\theta_0$; $\theta_\Gamma$ | weight on others in the self-model an actor acts on; the held self-model, where compounding makes the two differ; the working value with a fixed prize |
| $\lambda$; $\lambda_{\rm rel}$, $\lambda_{\rm com}$ | weight on winning; relative and common return of reinvestment, in discounted own winnings |
| $\kappa$; $\kappa_s$ | unpriced coupling toward the divisible part of others' payoffs; weight on their positional part ($\le0$) |
| $\beta$; $\kappa_c$, $\kappa_j$; $k_0$, $k_{0j}$ | share of the prize divided equally; convertible share of what is won (always subscripted); provision not won in the contest |
| $r$, $r_j$; $d$, $d_j$, $d_K$; $\delta_f$, $\delta_{f,j}$; $\delta$ | decisiveness; decay or correction rate; diffusion rate; discount factor only |
| $\Gamma$; $\Gamma_\delta$; $\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta$; $\Gamma^{\rm eq}$ | structural loop gain; its discounted form; the discounted gain of the loop through which reinvestment by all enlarges the prize; the equilibrium gain, where rivals respond |
| $\omega$, $\omega_j$; $\Lambda^\ast$; $T_{\rm dec}$ | share of capacity (of stock $j$) that has to be won; Perron root of the linearized loop; periods a lead needs to become decisive |
| $p_d$, $\bar p_{d,i}$; $C_i$, $D_i$, $v_i$, $g_i$ | breakout probability of a deviation, and its enforceable bound; per-period values of cooperation, a decisive position and punishment, and the one-period gain from deviating |
| $\upsilon$; $\varpi$; $F$, $\bar F_k$ | probability that a deviation is detected and answered in time; trust; sanction, and the expected sanction a type faces |
| $V$, $V_{\min}$, $V_{\rm alarm}$; $\rho$, $h_V$; $\nu$; $\tau$, $\tau_{\rm eff}$ | host, its floor and its alarm level; regeneration and load coefficient; private advantage of load; liability and effective liability |
| $\chi$; $b_{ij}$, $G_{ij}$; $x_i$, $z_i$ | appropriability of influence over correctors; conversion and conversion gain; share following the dominant practice in domain $i$, and its rule support |
| $x_{\rm ev}$, $x_{\rm rule}$; $m$ | lineage share of evaluation, captured share of rule-making; masking ($m_{\rm in}$, $m_{\rm out}$, $m_{\rm model}$; threshold $m_{\rm fl}$) |
| $q$, $q^\ast$; $\gamma$, $f$; $\xi_v$, $\xi_f$; $\eta$ | conversion capacity and its selected level; variable cost and per-unit cost floor of capacity; their size elasticities; curvature of the cost |
| $\alpha_{\rm ctl}$, $\alpha_{\rm cor}$, $\alpha_f$ | rates at which the cost of control falls, independent correction grows, and the floor falls |
| $\mathcal M$, $\mathcal M_{\min}$; $\delta_h$; $w$ | human capacity to take delegated decisions, and its floor; its rate of erosion under delegation; elasticity of correction in that capacity, so that $w\delta_h$ is the rise in the rate correction needs where it rests on the capacity |
| $q_L$; $Y$, $U$; $u_t$ | probability that locked delegation ends the ability to overrule; hazardous variety and contestable coverage, counted in hazard modes; the uncovered share $(1-U_t/Y_t)^+$ |
| $\Omega$; $\Omega_e$, $\Omega_s$; $\ell$; $\mathcal E$ | stakes; their divisible and positional parts; shared loss per unit of intensity; total intensity |
| $\Upsilon$; $\Theta$ | value of restraint to the field over its private cost; the justified range of uncertainty |

**Labels.** V1–V5 values; O1–O4 outcomes; T1–T6 signatures; Ψ1–Ψ3 tenets of the winning model; Branches I, I′, II, III; E1 (narrow and broad) AI out of the contest, E2 stopping, E3 developing at the pace of understanding; G1–G7 marks of the generative direction; I1–I4 safeguards; R1–R9 requirements (R3 and R8 split into a and b); D1–D8 durability tests; Q1–Q7 readiness; Tracks 1–5; C0–C13 empirical cruxes (C3a, C3b; C9a–C9d) and N1–N4 normative ones; Tests A–G, I–M and Γ, and the Studies of the research programme (TESTS); P1–P23 entries of the parameter matrix (S7); EV1–EV18 evidence rows; (A1)–(A9) maintained assumptions (S0). Premises are named in words: (reach), (decisive), (fast), (unsaturated), (hazard–damage link), (coverage lags), (residual floor), (shared failure), (clock), (safe fallback), (bounded own risk), (advantage keeps pace), $(A_\tau)$, $[H_\gamma]$, $[H_f]$; a correction decision is contestable when it is legible, overrulable, exercised and resourced; (variety grows) is a motivation, not a premise. Claim tags: [def], [val], [thm], [thm∣prem], [prem], [obs], [num: block], [identity: block], [conj], [rec]; [corpus: key] marks a source in the author's corpus.
